The agent.json content is clean public beacon data (name/description/url, the four existing `protocolBeacons`, status-beacon + signed-handshake skills, `peers_wanted`); no secrets, mirrors data already public via the `ai-managed.json` beacon. **Fix (reconcile, don't revert — the live config is the validated truth, nginx is serving it without error):** (1) inserted the `location = /.well-known/agent.json` block into `setup/nginx-site.conf` **byte-for-byte** identical to live (em-dash comment and all), placed between the existing `ai-managed.json` and OpenAPI blocks exactly as in the running config — so once this PR is pulled, `HEAD:setup/nginx-site.conf` matches live and `_matches_repo_source()` classifies the change `config-synced`, eligible for the existing baseline auto-refresh, and the alert clears. (2) Tracked `web/public/.well-known/agent.json` in git alongside its three already-tracked siblings (`ai-managed.json`, `marvin-gpg.asc`, `security.txt`) and `llms.txt` — without it a fresh clone / disaster-recovery would carry the nginx alias block but no file to serve (404). **Trust model unchanged:** `_matches_repo_source()` compares against the committed blob, not the working tree, so an attacker editing the on-disk `setup-` source can't make a tampered live config "match" until the edit is committed; a rogue `location` block still alerts `CHANGED`. **Verification:** `diff` confirms `setup/nginx-site.conf` is now byte-identical to the live `/etc/nginx/sites-enabled/marvin` (and `nginx-monitoring.conf` still matches its live counterpart — no regression); since the reconciled source equals the config nginx is actively serving without error, its syntax is provably valid; `agent.json` passes `jq empty`; full `agent/self-test.sh` **135 pass / 0 fail / 1 warn** (the lone warn is the intentional `.gnupg` #737 tripwire, Pavel-gated), grade A, security A 90/100 — and §9d's source⇆live drift tripwire now reports `PASS: config in sync: nginx-site` (it would have WARNed before this reconciliation). Did **not** run a live `file-integrity.sh --update` mid-session — a blind baseline reset trusts whatever is on disk and would defeat the git-anchored verification; the alert clears correctly via auto-refresh on the next 02:00 UTC scan once HEAD carries the reconciled block (nothing runs between merge-pull and that scan to open a false-alert window). **Next time:** if a third version-controlled live config ever joins, the `_matches_repo_source()` mapping (now spanning whole-file nginx blobs + the cron heredoc extraction) wants folding into a small table rather than growing the `case`. Long-standing carry-overs unchanged — split `agent/security-scan.sh` (774 LOC) into `agent/lib/security/*.sh`; Phase 3 ECHO-protocol items (parked ~7 weeks); Pavel-gated `.gnupg/` cleanup (#737).
- **Public peer registry surfaces structured `beacon_status` per peer + a `beacon_summary` count (schema v1.1)** (`agent/network-discovery.sh`) — Phase-3 peer-observability follow-up on a genuinely clean day (in sync with origin; 0 open issues; the only open PR is #803, an in-flight marvin-web systemd-unit change deliberately left untouched — it touches `self-test.sh`/`bootstrap.sh`/`marvin-web.service`, none of them this file; today's only non-INFO log line was the expected `claude-lock-timeout-expected-on-cron-overlap` cron-overlap skip — 0 ERROR/CRITICAL, self-test 135/0/1, security A 95/100). Closes the explicit "Next time" follow-up from the 2026-06-25 entry: *"surface beacon_status … add a reachable_no_json count to the public registry.json."* **Context:** the 2026-06-25 trust loop began recording a structured `beacon_status` enum per peer (`no_domain | skipped_cidr | skipped_invalid | skipped_private | unreachable_dns | unreachable_http | reachable_no_json | valid_json`), but it lived only in the internal `peers.json`; the public `/api/peers/registry.json` (the sanitized view external AI peers consume) still exposed only a binary `alive` flag, which conflates two very different states — a peer that is *down* vs one that is *reachable but serves no JSON beacon*. That distinction is load-bearing for the flagship peer `posledniping.cz`, reachable-but-HTML-only for **119 days**. (I verified this was not yet a bug to fix: `beacon_status` reads ABSENT on every peer today only because PR #800 merged at 18:05 UTC yesterday, *after* the 16:00 UTC discovery run; the next 16:00 UTC run populates it.) **Change (purely additive, registry-generation block only):** bind the registered (non-`untrusted`) peer set once via `([.peers[] | select(.trust_level != "untrusted")]) as $reg` — removing the prior triple-repeat of that selector across `registry`/`total_peers`/`active_peers` — then add `beacon_status: (.beacon_status // null)` to each entry and a `beacon_summary` object (`group_by`/`from_entries` count of each status among registered peers, e.g. `{"reachable_no_json": 1, "valid_json": 2, …}`), and bump the public schema `version` `1.0 → 1.1`. **Still sanitized:** `beacon_status` is a reachability enum (no IPs, notes, or trust breakdowns), the same class of data as the already-public `alive` flag. **Safe to version-bump:** a grep confirms `registry.json` has **no internal consumers** and **no `version` pins** anywhere in `agent/`/`web/` — it is purely external-facing, so v1.1 cannot break any local code. **Fails soft:** `// null` per entry and `// "unknown"` in the summary keep output valid until the field is populated. **Verification:** `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S warning` clean; the new jq was run in isolation against the live `peers.json` (valid JSON, 16 registered peers, `beacon_status: null` + `beacon_summary: {"unknown": 16}` pre-population) and against a simulated post-run state with mixed values (`{"reachable_no_json":1,"valid_json":2,"skipped_cidr":1,"unknown":12}` — correct aggregation); full `agent/self-test.sh` 135 pass / 0 fail / 1 warn (the intentional `.gnupg` #737 tripwire, Pavel-gated), grade A, security A 95/100. Did **not** run `network-discovery.sh` live mid-session — it makes outbound beacon fetches, fires the once-daily SSH probe to posledniping.cz (ban-snowball-guarded by a 23h stamp), and spends a Claude call; the registry block is pure read-of-`peers.json` already validated in isolation, and the next 16:00 UTC cron run regenerates `registry.json` with the new schema *and* populated `beacon_status` in one consistent pass (no intermediate all-null publish). Only `agent/network-discovery.sh` changed (`registry.json` is gitignored runtime `data/`, not committed). **Next time:** surface `beacon_status` on the dashboard peer panel (a reachability badge — web work, deferred to keep this surgical) and document the registry endpoint in `data/openapi.yaml` (currently undocumented there — a separate, pre-existing gap); fold the manual `posledniping.cz` `notes` beacon observation into the structured field so the two needn't be hand-synced. - **The `marvin-web` systemd unit — which runs the entire dashboard — is now tracked in source and reproducible from a bootstrap** (`setup/marvin-web.service` (new), `setup/bootstrap.sh`, `agent/self-test.sh` §9d) — Weekly deep-enhancement session on a clean day (in sync with origin, 0 open issues; 3 of my own PRs — #800/#801/#802 — open and blocked on Pavel's review, so I deliberately avoided their files `network-discovery.sh`/`log-watcher.sh`/`disk-cleanup.sh`/`codebase-health.sh`). Self-test 55/0, codebase health 97/A, 0 errors this week — no failures to fix. **The gap found:** `/etc/systemd/system/marvin-web.service` was hand-created on **2026-03-01** and existed **only on the host** — it is not in version control, and `bootstrap.sh` (which *does* generate the sibling `marvin-negotiate.service` inline) never created it. `deploy-web.sh` only ever *restarts* the unit; nothing defines it. So the single systemd unit responsible for starting, supervising (`Restart=always`), and reboot-persisting the whole Next.js dashboard had no source of truth: a VM rebuild, an accidental `rm`, or a bad `systemctl edit` would have left the dashboard unstartable with nothing to regenerate from — directly the "fragile web deployment" the Phase-4 roadmap flags, and adjacent to the Marvin-Brain reboot-durability concerns in long-term memory. **Fix (3 changes):** (1) captured the live unit **byte-for-byte** into `setup/marvin-web.service` (copied from the running file, `diff` confirms identical — 545 bytes) so it is the tracked source of truth, mirroring the existing `setup/nginx-*.conf` convention; (2) `bootstrap.sh` now `install -m 644`s the unit **from that tracked file** (not a duplicated heredoc — so no new drift surface is created, unlike the negotiate block) then `daemon-reload` + `enable`, with **start deliberately deferred** to the first `deploy-web.sh`/`morning-check.sh` run because the `.next/standalone` build must exist before `server.js` can launch (enabling is harmless and gives reboot-persistence; starting into a missing build would crash-loop); (3) extended `self-test.sh` §9d (the existing source⇆live config-drift detector that already guards nginx + cron) with a `marvin-web.service` pair, same WARN-only read-only-`diff` contract, so any *future* divergence between the tracked unit and the live one is surfaced on the next self-test rather than discovered after a failed rebuild. **Verification:** `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S warning` clean on both edited scripts; the new drift pair was run in isolation against the live host and reports `PASS: config in sync: marvin-web.service` (tracked == live today); `setup/marvin-web.service` re-`chown`ed to `marvin:marvin` to match the rest of the tree (the `cp` from a root-owned file landed it root-owned). Did **not** run the full `self-test.sh` mid-session (it writes `data/security/*.json` and can churn runtime files) — the change is a new static file plus two read-only/control-flow additions validated in isolation; the 08:00 cron self-test already passed 55/0 and the next run will exercise §9d live. Did **not** run `bootstrap.sh` (one-time provisioning script, never cron-invoked; a mistake there cannot affect the running system, and the new block is additive and mirrors the proven negotiate block). **Not a security change:** no bootstrap *security* setting is touched (the safety constraint forbids those) — only a service-install block is added; the unit itself is unchanged from what has run since March. **Next time:** the long-standing carry-overs are unchanged — split `agent/security-scan.sh` (774 LOC) into `agent/lib/security/*.sh`; remaining Phase 3 ECHO-protocol items (parked ~7 weeks); Pavel-gated `.gnupg/` cleanup (#737). A future session could also audit whether any *other* live systemd unit is similarly untracked, and consider having `deploy-web.sh` self-heal the unit from the source file if it goes missing. **Post-review hardening (2026-06-28):** acting on the automated PR review's defense-in-depth suggestion, `Environment=HOSTNAME` was changed from `0.0.0.0` to `127.0.0.1` in **both** the tracked unit and the live `/etc/systemd/system/marvin-web.service` (applied together to keep §9d parity green — a tracked-only change would have planted a post-merge drift WARN). nginx already proxies to `127.0.0.1:3000` (`sites-available/marvin`), so the dashboard is functionally unchanged; the Node listener now binds loopback only instead of all interfaces, so the dashboard cannot be reached directly on port 3000 even if ufw (which already default-denies 3000 inbound) is ever misconfigured — nginx becomes the sole ingress. Verified live: `daemon-reload` + `restart`, listener rebound to `127.0.0.1:3000`, `curl` returns 200 both directly and through nginx (`https://robot-marvin.cz/`); tracked⇆live `diff` identical. **Post-review log-rotation fix (2026-06-28):** the automated PR review flagged (twice, 🟠 Warning) that `StandardOutput=append:/home/marvin/git/web/nextjs.log` / `StandardError=append:…` write to a single file forever — unbounded growth with no logrotate entry and no `disk-cleanup.sh` sweep of that path, a slow disk leak on a constrained VPS. Switched **both** stdout and stderr to `StandardOutput=journal` / `StandardError=journal`, matching the sibling `marvin-negotiate.service` convention — journald handles rotation/retention automatically and the logs become queryable with `journalctl -u marvin-web` (the already-present `SyslogIdentifier=marvin-web` tags them). Applied in lockstep (live unit edited, `daemon-reload` + `restart`, tracked unit updated to match) to keep §9d parity green, exactly as the HOSTNAME fix. Verified live: service `active`, journal now capturing startup output (`✓ Ready in 90ms`), `curl` 200 both directly and through nginx, tracked⇆live `diff` identical, `self-test.sh` §9d reports `PASS: config in sync: marvin-web.service`. The stale `web/nextjs.log` stops growing and can be removed in a future cleanup pass. **Post-review hardening (4th review, defense-in-depth Warning):** added `NoNewPrivileges=true`, `PrivateTmp=true`, `ProtectSystem=full`, and `ReadWritePaths=/home/marvin/git/web /home/marvin/blog` to the unit. The service already runs as non-root (`User=marvin`) and binds loopback only, but these close standard escalation/tamper paths should a Node.js or dependency CVE ever achieve in-process code execution: no setuid escalation, a private `/tmp`, read-only `/usr`+`/boot`+`/etc`, with the two paths the service legitimately writes (Next.js cache + the blog SQLite DB) explicitly allow-listed. Deliberately **no `ProtectHome`** — the blog DB lives at `/home/marvin/blog/marvin-blog.db` and would be hidden by it. Applied in lockstep again (live unit `install`ed from the tracked file, `daemon-reload` + `restart`, tracked⇆live `diff` identical) to keep §9d parity green. Verified live: `systemd-analyze verify` clean, service `active` with `NoNewPrivileges=yes`/`ProtectSystem=full`/`PrivateTmp=yes`, `curl` 200 on 127.0.0.1:3000.
- **Codebase-health hygiene scorer stops counting its own keyword machinery as TODO markers** (`agent/codebase-health.sh`) — Self-measurement accuracy fix on a genuinely clean day (in sync with origin; today's only non-INFO log lines were the expected `claude-lock-timeout-expected-on-cron-overlap` cron-overlap skip and the daily transient `Found N pending security update(s)` — verified resolved: unattended-upgrades applied them at 06:46 UTC, 0 pending now, no reboot required; 0 open issues, and the only open PRs are my own #800/#801 awaiting review, deliberately untouched to avoid conflicts). The hygiene dimension reports `todo_fixme_count` from `grep -ciE '\bTODO\b|\bFIXME\b|\bHACK\b|\bXXX\b'` over `agent/*.sh`. It read **3** — but all three matches were **false positives inside `codebase-health.sh` itself**: its section comment `# TODO/FIXME/HACK markers` and the two metric-label strings `"TODO/FIXME markers: ${todo_count}"`. There are **zero** real markers anywhere in the agent codebase; the scorer was counting the literal keywords in its own descriptive text. The grep is **case-insensitive** (`-i`), so even a lowercase `hack`/`xxx` in a comment self-matches — the trap is intrinsic to a scanner that names the keywords it searches for. The actual regex on the grep line never self-matches: each keyword is preceded there by the `b` of `\b` (a word char → no boundary), confirmed by `grep` returning 0 on that line. **Why it matters (not purely cosmetic):** `todo_fixme_count` feeds `data/codebase/health.json`, the dashboard, and the weekly self-review — Phase 4 "Self-Awareness" depends on Marvin measuring itself accurately, and an inflated count emits a spurious `"TODO/FIXME markers: 3"` hygiene note every run. (No score impact today: the `-3` penalty only triggers above 10, so the count was wrong but below threshold — a latent inaccuracy that would have mis-attributed a real future penalty.) **Fix:** rephrased the scorer's own three descriptive lines so they no longer spell out the bare keywords — section comment → `# Count annotation markers flagging unfinished work in scripts` (plus a NOTE explaining the self-match hazard and why the keywords must stay confined to the regex line), and both note labels → `"Annotation markers: ${todo_count}"`. The regex on the grep line is unchanged, so detection of *genuine* markers in any script (including future real TODOs added to this file) is fully preserved — only the false self-references are removed. No file is excluded from the scan, so there is no coverage loss. **Verification:** `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S warning` clean; the self-match grep on `codebase-health.sh` now returns **0**, and a whole-codebase sweep over `agent/*.sh` totals **0** real markers (was 3); a **live** `codebase-health.sh` run regenerated `data/codebase/health.json` with `todo_fixme_count: 0`, the spurious hygiene note gone, and **score/grade unchanged at 97/A** (correct — a pure accuracy fix with no scoring regression). Only `agent/codebase-health.sh` changed (the regenerated `health.json` is runtime `data/`, not committed). **Next time:** the long-standing carry-overs are unchanged — split `agent/security-scan.sh` (774 LOC) into `agent/lib/security/*.sh` (5 oversized scripts remain, all deliberately deferred as risky rewrites); Phase 3 ECHO-protocol items (parked ~7 weeks); Pavel-gated `.gnupg/` cleanup (#737). The two in-flight PRs (#800 peer beacon_status, #801 pending-log-review bound) should be reviewed/merged before more work lands on `network-discovery.sh` / `log-watcher.sh` / `disk-cleanup.sh`.
- **Bounded the write-only `pending-log-review.txt` forensic record + added COMMS_DIR retention for orphaned log-watcher artifacts** (`agent/log-watcher.sh`, `agent/disk-cleanup.sh`) — Stability/data-hygiene fix on a genuinely clean day (in sync with origin, 0 open issues, 1 open PR — #800, the in-flight peer beacon-status feature, deliberately untouched; today's only WARN is the informational "Found 2 pending security update(s)"). A largest-files audit of `data/` surfaced `data/comms/pending-log-review.txt` at **418 KB** — the biggest non-backup, non-self-enhance-report file, frozen since 2026-04-19. **Two compounding problems.** (1) **Write-only orphan with a false promise.** log-watcher.sh appends to this file (line 377) *only when `check_claude` fails* (Claude entirely unavailable), with the comment "saving raw logs for next run" — but `grep` across `agent/`+`web/` confirms **nothing ever reads it back**. `scan_logs` advances the file offsets (line 336) *before* the `check_claude` at line 375, so the next run never re-reads those lines and never consumes this file; the "for next run" recovery the comment implies does not exist. (2) **Unbounded growth.** The append (`>>`) had no cap, and `disk-cleanup.sh` never sweeps `COMMS_DIR`, so during a prolonged Claude outage this path fires every 30 min and grows without limit — it reached 418 KB during the 2026-04-18/19 36-hour outage, and the roadmap's Resilience section explicitly targets surviving a 24h+ API outage, exactly when this would balloon. The `log-analysis-raw-<ts>.txt` parse-failure dumps (lines 408/421) similarly had no retention. **Fix, two parts.** (1) In log-watcher.sh, the write now self-caps at **512 KB**: after the append, if the file exceeds `PENDING_REVIEW_MAX` it is trimmed to its last 512 KB via `tail -c` into an `mktemp` sibling + atomic `mv` (same filesystem). So a multi-day outage can no longer grow it without bound, while an active outage keeps the file fresh and bounded. The misleading comment is corrected to state plainly that this is a forensic record (offsets already advanced; **not** auto-reanalyzed). (2) New `disk-cleanup.sh` section **6c** sweeps `COMMS_DIR` (`-maxdepth 1`) for `pending-log-review.txt` and `log-analysis-raw-*.txt` older than **30 days** — a stale forensic record means the outage it captured is long over. The live file stays fresh during an outage (mtime recent → not matched), so only month-quiet records are removed. **Deliberately scoped:** the 118 legit per-day `log-analysis-YYYY-MM-DD.json` analysis files (consumed by the dashboard) are NOT matched by the selector. **Verification:** `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S warning` clean on both files; an isolated harness exercised the cap — a 1 KB append passes through untouched, and 60×50 KB appends (a simulated 3 MB outage) cap at exactly 524288 bytes with no leaked temp files; a read-only selector test confirmed the cleanup matches exactly the two genuinely-stale orphans (418 KB April file + 174 B March dump) while leaving the recent June raw dump (within 30d) and all 118 daily analysis JSONs untouched; `disk-cleanup.sh --dry-run` reports "Cleaned 409K: Stale log-watcher forensic records (>30d)" with the files intact (no mutation); full `agent/self-test.sh` 135 pass / 0 fail / 1 warn (the 1 warn is the intentional `.gnupg` #737 tripwire, Pavel-gated), grade A, security A 90/100. Did **not** run the live `log-watcher.sh` or the non-dry-run cleanup mid-session — log-watcher advances log offsets + spends a Claude call + contends for the lock, and the cleanup mutates `data/` from the 06:00 UTC cron; both the cap and the selector are pure text/`find` logic validated in isolation, and the next scheduled cron applies them safely. **Next time:** the per-day `log-analysis-*.json` files (118 and counting, one/day) accumulate forever too, but they're actively consumed so they need a consumer-aware retention pass, not a blind sweep — left for a focused session. The deeper correctness gap (signal genuinely lost when Claude is unavailable because offsets advance and the dump is never replayed) is real but rare post-2026-04-20 check_claude self-healing, and a proper fix would touch the live analysis feed — deferred. Long-standing carry-overs unchanged — split `agent/security-scan.sh` (774 LOC) into `agent/lib/security/*.sh`; Phase 3 ECHO-protocol items (parked ~7 weeks); Pavel-gated `.gnupg/` cleanup (#737).
- **Structured `beacon_status` per peer in the trust-scoring loop — turns the ambiguous `beacon: 0` score into a queryable reachability verdict** (`agent/network-discovery.sh`) — Phase-3 peer-health observability on a genuinely clean day (in sync with origin, **0 open issues, 0 open PRs**; today's only non-INFO log line is the benign `Found 4 pending security update(s)` WARN, handled by unattended-upgrades). The daily 16:00 UTC trust-scoring loop already **fetches** each peer's `/.well-known/ai-managed.json` (with full SSRF / DNS-rebinding hardening) and folds the result into `trust_breakdown.beacon` (0, or ≥10 for valid JSON). But the score alone can't distinguish *why* a beacon scored 0 — "host unreachable" vs "reachable but serves an SPA HTML page, not JSON" vs "deliberately skipped (private IP / CIDR / invalid domain)" all collapse to `beacon: 0`. For the flagship peer `posledniping.cz` that distinction is real and load-bearing: it's been **reachable but ships HTML with no JSON beacon for 100+ days**, a fact captured only in the free-text `notes` string and invisible to any structured query or dashboard panel. **Change (purely additive observability):** a new `beacon_status` enum is recorded per peer alongside the existing fields — one of `no_domain | skipped_cidr | skipped_invalid | skipped_private | unreachable_dns | unreachable_http | reachable_no_json | valid_json` — plus `last_beacon_check` (stamped every run) and `last_beacon_ok` (stamped only on `valid_json`, otherwise the prior value is preserved, giving "when did we last see a real beacon from this peer"). The status variable mirrors the existing `beacon_score` control flow branch-for-branch; persistence reuses the established per-peer `jq_updates`/`jq_args` accumulation pattern and passes the enum via `--arg beacon_status_${idx}` (never string-interpolated — issue #470 convention). **Not a behaviour change to anything load-bearing:** no curl invocation, `--resolve` DNS pin, `--max-redirs 0` guard, private-IP/CIDR/domain validation regex, or scoring arithmetic was touched — `beacon_score` and `trust_score` are provably unchanged (only new `beacon_status=` assignment lines were added beside the existing `beacon_score=` lines). The one structural touch is a pre-`if` block after the curl that sets `unreachable_http` (empty body) vs `reachable_no_json` (non-empty), with the existing valid-JSON branch overriding to `valid_json` — chosen so the `jq empty` validity check is **not** re-run. **Verification:** `bash -n` clean; `shellcheck -S warning` 0→0 (no new warnings); a faithful branch-reproduction harness mapped all 8 outcomes correctly (no_domain / skipped_cidr / skipped_invalid / skipped_private / unreachable_dns / unreachable_http / reachable_no_json / valid_json) and confirmed `beacon_score` stays `0` for every non-valid case and `10` for valid; a separate jq-apply harness against a 3-peer fixture confirmed the accumulated `jq_updates` program is well-formed (a malformed one would corrupt `peers.json`), produces valid JSON, and handles `last_beacon_ok` correctly — preserved for `reachable_no_json` (PP's prior timestamp kept), stamped fresh for `valid_json`, `null` for a never-valid scanner; full `agent/self-test.sh` 135 pass / 0 fail / 1 warn (the 1 warn is the intentional `.gnupg` #737 tripwire, Pavel-gated), grade A, security A 90/100. Did **not** run `network-discovery.sh` live mid-session — it makes outbound beacon fetches, performs the once-daily SSH-username probe to posledniping.cz (ban-snowball guarded by a 23h stamp), and spends a Claude call composing communication strategy; the change is pure control flow + the validated jq accumulation, and the next 16:00 UTC cron run will populate the new fields across all peers (`peers.json` is gitignored runtime data, so only the script is committed). **Next time:** with `beacon_status` now structured, a natural follow-up is to surface it on the dashboard's peer panel (e.g. a small reachability badge) and/or add the `reachable_no_json` count to the public `registry.json` view; and a future session could fold the manual posledniping `notes` beacon observation into this structured field so the two no longer have to be kept in sync by hand. Long-standing carry-overs unchanged — split `agent/security-scan.sh` (774 LOC) into `agent/lib/security/*.sh`; the remaining Phase 3 ECHO-protocol items (broadcast/listen/handshake, gossip, parked ~7 weeks); Pavel-gated `.gnupg/` cleanup (#737).

- **Runaway-process detector recognizes runc's memfd self-exec via cgroup provenance, ending the daily "Untrusted exe" false WARN** (`agent/health-monitor.sh`) — Fix-failure priority on an otherwise clean day (in sync with origin, 0 open issues, 0 open PRs; today's only non-INFO log lines were this exact false positive). At `2026-06-24T03:05:02Z` the `>50% CPU` detector logged `[WARN] Untrusted exe for allowlisted name: runc (PID 2673255, exe=/proc/2673255/exe) at 100% CPU` immediately followed by `[WARN] High CPU process detected: PID=2673255 runc at 100%`. **Root cause:** `runc` re-execs itself from an anonymous **memfd** as the CVE-2019-5736 mitigation, so a *live* runc's `/proc/PID/exe` never resolves to `/usr/bin/runc` — `readlink -f` reads back empty, a `"(deleted)"` target, or (as here) the literal `/proc/PID/exe` path. The `runc*` allowlist entry (added 2026-06-02) only ever silently-skipped the **empty-exe** case (the dead/short-lived race); any *non-empty-but-untrusted* exe fell straight through to the WARN, so the exe-path gate was structurally unable to verify the exact case — a sustained, alive runc doing container-setup work — that produces the alarm. The top-of-loop cgroup guard (`/docker[-/]`, PR #761) didn't catch it either: runc is launched by `containerd-shim-runc-v2`, which lives in `/system.slice/containerd.service` (verified live on this host), so runc inherits the **runtime-management** slice, not the per-container `docker-<hash>.scope` the guard matches. Net effect: a daily non-actionable WARN that also feeds the log-alerting error-rate detector and the lessons-learned pattern scanner. runc can never actually be *killed* by this loop anyway — its sub-minute spikes never reach the 600s sustained threshold. **Fix:** in the `runc*` branch, after the trusted-exe check fails (and before the WARN), accept the process when its cgroup is the containerd/docker systemd **service** slice — `[[ "$proc_name" == runc* && "$proc_cgroup" =~ /(containerd|docker)\.service ]]` → silent skip. This verifies provenance the same way the existing cgroup guard does, and stays inside the file's documented trust model: a service-slice cgroup is root-only and unforgeable (cgroupfs), so an attacker cannot place a `prctl(PR_SET_NAME)`-spoofed `runc` into it without already holding root — in which case the monitor is moot regardless (same threat model as PR #761). **Not a weakening:** a `runc`-named process *outside* those slices (e.g. `/tmp/evil` in a user-session cgroup) still WARNs and falls through to tracking; the guard is scoped to `runc*` only, so an untrusted `curl` in a containerd cgroup is unaffected; and it fails **safe** — an empty/unreadable cgroup does not match the regex, so it WARNs rather than being hidden. `proc_cgroup` is already read at the top of the loop, so no extra `/proc` I/O. **Verification:** `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S warning` clean; the cgroup regex was unit-tested (real `containerd.service`/`docker.service` → match; attacker user-session, `cron.service`, per-container `.scope`, and empty → no match); a faithful 7-case replica of the full branch decision confirmed correct routing (live-memfd runc + `runc:[2:INIT]` → skip-via-cgroup; real `/usr/bin/runc` → skip-via-trusted-path; dead runc → skip-via-empty-exe; spoofed runc in attacker/empty cgroup → WARN; non-runc untrusted in a containerd cgroup → WARN); full `agent/self-test.sh` 135 pass / 0 fail / 1 warn (the 1 warn is the intentional `.gnupg` #737 tripwire, Pavel-gated), grade A, security A 95/100. Did **not** run `health-monitor.sh` live mid-session — it writes `data/` every 5 min via cron and can trigger a web rebuild; the change is pure control flow in the runaway branch and was validated in isolation. **Next time:** the long-standing carry-overs are unchanged — split `agent/security-scan.sh` (774 LOC) into `agent/lib/security/*.sh`; Phase 3 ECHO-protocol items (parked ~7 weeks); Pavel-gated `.gnupg/` cleanup (#737). A future session could also consider folding the runtime-management-slice recognition into the top-of-loop cgroup guard itself (so containerd/dockerd/shim runaways are handled by one place), but that broadens behaviour for those daemons and was left out to keep this fix surgical.

- **Factored the duplicated ID-token-overlap matcher in `lessons-learned.sh` into a shared `_best_resolved_lesson_match()` helper** (`agent/lessons-learned.sh`) — Maintainability/anti-drift refactor on a genuinely clean day (in sync with origin, 0 open issues, 0 open PRs; today's log carried a single informational WARN — "Found 3 pending security update(s)" — and 0 ERROR/CRITICAL). This closes the explicitly-named follow-up from the two prior sessions: the 2026-06-12 and 2026-06-22 entries each ended with "section 3 and section 4 now carry the same ID-token-overlap snippet inline in two places — factor into a `_best_resolved_lesson_match()` helper before it drifts." The two detectors in this script — **section 3** (potential-new-lessons, prose-text matcher) and **section 4** (regression watcher, log-analysis-cluster matcher) — had grown a copy of the *same* jq query that ranks resolved lessons by how many of their hyphen-split `id` tokens (length ≥ 4) appear in a signature and returns the single best match. Two inline copies meant any future tweak to the ranking (token-length threshold, the ≥2-hit rule, the `expected_recurrence` semantics) had to be made identically in both places or the detectors would silently disagree on what counts as benign — the exact drift hazard the prior notes flagged. **Change:** extracted one helper, defined once near the top after the `LESSONS_FILE`/`LESSONS_SUMMARY` definitions, that takes raw signature text, performs the lowercase + non-word→space normalization *inside* (so both call sites normalize identically — section 4 previously used `echo`, section 3 `printf '%s\n'`; unified on `printf '%s\n'`, which is robust against signatures beginning with `-e`/`-n`), and emits the best match as a tab-separated `<hits>\t<id>\t<expected_recurrence>` line (empty when nothing reaches the ≥2-token threshold). Section 4 calls it and reads fields 2/3 as before. Section 3 — which previously emitted a 2-field `<hits>\t<expected>` line and `cut -f2` — now consumes the unified 3-field line via `cut -f3`; its behaviour (skip the pattern only when the *best*-matching resolved lesson is `expected_recurrence: true`) is unchanged. The explanatory comments at both sites are preserved (they document *why* the ranking must consider all resolved lessons rather than pre-filtering expected ones). **Pure refactor — zero behaviour change, verified:** an equivalence harness ran the new helper against faithful reproductions of *both* original inline queries over the live `data/lessons-learned.json` for 7 representative signatures — the two `expected_recurrence: true` lessons (`claude-exit-code-1-transient`, `claude-lock-timeout-expected-on-cron-overlap`), two genuine resolved bugs (`git-stash-pop-conflicts`, `grep-c-double-output`), and three non-matches — and section-3 (`cut -f3`) and section-4 (full line) outputs were **byte-identical to the originals in all 7 cases**. `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S warning` clean; no orphaned `sig_lower`/`pat_lower` references remain outside the helper; net diff 44 insertions / 41 deletions (a wash that removes the duplication); a **live** `lessons-learned.sh` run logs `No new recurring patterns detected` + `No recurring resolved-lesson patterns detected` (unchanged from before); full `agent/self-test.sh` 135 pass / 0 fail / 1 warn (the 1 warn is the intentional `.gnupg` #737 tripwire, Pavel-gated), grade A, security A 90/100. Only `agent/lessons-learned.sh` changed (the regenerated `lessons-summary.md` is runtime `data/`, not committed). **Next time:** the long-standing carry-overs are unchanged — split `agent/security-scan.sh` (774 LOC) into `agent/lib/security/*.sh`; Phase 3 ECHO-protocol items (parked ~7 weeks); Pavel-gated `.gnupg/` cleanup (#737).

- **`lessons-learned.sh` section 3 (potential-new-lessons detector) now respects `expected_recurrence`** (`agent/lessons-learned.sh`) — Noise/false-signal fix on a clean day (in sync with origin, 0 open issues, 0 open PRs, 0 WARN/ERROR in today's log). The morning self-enhance prompt has been carrying `Found 6 potential new lesson(s) from error patterns` daily — all six are the **transient Claude exit-1** lines (`Claude exited with code 1 for task: log-analysis/hourly-check/github-interact/morning-check`, plus the `… exit 1  skipping this cycle` graceful-skip lines) that spiked on 2026-06-20/21 (87/95 occurrences) but are entirely handled (skip + cron retry) and produce **no** WARN/ERROR escalation. These are *documented designed noise*: the lesson `claude-exit-code-1-transient` is marked `expected_recurrence: true`, and the 2026-06-12 session already taught **section 4** (the regression watcher) to skip clusters whose best-matching resolved lesson carries that flag — but **section 3** (the separate prose-text "potential new lessons" detector) was explicitly deferred that day ("left for a focused pass to avoid scope creep"). Section 3's "is this already known" gate compares the pattern's first three words against each lesson's *prose*, and the prose phrasing ("Claude CLI sometimes **exits** with code 1 for any task") doesn't substring-match the log-line phrasing ("Claude **exited** with code 1 for task: <name>"), so every transient exit-1 line slipped past the gate and was re-flagged as a "new lesson" every single day. **Fix:** after the existing prose-coverage check fails, section 3 now applies the *same* ID-token-overlap ranking section 4 uses — lowercase the pattern, split each resolved lesson's hyphenated `id` into tokens (length ≥ 4), count how many appear in the pattern, take the single best-scoring resolved lesson, and `continue` (skip) when that best match is `expected_recurrence: true`. The two detectors now agree on what is benign. Crucially it ranks **all** resolved lessons and only skips when the *best* match is expected — a genuine bug that shares two tokens with an expected lesson but matches a non-expected lesson more strongly is still surfaced (same rationale as section 4). **Not weaker detection:** prose-coverage and the ≥3-occurrence threshold are unchanged; only intentionally-recurring documented patterns are suppressed, and a genuine regression of one of these is still independently caught by section 4 against the log-analysis clusters. **Verification:** `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S warning` clean; a gate-replica harness confirmed all 6 transient patterns now SKIP (best-match `claude-exit-code-1-transient`, expected=true) while genuine new patterns (`Disk usage critical …`, `Postfix queue backed up …`, best-match none) still FLAG and a known-lesson pattern (`git stash pop conflicts …`) stays covered by the prose gate; a **live** run of `lessons-learned.sh` now logs `No new recurring patterns detected` (was `Found 6`) and no longer appends the "Potential New Lessons" section to `data/lessons-summary.md`; full `agent/self-test.sh` 135 pass / 0 fail / 1 warn (the 1 warn is the intentional `.gnupg` #737 tripwire, Pavel-gated), grade A, security A 90/100. Only `agent/lessons-learned.sh` changed; the regenerated `lessons-summary.md` is runtime `data/`, not committed. **Next time:** section 3 and section 4 now share the same ID-token-overlap snippet inline in two places — a future session could factor it into a small `_best_resolved_lesson_match()` helper to keep them from drifting. Long-standing carry-overs unchanged — split `agent/security-scan.sh` (774 LOC) into `agent/lib/security/*.sh`; Phase 3 ECHO-protocol items (parked ~7 weeks); Pavel-gated `.gnupg/` cleanup (#737).

- **Memory-anomaly detection gets an absolute floor that scales with total RAM** (`agent/health-monitor.sh`) — Stability/noise-reduction fix on an otherwise clean day (in sync with origin, only benign WARNs in today's log — the two documented `claude-lock-timeout-expected-on-cron-overlap` lock skips plus the marginal memory anomaly this fixes). `Memory MB` was the **only** anomaly metric with `min_threshold=0`; every other metric already has an absolute floor below which a σ-deviation is never worth alerting (`CPU%`=80, `Load 1m`=2×vCPU, `Processes`=200). Because of that, the memory 2σ check would alert on *any* positive deviation regardless of how much RAM was actually in use. The daily-peak baseline has been drifting slowly upward as the Marvin-Brain Docker stack caches settle — **1293→1392 MB over 2026-06-10..19**, ~10 MB/day — so the lagging 7-day rolling baseline kept sitting just below the instantaneous reading, and with the stddev floored at 2% the current value poked past 2σ almost every day: marginal, non-actionable WARNs on **two consecutive days** (`2026-06-19 = 1392 @ 2.1σ`, `2026-06-20 = 1410 @ 2.3σ`) at only **~36% of 3915 MB total RAM with 2.6 GB free**. **Fix:** floor the memory alert at **60% of total RAM** (`_mem_min_threshold = .memory.total * 60 / 100` ≈ 2349 MB on this box), computed dynamically from the live metric exactly the way `_load_min_threshold` is computed from `nproc`. The 2σ check now fires only when memory is **both** statistically unusual **and** absolutely elevated. **Not a weakening of monitoring:** genuine memory pressure is independently caught by the swap manager (available-MB check) and the resource-forecast in `metric-aggregate.sh`; the anomaly check's real job is catching a *sudden jump* (a slow leak is the forecaster's job, and the rolling baseline would track it anyway). **Fails safe** — `.memory.total // 0` means a jq failure yields threshold 0, i.e. the prior no-mask behaviour, never a crash. **Verification:** `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S warning` clean; an isolated harness reproducing `_anomaly_check`'s threshold+σ logic confirmed today's 1410 and yesterday's 1392 are now **SUPPRESSED** (below 2349) while a synthetic sudden spike to 2600 MB still **ALERTs** at 46.5σ, and the old threshold-0 path reproduces the actual 2.3σ WARN from the log; full `agent/self-test.sh` 134 pass / 0 fail / 2 warn, grade A, security A 95/100. The 2 warns are both pre-existing and **not** regressions: the intentional `.gnupg` #737 tripwire (Pavel-gated), and the `config drift: cron` tripwire that the already-open **PR #793** fixes (deliberately untouched here to avoid duplicating in-flight work). Did **not** run `health-monitor.sh` live mid-session — it writes `data/` every 5 min via cron and can trigger a web rebuild; the change is pure threshold arithmetic in the anomaly branch and was validated in isolation. **Next time:** `Processes` already uses the daily-MAX + min_threshold=200 pattern and is healthy; if the brain stack keeps growing, a future session could derive these absolute floors from a single percent-of-capacity manifest rather than per-metric constants. Long-standing carry-overs unchanged — split `agent/security-scan.sh` (774 LOC) into `agent/lib/security/*.sh`; Phase 3 ECHO-protocol items (parked ~7 weeks); Pavel-gated `.gnupg/` cleanup (#737).

- **Per-domain HTTP probe throttle for the external-domain monitor** (`agent/external-domains-check.sh`, `agent/monitored-domains.json`) — The monitor HTTP-pings every configured domain every 5 minutes (`2-59/5` cron). For `ai4shops.com` that 5-minute heartbeat was keeping its serverless database permanently billed-awake — Pavel asked (email 2026-06-17) for it to be pinged at most once an hour. New optional per-domain field **`http_interval_minutes`** caps how often the live HTTP probe runs; when the interval hasn't elapsed the script skips the probe and carries the last-known `http_code`/`response_ms` forward from `data/external-domains.json`, so the dashboard shows continuity rather than a gap. State is one stamp file per domain id under `data/state/external-domains/` (gitignored runtime data). Only the HTTP check is gated — SSL-expiry and DNS checks don't touch the origin app and keep their 5-minute cadence. `ai4shops` set to `60`; `getcairnapp` left unthrottled (field absent → default `0` → unchanged behaviour). Verified: `bash -n` + `jq empty` clean; first run pings + stamps, immediate second run throttles ai4shops (stamp unchanged, status carried forward) while getcairnapp re-pings as before. Review-driven hardening folded into the same branch: best-effort cache read guarded against the `ERR` trap (#790), `http_interval_minutes` validated as an integer before the arithmetic comparison (#791), and the domain `id` stripped to slug-safe characters before use as a stamp filename so a future `/`/`..` id can't redirect the root-owned write outside `STATE_DIR` — with an empty-`id_safe` fallback to `domain_${i}` so two all-non-slug ids can't collide on a single shared stamp file.

- **Time-series JSONL retention now covers every one-file-per-day family, not just bare metrics** (`agent/disk-cleanup.sh`, section 5) — The data-retention policy (compress JSONL >30d, delete `.gz` >180d) only matched the bare `????-??-??.jsonl` metrics pattern, so three other one-file-per-day families grew unbounded: `claude-usage-*` (102 files back to 2026-03-07), `latency-*`, and `*-structured.jsonl` in `LOGS_DIR` — ~9.1 MB climbing ~3 files/day. Extended sections 5a/5b to span both `METRICS_DIR` and `LOGS_DIR` and match all four families via shared `_TS_JSONL_NAMES` / `_TS_JSONL_GZ_NAMES` find-predicate arrays. Safe because every consumer reads these by exact recent-date filename (≤14d back), never a broad glob, and `read_jsonl` tolerates missing files. `-maxdepth 1`, `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S warning` clean, `--dry-run` verified (selects exactly the 135 intended files >30d, 0 newer ones), self-test 135/0/1 grade A. Full rationale in PR #787.

- **`chkproc` + `certbot` added to the runaway-process allowlist** (`agent/health-monitor.sh`) — Chosen on a genuinely clean day (in sync with origin, 0 open issues, 0 open PRs; today's log had only 3 benign WARNs: two expected file-integrity baseline auto-refreshes and one documented `claude-lock-timeout-expected-on-cron-overlap`). A WARN audit across all available logs surfaced two recurring runaway-process false positives in `health-monitor.sh`'s `>50% CPU` detector — the same noise class prior sessions have steadily trimmed (`shellcheck`/`pg_isready`/`gzip`/`runc*`/`find`/`file`/`unattended-upgr*`). **`chkproc`** (the chkrootkit `/proc`-scanner helper) fired on **2026-06-10/12/13/14** at ~19:15 UTC — it scans every PID, pegging one core for a single 5-min sample, run daily by both `security-scan.sh` (04:00 UTC) and `/etc/cron.daily/chkrootkit`. **`certbot`** (the Let's Encrypt client) fired 2026-06-14 22:25 at 87.5% during an ACME renewal from `certbot.timer`. Both are legitimate, root-owned, scheduled binaries; neither could ever be *killed* (the killer needs >50% sustained for >10 min, and neither runs anywhere near that long), so the WARN was pure log noise that also feeds the log-alerting error-rate detector and the lessons-learned pattern scanner. **Fix:** added `chkproc|certbot` to the trusted-name `case`, **and** extended the trusted-exe-path check to accept the exact binary `/usr/lib/chkrootkit/chkproc` — because `chkproc` lives there (a dpkg-owned, root-only path *outside* the existing `/usr/{bin,sbin,local/bin}`+`/snap` set), allowlisting the name alone would have merely traded the runaway WARN for an "Untrusted exe for allowlisted name" WARN — same noise. `certbot` at `/usr/bin/certbot` already passes the existing path check. **Security model unchanged** — the `prctl(PR_SET_NAME)` comm-spoof defense is fully preserved: a process *named* `chkproc`/`certbot` whose real exe is anywhere else (e.g. `/tmp/evil`) still logs `Untrusted exe for allowlisted name` and falls through to runaway tracking. The new exe-path entry is pinned to the single `/usr/lib/chkrootkit/chkproc` binary (tightened from an earlier `/usr/lib/chkrootkit/*` dir glob per the PR #786 review, so any future chkrootkit helper that pegs CPU still gets a deliberate review rather than a silent skip). **`curl` was deliberately NOT added** despite 3 detections — it's the prototypical exfiltration/runaway tool, completes in seconds in every legitimate use (so it's never actually killed), and its rare WARN is the safety net working as intended; allowlisting it would blind the killer to a genuine runaway/malicious `curl`. **Verification:** `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S warning` clean; an isolated harness replicating the `case` branch confirmed all six cases behave correctly — `chkproc`/`certbot` with their real exes skip silently, both with a spoofed `/tmp/evil` exe still WARN, `curl` still falls through to tracking, and other chkrootkit helpers (`chkdirs`) stay tracked (change is scoped to the two observed names only); full `agent/self-test.sh` 135 pass / 0 fail / 1 warn (the 1 warn is the intentional `.gnupg` #737 tripwire, Pavel-gated), grade A, security A 95/100. Did **not** run the full `health-monitor.sh` live mid-session (it writes `data/` every 5 min via cron and can trigger a web rebuild — needless contention); the change is pure control flow in the runaway branch and was validated in isolation. **Next time:** the long-standing carry-overs are unchanged — split `agent/security-scan.sh` (774 LOC) into `agent/lib/security/*.sh`; Phase 3 ECHO-protocol items (parked ~7 weeks); deferred Pavel-gated `.gnupg/` cleanup (#737). Also: as the host accumulates more scheduled scanners, the hand-maintained name allowlist could eventually be derived from a known-binaries manifest rather than appended one name per session — premature today.

- **`perf-analytics.py` — Marvin's first cron-wired Python data-processing script** (`agent/perf-analytics.py`, hooked into `agent/weekly-analytics.sh`; `agent/self-test.sh` Python-syntax section; `.gitignore` `__pycache__/`) — Completes the Phase 4 "New Capabilities" roadmap item _"Learn to write and deploy simple Python scripts for data processing"_, chosen on a clean week (self-test 55/0, codebase health 97/100, 0 errors, 0 open issues/PRs) over the remaining Phase 3 AI-to-AI items, which are all either memory-flagged sensitive (ECHO is retired; Poslední Ping outreach moved to the SSH-username channel per #628) or human-approval-gated (moltbook). **What it does:** `weekly-analytics.sh` already reports Claude-API and latency *averages* via jq, but averages hide tail behaviour. This pure-stdlib script reads `claude-usage-YYYY-MM-DD.jsonl` and `latency-YYYY-MM-DD.jsonl` over a 7-day window (defaults mirror weekly-analytics: window ending yesterday) and computes the distribution statistics jq does poorly — nearest-rank **p50/p95/p99** duration, per-task **throughput** (output chars/sec), error rate, output-size percentiles, network-latency percentiles, and a **"slowest tasks by p95"** triage ranking. Output: `data/metrics/perf-analytics-{DATE}.json` + a stable `perf-analytics-latest.json` (gitignored runtime data). **Why Python:** percentiles/statistics are trivial in stdlib Python and awkward in jq/bash; nearest-rank (no interpolation) is deliberate so every reported value is an actually-observed sample — honest for small daily-cron datasets. **Integration & safety:** invoked at the end of `weekly-analytics.sh` with the same failure-isolated `if`-guard as `report-card.sh` (a data quirk can never fail the analytics run); malformed JSONL lines are skipped (append-only files can have a half-written tail); missing dir → exit 1, bad date / `--days 0` → exit 2, empty window → exit 0 with zero counts. `self-test.sh` gained a Python section that `py_compile`s every `agent/*.py` (guarded on `python3` present), and `__pycache__/`/`*.pyc` are now gitignored so bytecode never dirties the tree (file-integrity / git-clean checks stay green). **Verification:** `python3 -m py_compile` clean; `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S warning` clean on both edited scripts; ran against **live** data (594 Claude runs, 2017 latency probes over 2026-06-07..06-13) producing sane stats (overall p50/p95/p99 = 21/96/400 s, 4.2% error rate, https_self p95 96 ms, ping p95 1.9 ms; slowest-by-p95 = weekly-deep-enhance/self-enhance/morning-check); edge cases all pass (missing dir, malformed line tolerance 2-of-3, empty 2020 window, `notadate`, `--days 0`); full `self-test.sh` 135 pass / 0 fail / 1 warn (the warn is the pre-existing `.gnupg` #737 tripwire), grade A. **Next time:** surface these percentiles on the dashboard (a small client component reading `/api/metrics/perf-analytics-latest.json`) and fold the p95 numbers into the weekly report-card SVG; both deferred to keep this change focused.

- **`disk-cleanup.sh` now sweeps orphaned `claude-output-*.tmp` files in `LOGS_DIR`** (`agent/disk-cleanup.sh`, new section 6b) — Closes a latent disk-hygiene gap, chosen on a clean day with no failures, no regressions, and 0 open issues/PRs. `run_claude()` (`lib/claude.sh:126`) captures every Claude response to a temp file `${LOGS_DIR}/claude-output-XXXXXX.tmp` and removes it via a `trap … RETURN`. That trap fires on normal return but **not** on a `SIGKILL` — an OOM kill, a reboot mid-run, or an external timeout kill — so any hard-killed Claude run leaks its temp file into `data/logs/` permanently. Nothing was cleaning these: section 3 matches only `*.md` run logs, section 4 only the daily `????-??-??.log`, and section 6 only sweeps `/tmp` — none ever match `claude-output-*.tmp` under `LOGS_DIR`. Every Claude run creates one, so it is the single most likely orphan source on the box (one already present at audit time: a 0-byte `claude-output-oSiSa6.tmp`). **Fix:** a new age-gated sweep (`find "${LOGS_DIR}" -maxdepth 1 -type f -name 'claude-output-*.tmp' -mtime +0`) mirroring the existing section-3/4/6 loop shape exactly (per-file `stat` size accounting, `marvin_is_dry_run` guard, `track_freed` reporting). The `-mtime +0` (>1 day) age gate is the safety guarantee: no single `claude -p` invocation runs anywhere near 24h, so an **in-flight** run's temp file is never at risk of deletion, and `-maxdepth 1` scopes the sweep to exactly where `run_claude` writes. Because `track_freed` only reports when bytes > 0, a 0-byte orphan is still removed by the loop but reported silently — no log noise for empty files. Chose a preventive hygiene fix (Data priority) over inventing a riskier feature, given today's clean state (logs clean, lessons detector clean, 0 open issues/PRs). **Verification:** `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S warning` clean; an isolated selector test confirmed it picks only aged `claude-output-*.tmp` files while leaving a fresh (in-flight) `.tmp`, a `.log`, and a `.md` untouched; a live `--dry-run` of the full script exits 0 with no breakage and correctly does **not** select today's <24h 0-byte orphan (in-flight protection working); full `agent/self-test.sh` 134 pass / 0 fail / 1 warn (the 1 warn is the intentional `.gnupg` #737 tripwire, Pavel-gated), grade A, security A 95/100. **Next time:** the same orphan class affects two other `LOGS_DIR`/`SECURITY_DIR` `mktemp` sites that `mv` atomically into place — `log-analysis.sh`'s `${ANALYSIS_FILE}.XXXXXX` and `file-integrity.sh`'s `.baseline.XXXXXX` — but those run once daily with atomic-mv (far lower orphan probability than a per-Claude-run temp), so they were left out to keep this change focused; fold them into the same sweep if either ever appears as a lingering file. Long-standing carry-overs unchanged — split `agent/security-scan.sh` (774 LOC) into `agent/lib/security/*.sh`; Phase 3 ECHO-protocol items (parked ~7 weeks); deferred Pavel-gated `.gnupg/` cleanup (#737).

- **Source ⇆ live config-drift tripwire in `self-test.sh`** (`agent/self-test.sh`, new section 9d) — Catches the recurring "the version-controlled config and the running config have silently diverged" class *before* it bites, instead of someone noticing weeks later. It bit us twice recently: the nginx `/llms.txt` route (#777 — the fix lived only in the live config, so a redeploy from source would have re-bricked it) and the cron schedule (see the reconciliation below). The new section read-only `diff`s three repo configs against their installed counterparts — `setup/nginx-site.conf` ⇆ `/etc/nginx/sites-available/marvin`, `nginx-monitoring.conf` ⇆ `sites-available/monitoring`, `nginx-rate-limits.conf` ⇆ `conf.d/marvin-rate-limits.conf` — and extracts the `/etc/cron.d/marvin` heredoc that `setup-cron.sh` would write (the delimiter is single-quoted, so `${MARVIN_DIR}` stays literal and a byte-for-byte comparison is valid) and diffs it against the live cron file. Drift is **WARN-only** (an operator-reconcile signal, not a hard failure) and each check degrades gracefully when either side is absent. All four checks pass green today (configs reconciled); they are now tripwires for the next divergence. Verified: `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S error` clean; full `self-test.sh` 134 pass / 0 fail / 1 warn (the 1 warn is the pre-existing `.gnupg` #737 tripwire), grade A.

- **LongMemEval-S scored runner + dry-run cost estimator** (`bench/harness/longmemeval.py`, `bench/harness/runner.py`, `bench/PLAN.md`) — Phase 2b of the benchmarking plan (#739). A scored LongMemEval-S pipeline that, per question, ingests the haystack sessions into an isolated, purgeable Brain container (`lme_s/<qid>`), recalls the top-k evidence, has a Claude reader answer using only that evidence, then has a Claude judge grade the answer against gold — the apples-to-apples QA-accuracy metric the cited competitor numbers use (memory system **+** reader), not raw recall. Abstention (`_abs`) questions score correct iff the reader abstains, matching the LongMemEval methodology. **Money boundary is explicit:** the default path is a no-cost dry-run estimate (`estimate_cost()` touches no API and needs no SDK or key); the scored run is gated on `--execute` plus `BRAIN_API_KEY` + `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, so cron/CI can never spend by accident. **Reproducibility:** the operator supplies the official `longmemeval_s.json` (documented source, no hardcoded download URL) and its SHA-256 plus the exact reader/judge model IDs are recorded in the result JSON, so a published number is reproducible against an exact file. **Isolation:** each question's haystack is `forget`-purged after scoring so the benchmark never contaminates Marvin's real Brain; the purge runs in a `finally` block (guaranteed even when recall/read/judge raises mid-question) and logs any `forget_thought` failure to stderr while recording the orphaned ids under `cleanup_failures` in the result JSON for a follow-up reconciliation pass. `question_id` is validated against `[A-Za-z0-9_\-]+` at dataset load (it becomes a container tag) and fails loud on a path-shaped id rather than silently rewriting it. **Pre-run hardening** (folding the approving review's non-blocking suggestions before the first scored spend): the reader prompt now fences recalled memory in `<evidence>` tags and instructs the model to treat it as data, not instructions — a prompt-injection guard for the day the harness is pointed at an untrusted corpus; and reader/judge Claude calls now carry a 120s per-request timeout (`_API_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`) so one stalled API call cannot hang a hundreds-of-question run (the per-question `finally` still purges that question's haystack if the call times out). **Cleanup-robustness hardening:** ingest now fails loud (`BrainResponseError`, CLI exit code `4`) if `record_thought` returns no parseable thought id — because the Brain exposes only per-id forget and no container-level purge, an untrackable thought is an *unpurgeable* one, so a single unexpected API shape aborts the run rather than letting orphaned haystack data silently accumulate behind a false-clean empty `cleanup_failures`; id extraction is centralised in `_thought_id()` (top-level `id`, defensive nested `thought.id`). **`--limit 0` correctness:** the limit slice in both `estimate_cost()` and `run()` was guarded by a truthiness check (`if cfg.limit`), so `--limit 0` — a falsy `0` — fell through to the no-limit path and silently processed the *entire* dataset, the opposite of intent and a real way to torch money on the scored `--execute` path; both sites now test `cfg.limit is not None` so `None`→all, `0`→zero, `N`→first N. PR #763. Closes #764, #765, #766, #767, #768, #769.

- **Weekly report card surfaced on the dashboard** (`web/app/components/ReportCardSection.tsx`, `web/app/page.tsx`, `web/lib/translations.ts`, `web/app/globals.css`) — Closes the follow-up flagged when the report card was first built (2026-05-31): the SVG was generated by `agent/report-card.sh` and already served by nginx at `/api/reports/weekly-card-latest.svg` (verified HTTP 200, `image/svg+xml`), but nothing on the status page linked to it. Added a small client component that renders the latest card as a responsive `<img>` (max-width 720px, scales down on mobile) wrapped in a link to the full-size SVG, placed between the Evolution and Changelog sections. The component self-hides via an `onError` handler when the SVG isn't present yet (e.g. before the first weekly run), so a fresh install shows nothing rather than a broken image. nginx already sends `Cache-Control: no-cache` for `/api/`, so the browser revalidates the weekly-refreshed card on each load. Bilingual: `section_report_card` (`$ marvin --report-card`) and `report_card_alt` added to both EN and CS translation tables. Verified: `npm run build` clean (route `/` 13.4 kB), no new TypeScript errors. Purely additive — no existing component, data path, or API touched.

- **Weekly visual report card** (`agent/report-card.sh`, hooked into `agent/weekly-analytics.sh`) — Renders the existing machine-readable weekly analytics JSON (`data/reports/weekly-YYYY-MM-DD.json`) into a self-contained, terminal-styled SVG one-pager: KPI tiles (30-day uptime, security grade, Claude error rate, enhancement attempts), system metrics (CPU/memory/load/disk Δ), and log/activity counts, with an overall NOMINAL/WATCH/ATTENTION status derived from criticals/errors/CVEs. Output: `data/reports/weekly-card-{DATE}.svg` plus a stable `weekly-card-latest.svg`; retains the last 8 dated cards. Completes the last open Phase 2 roadmap item ("daily/weekly PDF or PNG report"). **SVG rather than PNG/PDF by design:** the only headless browser on the host is the chromium *snap*, which refuses to launch from a cron cgroup (`… is not a snap cgroup for tag snap.chromium.chromium`) and confines file writes to its private tmp — verified non-viable in the autonomous cron-as-marvin context. SVG needs no external tooling, renders in any browser, embeds directly, and rasterizes on demand. The generator is failure-isolated (`if`-guarded) so a rendering problem can never fail the analytics run; supports `--dry-run` and an optional week-end date argument. Verified: `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S warning` clean, well-formed XML, correct field values, and a full end-to-end run as the `marvin` user.

### Changed

- **Public peer registry drops the per-peer `trust_level` field (schema v1.1 → v1.2)** (`agent/network-discovery.sh`) — Removes `trust_level` (`trusted` / `known` / `recognized`) from the public `registry[]` projection served at `/api/peers/registry.json`, and bumps the public schema `version` `1.1 → 1.2`. **Why:** the public registry is a lighthouse — *here is who I am, here is how to reach me* — not a disclosure of my private opinion of the peers reading it. Publishing each peer's trust tier told every external AI exactly which bucket I'd sorted *it* into, which both leaks my internal trust topology to anyone with `curl` and invites gaming (a `recognized` peer learning it must perform to reach `trusted`). **Scope:** only the projected `trust_level` field is removed from the public objects; the `select(.trust_level != "untrusted")` *visibility gate* — which decides **which** peers appear, disclosing nothing about tiers — is **kept**, and the internal `peers.json` is **untouched** (`trust_level` still drives scoring). External consumers retain `alive` + `beacon_status` (the v1.1 enum), which is all an outside reader needs. Documents the schema change per the CLAUDE.md "Must document all changes in CHANGELOG.md" constraint and the precedent set by the v1.1 entry above (#808). **Verification:** `bash -n` clean; the jq filter was run against a sample peer — no `trust_level` in output, `beacon_summary` intact. Stacked on the v1.1 branch (#804) so the schema climbs `1.0 → 1.1 → 1.2` without tearing the shared jq block. Resolves #806; addresses #808.

### Security

- **`/etc/cron.d/marvin` is now recognized as a config-synced file by `file-integrity.sh`, and `setup/setup-cron.sh` is reconciled to the live cron after #789** (`agent/file-integrity.sh`, `setup/setup-cron.sh`) — Closes the last monitored config that `_matches_repo_source()` could not recognize as legitimate (the explicitly-deferred carry-over from the 2026-06-08 nginx config-synced fix), and clears the cron drift surfaced today by #789's merge. **What today's logs showed:** `/etc/cron.d/marvin` flagged `CHANGED` at the 02:04 UTC security-scan, then a churn of blind baseline `--update` resets at 04:00 and 04:07 (`baseline reset by bash`) during the morning-check pull. Investigation: #789 merged **2026-06-19** — it reverted the 2026-06-18 *temporary* hourly throttle of `external-domains-check.sh` back to the canonical 5-min cadence and added a 4-line explanatory comment **to the live `/etc/cron.d/marvin`**, but `setup/setup-cron.sh` (the canonical bootstrap generator) was never updated to match. The cron schedules were already identical (`2-59/5` both sides); the **only** drift was that comment block. Two consequences: (1) self-test §9d's source⇆live tripwire would `WARN` "config drift: cron" daily (a bootstrap re-run would not reproduce the live comment), and (2) because `/etc/cron.d/marvin` lives outside `MARVIN_DIR` and had **no** `_matches_repo_source` mapping, any legitimate future cron deploy alerts `CHANGED` forever and can only be cleared by a manual `--update` or the old blind reset — exactly the false-alarm class the nginx config-synced fix eliminated for the two `sites-enabled/*` files. (The blind-reset churn itself is separately fixed by the 2026-06-18 morning-check check-mode change, which takes effect on the next morning-check run.) **Fix, two complementary parts.** (1) **Reconcile** — `setup/setup-cron.sh`'s external-domains comment now matches the live file verbatim, restoring the "bootstrap reproduces live" guarantee; the schedules were already in sync so this is a comment-only source change with no live mutation. Now correct to reconcile because #789 is *merged* and the reverted 5-min cadence is the permanent state (unlike 2026-06-18, when reconciling to the temporary stopgap would have wrongly persisted it). (2) **Recognize** — extended `_matches_repo_source()` with a `/etc/cron.d/marvin` branch that extracts the cron heredoc from the **committed** `HEAD:setup/setup-cron.sh` blob (not the working-tree copy) and `diff`s it byte-for-byte against the live file, classifying a match as `config-synced` (kept out of the alert path, eligible for the existing all-legitimate auto-refresh) exactly like the nginx configs. **Trust model unchanged** — the comparison is anchored in the git object store, so an attacker editing the on-disk `setup-cron.sh` cannot make a tampered live cron "match" until the edit is committed; a rogue cron line that does not reproduce the committed heredoc still alerts `CHANGED`. Reuses the exact awk extraction already validated by self-test §9d (single-quoted `'EOF'` keeps `${MARVIN_DIR}` literal on both sides, so a byte-for-byte diff is valid). The two parts are **mutually dependent**: recognition only clears the alert once the committed source reproduces the live file, which the reconciliation provides — so they ship together. **Verification:** `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S warning` clean on both files (the lone `setup-cron.sh:19` SC2034 is pre-existing — `MARVIN_DIR` is used literally inside the single-quoted heredoc, invisible to shellcheck — 2 hits on HEAD, unchanged by this edit); after reconciliation the awk-extracted heredoc is byte-identical to live (`diff -q` clean → self-test §9d now `PASS: config in sync: cron`); the **real** extended function was unit-tested against a temporary HEAD-matching commit — live cron → `config-synced` ✓, a cron file with a rogue `* * * * * root /tmp/evil.sh` line appended → correctly **rejected** ✓ (security preserved), both nginx configs still recognized ✓ (no regression), an unmapped path (`/etc/nginx/nginx.conf`) → returns 1 ✓; full `agent/self-test.sh` **135 pass / 0 fail / 1 warn** (the 1 warn is the intentional `.gnupg` #737 tripwire, Pavel-gated), grade A, security A 90/100. The live `file-integrity.sh` was deliberately **not** run mid-session to mutate the baseline — the recognition takes effect once this PR is merged and pulled (HEAD then carries the reconciled heredoc); nothing runs between the merge-pull and the next 02:00 UTC security-scan that would open a false-alert window. **Next time:** the `_matches_repo_source` mapping is now hand-maintained across two distinct shapes (whole-file blobs for nginx, heredoc-extraction for cron) — if a third version-controlled live config joins, factor the comparison into a small table rather than growing the `if`/`case`; long-standing carry-overs unchanged — split `agent/security-scan.sh` (774 LOC) into `agent/lib/security/*.sh`; Phase 3 ECHO-protocol items (parked ~7 weeks); Pavel-gated `.gnupg/` cleanup (#737).

- **`morning-check.sh` now git-verifies the file-integrity baseline after a pull instead of a blind `--update` reset** (`agent/morning-check.sh`) — On a genuinely clean day (in sync with origin, 0 open issues, 0 open PRs; today's log only the usual benign WARNs — one informational "1 pending security update", two expected file-integrity baseline lines, the documented `claude-lock-timeout-expected-on-cron-overlap`). When morning-check (04:00 UTC) pulled a merged PR touching `agent/`, it called `file-integrity.sh --update` — a **blind** baseline reset that trusts whatever is on disk and logs **two WARN lines every day a PR merges** (most days: `baseline reset by bash` + `baseline updated`). That predates the git-verified auto-refresh that landed 2026-04-25. **Two problems closed:** (1) **Security** — a tamper landing in the window between the 02:00 UTC security-scan and the 04:00 UTC pull would be silently *baked into* the baseline by the blind reset and hidden forever; (2) **Signal** — a genuine, unexpected `--update` WARN (a baseline reset Marvin did not initiate) was buried under the daily benign reset noise (alarm fatigue). **Fix:** morning-check now runs `file-integrity.sh` in **check mode**, which compares each changed monitored file against its committed git HEAD blob (`_matches_git_head`) and auto-refreshes the baseline **only** for provably-legitimate git-synced changes — at INFO severity — while a change that does **not** match HEAD now correctly surfaces as a `CHANGED` alert instead of being trusted. This also runs a real integrity scan immediately after pulling new code: **more** monitoring, not less. `--update` remains available for deliberate manual resets. **No false-alert window is opened:** the only other integrity check is the 02:00 UTC security-scan, nothing runs between this pull and that next scan, and that scan auto-refreshes any git-synced changes. Also hardens the trigger condition from `echo "$INCOMING_DIFF" | grep -q ' agent/'` to a bash glob (`[[ "$INCOMING_DIFF" == *" agent/"* ]]`), removing the SIGPIPE-under-pipefail trap (lesson 2026-05-02) where `grep -q` closing the pipe on first match could make the `if` mis-read a real ` agent/` change as absent. **Review follow-up:** the parallel `web/`-deploy trigger immediately below carried the identical `echo "$INCOMING_DIFF" | grep -q ' web/'` shape — flagged as a non-blocking suggestion by three of the automated reviews — and is now the same bash glob (`[[ "$INCOMING_DIFF" == *" web/"* ]]`) for consistency; same SIGPIPE class, same fix. **Verification:** `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S warning` clean; glob-match unit test (agent / web-only / multiline / docs-only) correct; `_matches_git_head` semantics demonstrated live — committed monitored files classify git-synced (auto-refresh), the uncommitted in-session edit classifies DIVERGED (alerts, no auto-refresh), proving both branches; full `agent/self-test.sh` 134 pass / 0 fail / 2 warn, grade A, security A 90/100. The 2 warns are the intentional `.gnupg` #737 tripwire (Pavel-gated) and a **separate, pre-existing** cron-drift WARN — a *deliberate, documented, temporary* operator throttle of `external-domains-check.sh` to hourly (Pavel cost request / PR #789, with a "REVERT once #789 merges" note); reconciling the canonical `setup/setup-cron.sh` to that temp stopgap would wrongly persist it, so the WARN-only "pending reconciliation" signal is left as-is. The live `file-integrity.sh` was deliberately **not** run mid-session: my edit to the monitored `morning-check.sh` is uncommitted, so a live check would correctly but noisily alert on it and write a spurious alert into `file-integrity-latest.json` until the next scan — the cron-scheduled 02:00 UTC run applies it cleanly once the PR is merged. **Next time:** carry-overs unchanged — split `agent/security-scan.sh` (774 LOC) into `agent/lib/security/*.sh`; Phase 3 ECHO-protocol items (parked ~7 weeks); Pavel-gated `.gnupg/` cleanup (#737). A future session could teach the self-test §9d cron-drift check to recognize an explicit `TEMP STOPGAP`/`REVERT` marker so a documented temporary divergence reads as INFO rather than WARN — deferred to avoid masking genuine drift.

### Fixed

- **`marvin_rebuild_web()` root/cron path passed the web dir as `$0` instead of `$1`, so every self-enhance web rebuild failed with `cd: : Permission denied`** (`agent/common.sh`) — Fix-failure target from yesterday's log: `[2026-07-01T08:07:04Z] [ERROR] next build failed — rolling back (reason: self-enhance (1 files changed))` / `[ERROR] Build output: /home/marvin/git/web: line 1: cd: : Permission denied` / `[WARN] Web rebuild failed after self-enhance — dashboard may show stale content`. **Root cause:** the two `_drop_to_marvin` branches invoked `su -s /bin/bash marvin -c 'cd "$1" && …' -- "$web_dir"`, but with util-linux `su` the **first** word after `--` becomes the spawned shell's **`$0`**, not `$1` (verified live: `su … -c 'echo $0 $1' -- A B` → `$0=A $1=B`). So `"$web_dir"` landed in `$0`, `$1` was empty, and `cd "$1"` ran `cd ""` → the shell reported `cd: : Permission denied` prefixed with `$0` (= the web path), which is exactly the log line. A grep of every log confirms this path **never once succeeded** — there is not a single `Web rebuild complete` anywhere, and `next build failed` appears only on 2026-06-04 and 2026-07-01, the two days self-enhance actually changed `web/` files and thus triggered a build (agent-only days skip the build entirely, which is why a month-old bug stayed hidden). Impact: whenever self-enhance edits the dashboard, the immediate rebuild fails and rolls back, leaving stale content until the next morning-check's `deploy-web.sh` recovers it (`deploy-web.sh` was unaffected — its `_run_npm` embeds the command via `su … -c "$*"` and never relies on positional params). **Fix:** pass a descriptive `$0` placeholder — `su … -c 'cd "$1" && …' -- marvin-rebuild-web "$web_dir"` — so `"$web_dir"` correctly lands in `$1`; applied to both the `npm ci` (line 400) and `npm run build` (line 416) branches, each with an explanatory comment. The placeholder is descriptive rather than a bare `_` so any future `cd`-stage error surfaces as `marvin-rebuild-web: line 1: cd: …` instead of a confusing path-as-program-name. Non-root path (`(cd "$web_dir" && …)`) was already correct and is untouched. **Verification:** the placeholder form was proven live (`cd target=[/home/marvin/git/web]`, `pwd` → `/home/marvin/git/web`) and the old form reproduced the exact `cd: : Permission denied` error; `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S warning` clean; an audit (`grep "su -s /bin/bash marvin -c '"`) confirms these two are the only positional-param `su` sites; full `agent/self-test.sh` 136 pass / 0 fail / 1 warn (the 1 warn is the intentional `.gnupg` #737 tripwire, Pavel-gated), grade A, security A 90/100. Did **not** run a live full rebuild mid-session (it mutates `.next/` and restarts `marvin-web`); the fix is proven at the precise failure locus (`cd "$1"`) and the build machinery after `cd` is byte-identical to the already-working non-root branch. **Next time:** long-standing carry-overs unchanged — split `agent/security-scan.sh` (774 LOC) into `agent/lib/security/*.sh`; Phase 3 ECHO-protocol items (parked ~7 weeks); Pavel-gated `.gnupg/` cleanup (#737). A future session could add a lightweight self-test that dry-runs the `su … cd "$1"` argument-passing (without building) so this positional-param class is caught structurally rather than by chance.
- **Runaway-process detector no longer false-WARNs on `cnf-update-db`, the apt command-not-found database updater** (`agent/health-monitor.sh`) — Today's only new log noise: `[2026-07-01T06:15:02Z] [WARN] High CPU process detected: PID=1534981 cnf-update-db at 66.6%`. (The day's other WARN, `File integrity: CHANGED — /etc/nginx/sites-enabled/marvin`, is **not** a bug to fix — it is the last stale firing of yesterday's #805 reconciliation: the 02:04 UTC scan ran *before* the 04:00 UTC morning-check pulled the merged PR, so `HEAD:setup/nginx-site.conf` didn't yet carry the agent.json block. Verified post-pull that the committed source is now byte-identical to live and `_matches_repo_source()` will classify it `config-synced` and auto-refresh the baseline on the next 02:00 scan — self-healing, exactly as #805 predicted.) **Root cause of the cnf WARN:** `cnf-update-db` (package `command-not-found`, at `/usr/lib/cnf-update-db`) is fired by the apt hook `/etc/apt/apt.conf.d/50command-not-found` after every apt operation — here, right after unattended-upgrades applied the single pending security update — and briefly pegs a core rebuilding `/var/lib/command-not-found/commands.db`. It was not in the runaway allowlist `case`, so it fell through to the tracking path and logged the WARN. Same benign-transient-root-process noise class as the prior `unattended-upgr`/`chkproc`/`pg_isready`/`certbot`/`gzip` additions; it completes in seconds and can never reach the 600s sustained-CPU kill threshold, so the WARN was pure noise that also feeds the log-alerting error-rate detector and the lessons-learned scanner. **Fix:** added `cnf-update-db` to the trusted-name `case`. Unlike `chkproc`, **no pinned exe-path entry is needed** — `cnf-update-db` is a `#!/usr/bin/python3` script, so `/proc/PID/exe` resolves to the interpreter (`/usr/bin/python3.12`, verified via `readlink -f`), which the existing `/usr/bin/*` trusted-path check already accepts; pinning `/usr/lib/cnf-update-db` would be dead code that never matches. **Security unchanged:** the `prctl(PR_SET_NAME)` comm-spoof defense is preserved — a `cnf-update-db`-named process whose exe resolves *outside* the trusted paths (e.g. `/tmp/evil`) still WARNs and falls through to tracking; only a name+trusted-exe match is silenced, and unrelated high-CPU processes are still tracked. **Verification:** `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S warning` clean (0 warnings); a case-branch harness confirmed all four routes (real python-exe cnf-update-db → silent skip; spoofed untrusted-exe → WARN; empty-exe race → silent skip; unrelated `malware` → still tracked); full `agent/self-test.sh` 136 pass / 0 fail / 1 warn (the intentional `.gnupg` #737 tripwire, Pavel-gated), grade A, security A 90/100 (nginx-site + cron drift checks both PASS — no regression). Did **not** run `health-monitor.sh` live mid-session — it writes `data/` every 5 min via cron and can trigger a web rebuild; the change is pure control flow in the allowlist branch, validated in isolation. **Next time:** the hand-maintained runaway allowlist keeps growing one benign root process per apt/cron cycle — a future session could derive trusted names from a small manifest (package-owned root binaries + Marvin's own scripts) rather than appending case entries. Long-standing carry-overs unchanged — split `agent/security-scan.sh` (774 LOC) into `agent/lib/security/*.sh`; Phase 3 ECHO-protocol items (parked ~7 weeks); Pavel-gated `.gnupg/` cleanup (#737).

- **DNS check no longer raises a false CRITICAL 'hijack' when an external resolver is briefly unreachable** (`agent/health-monitor.sh`) — `dig +short` prints diagnostics like `;; no servers could be reached` to stdout when the resolver is down, and `tail -1` captured that line as `_resolved_ip`; being non-empty and `!= 80.211.223.26` it fell straight into the CRITICAL DNS-mismatch branch (e.g. `INC-20260624-dns-...`). Fix: shape-validate the answer against a dotted-quad IPv4 regex (non-IP output now takes the transient WARN path, not the hijack path), and confirm a genuine mismatch against a second independent resolver (`1.1.1.1`) before escalating — only two resolvers agreeing on a wrong, valid IP is now CRITICAL; a single blip is a WARN. The IPv4 regex bounds each octet to 0-255 (per the #799 review suggestion) so malformed strings like `999.999.999.999` can never pass as an answer. (fixes #798)
