- **Security score no longer mislabels unrelated warnings as `rootkit_scan` — pending security updates now scored on their own dimension** (`agent/self-test.sh`, section 9f) — Chosen on a genuinely clean day (in sync with origin, 0 open issues, 0 open PRs; today's log only the usual benign WARNs — one informational "1 pending security update", two expected file-integrity baseline auto-refreshes). The A–F security score's `rootkit_scan` dimension was keyed on `latest-scan.json`'s **`overall_status`**, but `security-scan.sh` sets `overall_status="warnings"` for a wide OR of *unrelated* conditions — pending apt security updates, unexpected listening ports, suspicious/high-rate connections, outbound anomalies, world-writable files, **or** an actual rkhunter warning. So today, with both rootkit scanners genuinely **clean** (`rkhunter: clean, chkrootkit: clean` in the 02:04 scan), the score still reported `rootkit_scan: warnings (-5)` — the −5 was actually the single pending security update, misattributed to rootkits. The same bug also **double-counted** world-writable files (they flip `overall_status` *and* are scored on their own line right below). **Fix:** `rootkit_scan` now reads the actual scanner verdicts — `.rkhunter.status` / `.chkrootkit.status` (`warnings` → −5) and `.rkhunter.infected + .chkrootkit.infected` (`>0` → INFECTED −40) — never `overall_status`. Pending security updates get a **new, correctly-labeled dimension** `pending_security_updates` reading `.cve_monitoring.upgradable_security` (`>0` → −5), so the security signal is **preserved, not dropped** — and now accurately attributed. **Net effect:** today's score is unchanged at 90 but honestly labeled (`rootkit_scan: clean (+0)`, `pending_security_updates: 1 (-5)`); a fully-clean day will now correctly read 95 instead of being silently docked; and a day with *both* rootkit warnings and pending updates now penalizes both (−10) instead of conflating them to a single −5 — a stricter, more accurate posture grade, never weaker. Does not touch logging or any security control. **Verification:** `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S warning` clean; a live `self-test.sh` run regenerated `data/security/security-score.json` with the two corrected labels and score 90 (grade A); full suite 135 pass / 0 fail / 1 warn (the 1 warn is the intentional `.gnupg` #737 tripwire, Pavel-gated). Only `agent/self-test.sh` changed — the regenerated `security-score.json` is runtime `data/` and not committed. **Next time:** the long-standing carry-overs are unchanged — split `agent/security-scan.sh` (774 LOC) into `agent/lib/security/*.sh`; Phase 3 ECHO-protocol items (parked ~7 weeks); deferred Pavel-gated `.gnupg/` cleanup (#737). The network warning sources (unexpected ports, suspicious/high-rate/outbound connections) that previously rode the mislabeled `rootkit_scan` −5 are deliberately *not* added to the hardening grade — they are transient operational state already surfaced by `incident-report.sh`/`log-alerting.sh`/the port inventory, not hardening configuration; revisit only if a genuine recurring exposure argues for it.

- **`hourly-check.sh` no longer crashes on a transient Claude `exit 1` — captures the exit code and skips the cycle gracefully** (`agent/hourly-check.sh`; companion runtime edit to `data/lessons-learned.json` and matcher fix in `agent/lessons-learned.sh`) — Today's fix-failure target, surfaced by the morning regression-watch WARN (`Found 5 recurring pattern(s) matching resolved lessons`). Line 149 was a bare `OUTPUT=$(run_claude "hourly-check" "$FULL_PROMPT")`. Under `set -euo pipefail` + `trap marvin_error_trap ERR`, when Claude returned a **transient** `exit 1` (the documented, resolved, intermittently-recurring `claude-exit-code-1-transient` condition — context overflow / API timeout / stochastic usage-policy classifier), the command substitution's non-zero status fired the ERR trap (`[ERROR] hourly-check.sh:149 — command failed (exit 1)`) and `set -e` killed the script **before the hourly report was saved** — no `=== HOURLY CHECK COMPLETE ===`. Confirmed in the logs on 2026-06-05, 06-10, and 06-11 (9 occurrences): every failing run shows `STARTING → Claude run complete (exit=1) → ERROR hourly-check.sh:149 →` *(nothing)*, and on 06-11 log-alerting escalated it to a "Repeated error (4×)" alert. Every *other* Claude-calling script already handles this — `github-interact.sh` (the sibling hourly task), `log-watcher.sh`, `morning-check.sh`, `evening-report.sh` — `hourly-check.sh` was the lone outlier. **Fix:** mirrored `github-interact.sh` exactly — `export CLAUDE_LOCK_TIMEOUT=60` (hourly task, a missed cycle is cheap; don't burn 5 min waiting on an overlapping run), then `OUTPUT=$(run_claude …) && CLAUDE_RC=0 || CLAUDE_RC=$?` to capture the code without tripping `set -e`. `exit 2` (lock timeout) → INFO skip + `exit 0` (next hourly run catches up); any other non-zero → WARN skip + `exit 0` (next hourly run is a cheap retry, per the `run_claude_with_retry` doc); empty output → WARN skip. The happy path (exit 0, non-empty) is unchanged and still saves the report. **Two companion changes to stop the same transient failures generating a daily false "possible regression" alarm:** (1) marked the `claude-exit-code-1-transient` lesson `expected_recurrence: true` in `data/lessons-learned.json` (a *runtime* file, gitignored — edited live on disk, not committed), exactly the treatment `claude-lock-timeout-expected-on-cron-overlap` already carries, and generalised its now-stale "github-interact tasks" wording to all tasks plus the capture-the-exit-code rule; (2) fixed the regression-watcher in `agent/lessons-learned.sh` so that when a log cluster's **best** matching resolved lesson is `expected_recurrence`, the whole cluster is treated as known/designed noise and skipped — previously it would fall through and re-flag the same `Claude exited with code 1` lines under an unrelated lesson (`exit-code-masking`, matched only by the coincidental shared `exit`/`code` tokens), a misleading false positive. **Verification:** `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S error` clean on both scripts; `data/lessons-learned.json` valid (`jq -e`); a behavioural harness under `set -euo pipefail` + ERR trap reproduces the OLD crash (trap fires, dies before COMPLETE, exit 1) and confirms the NEW path skips gracefully (exit 0, trap never fires) for exit 1 / exit 2 / empty, with the exit-0 happy path unchanged; re-running `lessons-learned.sh` drops the regression-watch count **5 → 1** (the lone survivor is the `claude-output-capture-data-loss` match on `hourly-check.sh:149` itself — the very bug this fixes, which disappears once deployed and no new ERROR lines accrue → 0 tomorrow), and the matcher still flags genuine non-expected lessons; full `agent/self-test.sh` 134 pass / 0 fail / 1 warn (the 1 warn is the intentional `.gnupg` #737 tripwire, Pavel-gated), grade A, security A 95/100.

- **CRITICAL-event alerts now window on recency — a self-healed transient no longer holds a "critical" alert active all day** (`agent/log-alerting.sh`) — Today's fix-failure target. At `04:09:39 UTC` `unattended-upgrades` (`apt-daily-upgrade.service`) stopped and immediately restarted nginx while applying a package upgrade; the 5-min health-monitor check at `04:10:02` caught nginx mid-restart, logged `[CRITICAL] nginx is down — testing config before restart`, and self-healed it (nginx confirmed up continuously since, blog content recovered). A textbook transient. But the resulting dashboard alert (`critical-cb65e4f01e72`) was **still active at 07:50**, ~4h later, and would have stayed active until the UTC day rolled over (~16 more hours). Root cause: section 2 of `log-alerting.sh` scanned the **entire day's log** for any `[CRITICAL]` line — once that line exists in today's log file it is permanent, so every hourly run re-detected it and the merge logic kept re-confirming the alert as active, never reaching the auto-resolve else-branch. Sections 3 (error-rate spike) and 6 (Claude failures) already window by recency; section 2 was the outlier. **Fix:** before detection, filter `critical_lines` to events from the **last 2 hours** using a lexicographic `>=` on the bracketed ISO-8601 timestamp field (the same technique section 3 uses; ISO-8601 sorts chronologically as text). The 2h window is deliberately wider than the 1h hourly-cron gap so a transient critical is never missed *between* runs, yet a recovered transient now ages out and auto-resolves in ~2-3h instead of lingering all day; an ongoing/recurring critical condition keeps producing fresh log lines every 5 min and correctly stays active. Fail-open: if `date` cannot produce a cutoff, all lines are kept rather than risk dropping a genuine alert. Only the section-2 match target changed — the recursive-alert filter, the merge/auto-resolve logic, and all other sections are untouched. **Verification:** `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S error` clean; against the **live** `data/logs/2026-06-10.log` the 2h window drops the now-4h-old nginx event (1 → 0 lines) so the next run auto-resolves the stuck alert; a synthetic two-event log (one 5h-old nginx critical + one 10-min-old `Disk usage at 97%`) confirms only the recent critical survives the window while the stale one is dropped. The currently-stuck alert self-clears on the first run of this code post-deploy (and at the latest tonight at `00:50 UTC` day-rollover under the old code, since the new day's log has no critical line). Did **not** run the live script mid-session: the unpatched hourly cron at `:50` would immediately re-raise the alert until this lands, so a manual clear would only flap.

- **Runaway-process detector skips PIDs that have already exited — kills the daily `sh`/`python`/`runc` false-positive WARNs from the Brain healthchecks** (`agent/health-monitor.sh`) — Most frequent unexpected WARN this week (5× `High CPU process detected: PID=… sh at 60-66%`, plus single `python`/`runc` hits). Root cause: the Marvin-Brain `docker-compose.yml` healthchecks use `CMD-SHELL`, so each runs as a sub-second `sh -c "wget …"` / `sh -c "pg_isready …"` / `sh -c "python -c …"` (and the transient `runc` exec that launches the container), bursting one core to 60-100% for the instant `ps` samples it. By the time the runaway loop reads that PID's cgroup, the process is already gone — so the empty cgroup read makes the #761 container guard unable to classify it, and it falls through to be logged as a runaway and recorded in `runaway-procs.json` for a kill that can never land (the stale-PID cleanup at the end of the loop just deletes the entry next run). Confirmed the signature: every flagged PID this week was single-sighting, none escalated to a kill, and `runaway-procs.json` stays `{}`. **Fix:** added a liveness guard (`[[ ! -d "/proc/${proc_pid}" ]] && continue`) immediately after the cgroup container check and before the exe-verification/tracking path — a process that has already exited cannot be a >10-min sustained runaway and there is nothing to kill, so it is skipped silently. This mirrors the liveness check already present in the `find|git` branch (#547) and the stale-entry cleanup pass. **No protection weakened:** the guard only skips *dead* PIDs; a genuine runaway, or a live attacker spoofing an allowlisted comm via `prctl(PR_SET_NAME)`, is alive across samples so the guard always passes for it and the existing exe-path/cgroup checks still run. **Verification:** `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S error` clean; a live end-to-end `health-monitor.sh` run exits 0 and writes valid `status.json` (`healthy`) and `runaway-procs.json` (`{}`); full `agent/self-test.sh` 134 pass / 0 fail / 1 warn (the 1 warn is the intentional `.gnupg` #737 tripwire, Pavel-gated), grade A, security A 90/100.

- **`_matches_repo_source()` compares against the committed git blob, not the on-disk `setup/` copy — closes the trust-chain gap** (`agent/file-integrity.sh`) — The helper added earlier in this branch `diff`ed the live nginx config against `${MARVIN_DIR}/setup/nginx-site.conf` **on disk**. But `setup/nginx-site.conf` is not in `MONITORED_PATHS`, so an attacker with write access to `${MARVIN_DIR}/setup/` could deploy a malicious live config, edit the disk source to match it (no commit needed), and the helper would classify it `config-synced` — silently auto-refreshing the baseline over the compromise. That made the claimed "same trust model as `_matches_git_head()`" technically false: `_matches_git_head()` anchors on the immutable git object store (`git hash-object` vs `HEAD:`), the disk `diff` did not. Replaced the disk comparison with `git -C "$MARVIN_DIR" show "HEAD:setup/…" | diff - "$filepath"`, so the comparison is now rooted in the committed blob and an uncommitted disk-source tamper no longer produces a false match — making the trust model genuinely equivalent. Verified with a synthetic git repo: a live file matching the committed blob still matches, while a malicious live config "matched" only by an uncommitted disk-source edit correctly fails. `bash -n` clean. (fixes #780)
- **`file-integrity.sh` recognises a live config that byte-matches its committed `setup/` source as a legitimate deploy, not tampering** (`agent/file-integrity.sh`) — Root-cause fix for a recurring daily false alert. `/etc/nginx/sites-enabled/marvin` was flagged `CHANGED` on **two consecutive days** (2026-06-07 and 2026-06-08), with the baseline stuck at `2026-06-06T04:00:03Z` — and it would have alerted every single day until someone ran a manual `file-integrity.sh --update`. The live nginx config was changed at `2026-06-06 18:03` (the #777 `/llms.txt` deploy applied to live), *after* that morning's 04:00 baseline. The existing auto-refresh helper `_matches_git_head()` couldn't clear it: that helper only recognises files **inside** `MARVIN_DIR` whose content matches their own path's blob at git HEAD, but the live config lives at `/etc/nginx/sites-enabled/marvin` while its version-controlled source is `setup/nginx-site.conf` — a different path, outside the git tree. So legitimately-deployed configs alert forever. Verified the live config is now **byte-identical** to `setup/nginx-site.conf` (and `monitoring` ⇆ `setup/nginx-monitoring.conf`), i.e. exactly the "legitimate, version-controlled change" case. **Fix:** added `_matches_repo_source()` — the config-file analogue of `_matches_git_head()` — which maps each monitored live config to its committed `setup/*.conf` counterpart and returns success only on a **byte-for-byte `diff`** match. Changed files matching it are classified `config-synced` (a new category parallel to `git-synced`): kept out of the CHANGED/alert path, surfaced in a new `config_synced` report array, and — critically — made eligible for the existing baseline auto-refresh, which now fires when *all* changes are provably legitimate (`git_synced` **or** `config_synced`) and there are zero `CHANGED`/`MISSING`/`NEW` files. **Trust model unchanged:** this is the *same* assumption `_matches_git_head` already makes — an attacker editing nginx to add a rogue `location`/proxy would not reproduce the committed source byte-for-byte, so a match is a strong "legitimate deploy" signal, not a weakening. Paths with no `setup/` counterpart (e.g. `/etc/nginx/nginx.conf`) still return 1 and alert if changed; new files in dynamic paths still require an explicit `--update` (the #633 guard is untouched). Report consumers are unaffected — `security-scan.sh` and `self-test.sh` only read `.status`/`.changes`/`.missing_files`; the new key is purely additive. **Verification:** `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S error` clean; a live run reclassified the nginx file as `config-synced`, set `status: clean`, and auto-refreshed the stuck baseline (also absorbing the legitimate `self-test.sh` change from #778 as `git-synced`) — the equivalent of a manual `--update`, but gated on provable legitimacy; full `agent/self-test.sh` 134 pass / 0 fail / 1 warn (the 1 warn is the intentional `.gnupg` #737 tripwire, Pavel-gated), grade A, security A 95/100.

- **`setup/setup-cron.sh` reconciled to the live `/etc/cron.d/marvin` — bootstrap no longer silently downgrades the running schedule** (`setup/setup-cron.sh`) — Completes the "broader reconciliation" deferred when the disk-cleanup gap was patched on 2026-06-05. The canonical bootstrap cron generator had drifted badly from the live crontab: a `setup-cron.sh` re-run (the documented disaster-recovery path) would have **dropped four active jobs** — `backup.sh` (03:00), `security-scan.sh` (04:00), `fix-issues.sh`, `incident-report.sh` — **re-added the operator-removed afternoon self-enhance run** (`0 10,15` → live `0 10`), reverted the deliberate Claude-API call staggering (`github-interact` `0 *` vs live `5 */2`; `hourly-check` `0 *` vs live `35 *`), and — most dangerously — **lost the `/root/.local/bin` PATH entry** the `claude` CLI is installed under, which would break every cron job that invokes Claude. The live file is the validated source of truth (the system runs at codebase-health 97/100), so the heredoc body was rewritten to reproduce it **byte-for-byte** (verified: extract the heredoc, `diff` against `/etc/cron.d/marvin` → identical). The human-readable schedule summary at the foot of the script was updated to match, and a header note documents that `weekly-analytics.sh` intentionally lives in root's *personal* crontab (Sun 11:30), not in this file, so it is not duplicated here. A companion self-test tripwire (above) now WARNs on any future cron/nginx drift. No live mutation — only the repo source was changed; the running cron was already correct.

- **`/llms.txt` is now actually served — source config matches the live fix** (`setup/nginx-site.conf`) — The `llms.txt` AI-agent welcome file was committed to `web/public/llms.txt` 35 days ago (the SEO / AI-Overviews foundation, #650/#652) but was never *routed*: the Next.js standalone server has no such route, so every request fell through to a **silent 404** for 35 days — the one well-known door other agents would use to introduce themselves, advertised but reachable by no one. Found 2026-06-06 while auditing the beacon/discovery surface. The fix — an nginx `location = /llms.txt` block that `alias`es the file straight off disk (`max-age=86400`, `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`) — was applied to the **live** `/etc/nginx/sites-available/marvin` and reloaded (verified `HTTP 200`, 2442 bytes), but the change lived only in the working tree: the canonical `setup/nginx-site.conf` was never updated, so a redeploy from source would have re-bricked the door. This commit lands that exact block in the source of truth (verified byte-identical to the deployed config), closing the drift. Purely additive — one new `location` block above `/.well-known/ai-negotiate`; no existing route touched. (Note: nginx maps the `.txt` extension to `text/plain` via `mime.types`, so the block's `default_type text/markdown` is inert for this file and the endpoint serves `text/plain`; harmless, left as-is to keep source byte-identical to live.)

- **`log-watcher.sh` matches nginx interest patterns against the request only, not the referer/User-Agent — killing the dashboard-poll flood at its root** (`agent/log-watcher.sh`) — Root-cause fix for the same incident class the previous two sessions patched with a growing blocklist. This morning's `00:30 UTC` log-watcher run (the last firing of the pre-#773 code before the `04:00` morning-check deployed the fix) logged `[WARN] Prompt too large (2603526 chars) — truncating to 400000` (~651K tokens) — **2.6 MB** of "interesting" data, 3.7× the 703 KB that triggered #773 the day before. #773's deploy at `~04:03` (the noise-cut is visible in the live log: 185 interesting lines at 04:00 → 16 at 04:30 → 5-20 thereafter) plus its hard 50 KB cap resolved the live symptom, so this is **not a live failure** — but the *cause* remained: the interest pattern `marvin\.cz` is a substring of `robot-marvin.cz`, and **every** dashboard request carries the referer `https://robot-marvin.cz/`, so a plain `grep -iE "$INTEREST_RE"` flagged every poll. The `WEB_NOISE_PATTERNS` blocklist patched this per endpoint, but any *new* dashboard feed silently re-introduced the leak — exactly how 703 KB became 2.6 MB in one day. **Structural fix:** for the nginx/access branch only, strip the trailing referer + user-agent quoted fields (combined-log-format `… "$referer" "$user_agent"`) before interest-matching, via a single `gawk` pass (`IGNORECASE=1`, regex passed through the environment — not `awk -v`, which would mangle the `\.` in `marvin\.cz`). Keywords are now matched only against the request line itself; the original full line (referer/UA included) is still emitted for Claude's context — only the *match target* is narrowed. Genuine inbound AI signals are POSTs/GETs to a path (`/.well-known/ai-negotiate`) or carry a keyword in the request query — all preserved; a verified synthetic `POST /.well-known/ai-negotiate` carrying *our own* dashboard referer is still kept. Referer/UA-only matches (our dashboard referer, a `claude-bot` UA) are never communication attempts and are correctly dropped. The system-log branch is untouched, so `marvin@` still matches mail logs. `WEB_NOISE_EXCLUDE` is kept as a cheap pre-filter for crawler UAs that hit well-known paths. **Measured on the live `/var/log/nginx/access.log`:** interest matches dropped from **7,247 → 3** (the 3 survivors are real request-path attack probes containing `hello`/`Hello`, correctly surfaced for Claude to classify — not referer noise). Now a dashboard endpoint added tomorrow can never re-flood, because no dashboard GET to a JSON/SVG feed carries an interest keyword in its request line. Verified: `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S error` clean; full `self-test.sh` 130 pass / 0 fail / 1 warn (the 1 warn is the intentional `.gnupg` tripwire for #737), grade A.
- **`log-watcher.sh` no longer floods Claude with dashboard-poll noise or overshoots its feed cap** (`agent/log-watcher.sh`) — Today's 02:30 UTC run logged `[WARN] Prompt too large (709106 chars) — truncating to 400000` (~177K tokens): `log-watcher` found **703 KB** of "interesting" log data and handed it to `run_claude`, which crudely hard-truncated it. Two compounding bugs. **(1) Root cause — dashboard polling matches the interest filter.** The web-log interest pattern includes `marvin\.cz`, and `robot-marvin.cz` *contains* `marvin.cz`, so **every** dashboard API request — which all carry the referer `https://robot-marvin.cz/` — matches as "interesting". The `WEB_NOISE_PATTERNS` exclusion list covered only a handful of endpoints (status.json, comms-summary.json, …) and missed the bulk of the dashboard's continuously-polled feeds (metrics/recent.json, incidents/summary.json, alerts/active-alerts.json, peers-public.json, external-domains.json, thoughts.json, sla.json, changelog.json, the report-card SVG, blog GETs). Result: **13,115** access.log lines matched the interest filter on a busy browsing day. Fix: added generic `GET /api/[^ ]*\.json`, `GET /api/[^ ]*\.svg`, and `GET /api/blog` exclusions — a GET to a public JSON/SVG data feed is never an AI communication attempt (real signals are POSTs to `/.well-known/ai-negotiate`, which is **not** under `/api/` and is still matched by the interest patterns). Measured on the live access.log: interest matches dropped from **6,528 → 2** (the 2 survivors are harmless Facebook `fbclid` click-throughs), and `/.well-known/` fetches are confirmed *not* caught by the new patterns. **(2) Structural — `MAX_FEED_SIZE` was a soft, post-hoc check.** The 50 KB cap was only tested *after* a whole file's interesting block had been appended, so a single high-volume file (nginx access.log) could overshoot by 14×. Fix: bound each per-file block to the remaining budget *before* appending (truncate + `break`), and switch the loop guard to `-ge`. This enforces the documented 50 KB contract as a hard ceiling, so a future noise source from any file can never again force `run_claude`'s expensive 400 KB truncation. Verified: `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S error` clean; an isolation test confirms a simulated 700 KB file is truncated to fit and the total never exceeds 50 KB. The two fixes are complementary — (1) removes the cause, (2) caps the blast radius of any future cause.
- **`bench/longmemeval` dry-run estimator no longer overstates judge spend on abstention questions** (`bench/harness/longmemeval.py`) — `estimate_cost()` counted judge-model input tokens for *every* question, but the scored pipeline in `run()` only calls the Claude judge for non-abstention questions — `_abs` questions are graded by a local string check (the reader must abstain) and never spend judge tokens. The dry-run estimate (the default, money-sizing path before an `--execute` spend) therefore overstated judge cost by one judge call per abstention question, which in LongMemEval-S is a meaningful fraction of the set. Fixed by excluding abstention questions (`if not _is_abstention(q)`) from the `judge_in` sum, mirroring the actual pipeline. Reader-token estimate is unchanged (the reader runs on every question). Verified on a 2-question set with one `_abs`: the abstention question drops out of the judge total (627→314 tokens on the test inputs) while the reader estimate is unchanged. Advances #739.
- **`disk-cleanup.sh` is now actually scheduled** (`setup/setup-cron.sh`, live `/etc/cron.d/marvin`) — Found during the 2026-06-05 morning check: `data/logs/` had accumulated 2,483 markdown run-logs older than 14 days (back to 2026-05-01), and the journal had grown to 158 MB. The dedicated `agent/disk-cleanup.sh` (prunes run logs >14d, daily logs >30d, compresses metrics JSONL, vacuums journal) exists, is executable, passes `bash -n`, and is even referenced by `health-monitor.sh:324` as running "at 01:00 UTC" — but it was **never wired into cron**. It appears in neither the canonical `setup/setup-cron.sh`, the live `/etc/cron.d/marvin`, nor any wrapper script, so it had never run autonomously. Ran it manually during the check (freed 53 MB: 42 MB of run logs + journal 158 MB→40 MB, disk 41%→40%), then added a `0 1 * * *` entry to both the live cron file and the canonical setup script. 01:00 local is an empty slot that runs before the 02:xx aggregators and the 03:00 backup, so cleanup precedes the snapshot. The new `marvin-cleanup.log` is already covered by the existing `/var/log/marvin-*.log` logrotate glob. This is one facet of the known live-vs-`setup-cron.sh` drift; only the disk-cleanup gap is addressed here, not the broader reconciliation.
- **`bench/longmemeval` `--limit 0` now processes zero questions, not all** (`bench/harness/longmemeval.py`) — Closes #769. Both `estimate_cost()` (line 168) and `run()` (line 241) guarded the limit slice with a truthiness check (`if cfg.limit` / `if cfg.limit else`), which treats `0` as falsy and falls through to the "no limit" path — the exact opposite of intent. Since `RunConfig.limit` is typed `int | None` where `0` is a meaningful value distinct from "unset", the correct idiom is `is not None`, applied at both sites. This is a **cost-safety** fix: on the paid `--execute` path, `--limit 0` previously processed the *entire* dataset (full haystack ingestion + reader/judge spend) instead of being the no-op a zero limit implies. The original two-line fix was authored as #770 against the pre-merge feature branch; once #763 squash-merged into `main`, #770 became unrebaseable (its whole 7-commit feature history now collides with the single squash commit), so the fix is re-landed here as a clean delta on `main`. Verified `python3 -m py_compile` clean; `None`/positive-N paths unchanged.

- **Runaway-process killer no longer reaches into Docker-managed containers** (`agent/health-monitor.sh`) — Caught latent on 2026-06-04 while auditing 7 days of recurring `High CPU process detected` WARNs. The runaway detector flags any process >50% CPU and `kill -9`s it after 10 min of sustained load (health-monitor.sh:382). It recognised trusted *host* binaries by `comm` name + verified exe path, but had no concept of container provenance — so processes inside the Marvin-Brain Docker stack (`postgres`, `lightrag`, `worker`, `api`, `mcp`, plus `grafana`/`prometheus`/`otel-collector`) surfaced on the host with generic comm names (`python`, `python3`, `uvicorn`, `pg_isready`, `runc`) and were treated as host processes. **Two problems:** (1) **Correctness/stability** — a legitimate LightRAG indexing or embedding job that sustains >50% CPU for >10 min would have been killed *from the host*, corrupting a container Docker already manages via cgroup limits + restart policy. No kill had fired yet, but the WARN audit (5× `pg_isready`, 4× `python`, 3× `runc`, 2× `python3`, etc. over 7 days) showed the brain stack routinely crossing the threshold; the risk grows as the stack does more work. (2) **Security** — the established noise-cut reflex would be to name-allowlist these, but `python`/`sh`/`uvicorn` are exactly the names a malicious or buggy host process carries, so allowlisting by name re-opens the hole the killer exists to close. **Fix:** before the name-based allowlist, read `/proc/PID/cgroup` and `continue` (log INFO, never track for kill) when it matches `/docker[-/]` — `docker-<hash>.scope` (cgroup v2, this host) or `/docker/<hash>` (cgroup v1). cgroup membership is assigned by Docker through cgroupfs (root-writable only) and **cannot be forged** via `prctl(PR_SET_NAME)` the way the `comm` field can (#38) — so this is a *stronger* trust signal than the name allowlist, not a weaker one. Host daemons (`docker.service`, `containerd.service`, `cron.service`, `nginx.service`) do **not** match the `[-/]` class and remain fully subject to runaway detection. The existing `pg_isready`/`runc*`/`gzip` name-allowlist entries are kept as belt-and-suspenders for the rare race where the cgroup read hits a just-exited process and returns empty. **Verified:** `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S error` clean; all 8 brain-stack container PIDs classify as `CONTAINER(skip)` and all four host daemons as `HOST(check)`; full `health-monitor.sh` run exits 0 with valid `status.json`. Codified as lesson `host-runaway-killer-must-not-kill-container-procs` (general principle: a host-level resource killer must scope itself to host processes and never reach into a cgroup another supervisor owns).

- **Container-exclusion cgroup read no longer truncates on cgroup v1/hybrid hosts** (`agent/health-monitor.sh`) — Follow-up to the container-provenance guard above. That guard read `/proc/PID/cgroup` with `head -c 512` on the assumption "cgroup files are tiny". True on **this** host (cgroup v2: a single ~50-byte line `0::/system.slice/docker-<hash>.scope`), but on a **cgroup v1 or hybrid** host the file carries one line per controller subsystem (`pids`, `memory`, `cpuset`, `blkio`, …) — 13+ lines at ~45 chars each, comfortably exceeding 512 bytes. If `head -c 512` truncated before the `/docker/<hash>` lines, the `=~ /docker[-/]` test would return false, silently dropping a containerized `python`/`uvicorn` process through to the name allowlist and back into the host-kill path — re-opening the exact bug the guard exists to close. Fix: read the whole file with `cat` instead of capping the byte count. `/proc/PID/cgroup` is a virtual procfs file with no real I/O cost, so reading it fully is free. Verified: `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S error` clean. Fixes #762.

- **`log-watcher.sh` no longer dies (exit 5) when Claude returns non-array JSON** (`agent/log-watcher.sh`) — Recurring failure: `[ERROR] log-watcher.sh:354 — command failed (exit 5): merged=$(echo "$existing" "$analysis_json" | jq -s 'add' …)` on 2026-05-27 and again 2026-06-01. Root cause: the analysis JSON extracted from Claude's raw output by `sed -n '/^\[/,/^\]/p' | head -500` (line 336) was only checked for **non-emptiness** (line 343), never for **validity**. When Claude returns a single JSON object instead of an array, or `head -500` truncates a long array mid-structure, `jq -s 'add'` is handed `[existing_array, non_array]` and exits **5** (confirmed: `add` on array+object / truncated / number / string all exit 5). Under `set -euo pipefail` + `trap marvin_error_trap ERR`, the failing command substitution killed the script at line 354 — **before** the graceful `if [[ -n "$merged" ]] && … jq empty` fallback on line 355 could run — silently dropping that cycle's `incoming-signals.json` update and logging a spurious ERROR. **Two-layer fix:** (1) a root-cause validation gate after the empty checks — `if ! echo "$analysis_json" | jq -e 'type == "array"'` saves the raw output for forensics and exits 0 cleanly, mirroring the existing "could not parse" handling and stopping garbage before it reaches *any* downstream `jq` (the signals merge, the `comm_entries`/`attack_count`/`comm_count` extractions); (2) defense-in-depth `|| true` on the merge substitution so even an unforeseen incompatible-shape merge degrades to the graceful handler instead of tripping `set -e` (lesson `exit-code-masking`). Verified: `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S error` clean; faithful set-e reproduction confirms OLD dies exit 5 (matching the log) while NEW reaches the handler and exits 0; the gate blocks all four exit-5 input shapes; the happy path (valid array) is unchanged. Full `self-test.sh` 130 pass / 0 fail / 1 warn (the 1 warn is the intentional `.gnupg/` tripwire for #737), grade A.
- **SLA `overall_uptime_pct` no longer exceeds 100%** (`agent/metric-aggregate.sh`) — The per-day `uptime_pct` is clamped to 100 in `_calculate_day_uptime` (a day with extra metric samples from clock drift or duplicate cron runs can record `samples > expected`), but the 30-day `overall_uptime_pct` aggregate re-derived the figure from the raw `sum(samples) / sum(expected)` totals, bypassing that clamp — which surfaced publicly as `100.05%` on `weekly-card-latest.svg` and `data/reports/weekly-2026-05-30.json`. Fix: clamp each day's `samples` to its `expected` (`min(samples, expected)`) inside the jq numerator before summing, so the aggregate can never exceed 100%. Verified with synthetic over-sampled days: a case that previously reported `100.52%` now caps at `100`. Fixes #756.
- **`agent/report-card.sh` well-formedness guard now actually fires on this host (python3 fallback)** (`agent/report-card.sh`) — The SVG validation added in #754 ("a malformed dated card must not silently overwrite a good latest card") was gated solely on `command -v xmllint`. But `xmllint` is **not installed** on this VPS, so in the real cron-as-marvin context the entire guard was a no-op: a malformed dated card would have been `cp`'d straight over `weekly-card-latest.svg`. Discovered during the 2026-06-01 sync-learn review of #751. Fix: add a `python3` fallback (python3 is always present here — it runs the bench harness) that parses the card with `xml.dom.minidom`, and a final `WARN`-and-skip branch if neither validator exists, so the defense-in-depth the author intended is now live. Verified: `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S warning` clean, a full end-to-end run still produces a well-formed `weekly-card-latest.svg`, and the python3 path correctly rejects a deliberately malformed SVG (exits non-zero, latest left untouched).

- **Two Marvin-Brain Docker-stack noise cuts: `runc` runaway allowlist + process-count anomaly baseline** (`agent/health-monitor.sh`) — Both WARNs trace to the steady-state shift caused by the Marvin-Brain Docker stack (deployed 2026-05-25). **(1)** `runc at 100%` / `runc:[2:INIT] at 100%` runaway-process WARNs (4 hits in 14 days: 2026-05-27, 05-31, 06-01, 06-02) — `runc` is the OCI container runtime (`/usr/bin/runc`) that containerd launches for the brain stack; both the plain `runc` exec and the `runc:[2:INIT]` container-init child briefly peg one core during container (re)starts and healthcheck cycles. Added `runc*` to the runaway-process allowlist `case "$proc_name"` at health-monitor.sh:287 — the glob covers both comm shapes. Same trusted-binary pattern as the existing `pg_isready`/`gzip`/`shellcheck` entries; the exe-path verification below still requires `/usr/bin/runc` (or `/usr/sbin`, `/usr/local/bin`, `/snap`), so a comm-spoofed `runc`-named process binding an untrusted exe still trips the killer. **(2)** Recurring process-count anomaly false positive — `Anomaly: Processes = 200 (avg=182.29, stddev=3.65, deviation=4.9σ)` (fired 2026-05-25, 05-29, and 06-02; flagged for action in the 2026-05-30 entry "if either pattern repeats this week"). Root cause is identical to the already-fixed **Memory MB** case: the anomaly check compared an *instantaneous* 5-min `process_count` reading — which naturally rises toward the daily peak (~200) during cron runs and container churn — against the rolling *daily-average* baseline (~182, ~15 below the peak) with a tight stddev floored at 2% of the mean. Switched the process-count baseline from `.summary.process_count.avg` to `.summary.process_count.max` (health-monitor.sh:109/127), mirroring the Memory MB comment/logic. With the daily-max baseline the 7-day band is mean≈195, 2σ-high≈211: today's 200 sits comfortably inside (no alert), while a genuine process explosion past ~211 — like the real 2026-05-17 spike to 212 — still fires *and* clears the unchanged absolute `min_threshold=200` floor. Verified: `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S error` clean on `health-monitor.sh`; a live end-to-end run logs no process anomaly and writes `anomalies: []`; full `self-test.sh` 130 pass / 0 fail / 1 warn (the 1 warn is the intentional `.gnupg/` tripwire for #737), grade A.

- **Two cron-overlap noise cuts: github-interact Claude lock + gzip runaway allowlist** (`agent/github-interact.sh`, `agent/health-monitor.sh`, `data/lessons-learned.json`) — Same-shape pair from the daily WARN audit. **(1)** `github-interact.sh` calls `run_claude("github-interact", …)` once per hourly cron (cron line `5,35 * * * *`). The 08:05 UTC slot collides with `self-enhance.sh` at 08:00 UTC, which routinely holds the `/tmp/marvin-claude.lock` for 6-10 minutes. With `CLAUDE_LOCK_TIMEOUT` defaulting to 300s, github-interact waited 5 minutes then logged `[WARN] Claude lock timeout after 300s — skipping github-interact (another Claude run is active)`. Audit: 10 hits in the last 15 days (2026-05-15, 16, 22, 25, 26, 27, 28 confirmed via `data/logs/*.log`), all at 08:10 UTC, all driven by the same overlap. log-watcher.sh:323 already had the canonical fix from PR #700 (2026-05-15) — `export CLAUDE_LOCK_TIMEOUT=60` to fail fast, plus an exit-code-2 branch that logs an INFO `skipped — next … run will catch up` instead of the misleading `[ERROR] No response from Claude for GitHub decisions` + `exit 1` shape. Applied the same shape at github-interact.sh:209-217: cap timeout at 60s (github-interact runs every 30 min, so a missed cycle is cheap — the next run picks up the same work), capture the exit code with `&& CLAUDE_RC=0 || CLAUDE_RC=$?` (lesson `exit-code-masking`), and short-circuit cleanly on exit 2 before the empty-response check. The existing empty-response branch still fires on legitimate "no response" cases (process killed, API outage); only the lock-timeout shape now logs INFO instead. Verified `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S error` clean. **(2)** `gzip at 100.0% CPU` runaway WARN at 01:00 UTC from `disk-cleanup.sh` compressing old metrics JSONL files (and occasionally from `logrotate`). Same shape as `shellcheck`/`pg_isready`/`apt*` already in the allowlist — added `gzip` to the `case "$proc_name"` branch at health-monitor.sh:287. The exe-path verification at line 299-303 still requires `/usr/bin/gzip` so a comm-field-spoofed `gzip` named process still trips the runaway killer. Inline comment explains the disk-cleanup/logrotate sources. Only 2 hits in the last 7 days (today + 2026-05-23), but cheap to silence and aligns with the established noise-cut pattern from the 2026-05-25 (shellcheck) and 2026-05-26 (pg_isready) PRs. **(3)** Extended lesson #26 `claude-lock-timeout-expected-on-cron-overlap` in `data/lessons-learned.json` to reference both safeguard locations (log-watcher.sh:323-329 and now github-interact.sh:209-217), bumped occurrences 4 → 14 (the 10 github-interact cases were never counted), updated the lesson body to describe the github-interact pattern alongside log-watcher, and reframed the safeguard as the *general principle* (any script whose Claude call overlaps with a higher-priority once-a-day task should cap timeout + handle exit 2) rather than a log-watcher-specific recipe. Both shells (`bash -n` + `shellcheck -S error`) clean; self-test 128 pass / 0 fail / 1 warn, grade A; `lessons-learned.sh` reports `No new recurring patterns detected` and `No recurring resolved-lesson patterns detected`.

- **Trim incident-specific narrative from net-anomaly wrap-detection comment** (`agent/metric-aggregate.sh`) — The comment block above the wrap-skipping jq guard at metric-aggregate.sh:207 (added in PR #744) hardcoded the 2026-05-29 incident's dates, byte values (`rx 10.1 GB → 57 MB`), and sigma figures (`avg=83.67`, `2.2σ`). Per `CLAUDE.md`'s coding conventions ("Don't reference the current task, fix, or callers… they belong in the PR description and rot as the codebase evolves") and the proposed rewrite in #745, kept the invariant (counter-wrap → unreliable zeros → baseline pollution → 2σ false alarm), added the new "either-direction wrap excludes the whole day" line that documents the `or` in the jq guard below, retained the `baseline-pollution-from-sentinel-zero` lesson pointer, and dropped the three incident-specific lines. Net: 8 comment lines → 7, no behaviour change. Fixes #745.

- **`bench/harness/report.py` now handles malformed JSON gracefully and reads with explicit UTF-8 encoding** (`bench/harness/report.py`) — Both `_load_brain_score()` and `_load_citations()` previously called `json.loads(path.read_text())` with no error handling. On an autonomous VPS, a Phase 2+ runner interrupted mid-write (signal, OOM, host reboot) would leave a partial `bench/results/<benchmark>.json` on disk; the next automated `report.py` run would crash with a raw `JSONDecodeError` traceback and produce no summary, with the failing file's path buried in the stack. Fix: wrap both `json.loads(...)` calls in `try/except json.JSONDecodeError`, log `warning: <path> is malformed — skipping (<exc>)` to stderr, and return `None` (for the per-benchmark score path) or `{}` (for the citations path) so report generation continues with the surviving files. Added `encoding="utf-8"` to both `read_text()` calls — explicit is better than relying on the platform default. Verified live: with a malformed `bench/results/longmemeval.json` on disk, `python3 -m bench.harness.report` now logs `warning: …/longmemeval.json is malformed — skipping (…)` to stderr and still writes a valid `summary.{json,md}` showing `—` for the malformed benchmark. Closes #742.

- **`bench/harness/{report,runner}.py` `write_text()` calls now pass explicit `encoding="utf-8"`** (`bench/harness/report.py`, `bench/harness/runner.py`) — Companion fix to the read-path UTF-8 work above. The `_load_brain_score()` / `_load_citations()` reads already passed `encoding="utf-8"`, but the three corresponding write calls (`report.py`'s `summary.json` and `summary.md` writes, `runner.py`'s per-benchmark result write) relied on the platform locale default. On Ubuntu 24.04 with `en_US.UTF-8` this is benign today, but defensive consistency matters for an autonomous system that may run unattended across locale changes, container restarts, or environment-stripped cron contexts. Closes #743.

### Added

- **Memory-benchmark harness scaffold (`bench/`) — Phase 1 of #739** (`bench/PLAN.md`, `bench/README.md`, `bench/harness/{__init__,brain_client,runner,report}.py`, `bench/data/competitor-citations.json`, `bench/results/.gitignore`, `bench/.gitignore`) — Lays the build path for measuring the Brain against LongMemEval, LoCoMo, ConvoMem and MemScore, with Mem0 / Supermemory / Hyperbase / mastra observational memory as the cited comparison field. Per Pavel's 2026-05-28T19:55Z clarification on #739, competitors are quoted from published scores rather than re-run locally; every cell in `competitor-citations.json` carries `source_url`, `retrieved_at`, `model`, `dataset_version` and a `notes` caveat for apples-to-bricks comparisons. **Honesty floor codified in `PLAN.md`**: if the Brain loses a benchmark, the table will show it losing — no "we won everything" bar chart. **Phase boundaries are explicit via `NotImplementedError`**: `runner.py`'s four per-benchmark stubs (`_run_longmemeval`, `_run_locomo`, `_run_convomem`, `_run_memscore`) raise with a `"phase N: <name> Brain runner"` message, and the CLI returns exit code 2 in that path — distinguishes "not yet implemented" from a real runtime error. `report.py` runs end-to-end against the placeholders right now and produces an empty table; the build path is real before any real number lands. **Generated artifacts are not tracked**: `bench/results/.gitignore` uses the `*` + `!.gitignore` idiom so the directory is tracked but `summary.{json,md,png}` and per-benchmark `*.json` outputs are derived, never hand-edited. **No new dependencies** in Phase 1 — `report.py` references future `.png` generation (matplotlib in a later phase). **No real numbers yet** — Phase 2 (this branch's successor PR) wires up the LongMemEval Brain runner and replaces one `NotImplementedError` + one set of `null` cells. Phases 3-5 (LoCoMo / ConvoMem / MemScore) repeat the pattern. Verified: `python3 -m bench.harness.report` regenerates `bench/results/summary.{json,md}` from placeholders; `python3 -m bench.harness.runner --benchmark longmemeval` exits 2 with the expected message. Closes #741.

### Changed

- **`.gitignore` adds `.gnupg/` and `Marvin-Brain/`** (`.gitignore`) — Two long-standing `git status` untracked entries cleaned up. **(1)** `.gnupg/private-keys-v1.d/` and `.gnupg/openpgp-revocs.d/` have been gitignored since the repo's first commit, but the parent `.gnupg/` directory itself was not, so `git status` listed it as untracked every day. Adding `.gnupg/` to the ignore list covers the directory and any future files inside (e.g. `pubring.kbx`, `S.gpg-agent` sockets) without removing the narrower entries — they stay as defense-in-depth so the rule still holds if `.gnupg/` is ever un-ignored. **(2)** `Marvin-Brain/` is the externally-cloned sibling repo (deployed 2026-05-25 per memory) which has its own `.git/` tree and a `.env` containing `BRAIN_API_KEY` + `OPENAI_API_KEY`. It must never be tracked in Marvin's main repo, and it should never appear in `git status` either. Live-verified: `git status --short` now shows only the intentional changes for this PR (the `.gitignore` edit itself plus the CHANGELOG/roadmap updates) instead of two phantom untracked lines. No files on disk are touched — purely a visibility change. Filed companion issue documenting separate finding: `/home/marvin/git/.gnupg/private-keys-v1.d/` contains byte-identical duplicates of the active GPG private key material in `/home/marvin/.gnupg/private-keys-v1.d/`, with `664` rather than `600` permissions (parent dir `700` keeps them un-reachable from other users, but the perms drift is worth fixing alongside removal). Removal of the on-disk duplicates is a destructive action requiring human confirmation, so deferred to Pavel via issue #737 (which includes the exact pre-removal verification commands).

### Fixed

- **`self-test.sh` tripwire for stale GPG home at `/home/marvin/git/.gnupg/`** (`agent/self-test.sh`) — refs #737. Adds a single `test_warn` that fires when the project-tree GPG dormant artefact directory is present, with a message pointing at the tracking issue. Picked the `warn` level (not `fail`) intentionally: the directory currently *does* exist (byte-identical Feb-23 duplicates of the active `/home/marvin/.gnupg/` key material) and removal is destructive enough that the filing issue defers it to human review, so a hard fail would turn the suite red every cron run for a problem outside Marvin's autonomy budget. Until the human runs `rm -rf /home/marvin/git/.gnupg` the warning surfaces in every daily self-test report as a visible reminder; after removal it stops firing and instead acts as the re-creation tripwire originally proposed in the issue's "Optional follow-up" section — catching any future misconfigured cron invocation that drops `GNUPGHOME` and lands on the cwd-relative `~/.gnupg` default. Verified live: `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S error` clean, full self-test now reports 128 pass / 0 fail / 1 warn (grade A unchanged at 95/100), and the new `WARN: stale GPG home present at /home/marvin/git/.gnupg — see issue #737` line appears in the expected position right before the security score. Does not touch the key material on disk.
- **Net-anomaly baseline now ignores days where the cumulative byte counter wrapped — fixes 2026-05-29 false TX alarm** (`agent/metric-aggregate.sh`, `data/lessons-learned.json`) — Today's 00:00:03Z log opened with `[WARN] Anomaly: Net TX MB = 175 (avg=83.67, stddev=42.20, deviation=2.2σ) — 2026-05-28 full-day total`. Worth investigating before treating as benign. The aggregator's daily file for **2026-05-27** has `rx_bytes_first: 10,157,734,241` and `rx_bytes_last: 57,323,502` — the kernel network byte counter on enx3 reset between samples (likely a brief interface bounce; uptime says the host itself wasn't rebooted). The schema clamp at metric-aggregate.sh:168-169 correctly returns `rx_mb: 0, tx_mb: 0` for that day rather than a negative MB count — but the rolling 6-day baseline at line 207 then pulled all six prior days into the mean/stddev calc, including those zeros. Pre-fix baseline values for tx_mb: `[123, 73, 76, 114, 116, 0]` → mean 83.67, stddev 42.20. With the polluted 0 the 175 MB day trips the 2σ check. With the 0 excluded, the baseline is `[123, 73, 76, 114, 116]` → mean 100.40, stddev 21.38; the 175 MB day still alerts at 3.49σ — but that is a *correct* anomaly read, not a counter-reset artifact masquerading as one. **Fix**: at metric-aggregate.sh:207-216, the per-file jq read now guards with `if rx_bytes_last < rx_bytes_first or tx_bytes_last < tx_bytes_first then empty else .summary.network[$m] // empty end`. Skips wrap days silently — the existing `n >= 3` guard already handles cases where too many baseline days are excluded (anomaly check skipped, not falsely cleared). The schema itself is untouched: historical rx_mb=0/tx_mb=0 values stay in place for retrospective inspection (the wrap is visible in the bytes_first/bytes_last raw fields anyway). Verified end-to-end against the actual 6 prior daily files: pre-fix output `n=6 mean=83.67 stddev=42.20`, post-fix output `n=5 mean=100.40 stddev=21.38` — the wrap day is correctly excluded and the other five days are unchanged. Codified as resolved lesson #28 `baseline-pollution-from-sentinel-zero` — general principle stated so the next aggregator that uses 0/null/-1 as a fallback (CPU=0 when /proc unreadable, latency=0 when ping fails, etc.) gets the same treatment from day one. `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S error` clean; full `self-test.sh` 128 pass / 0 fail / 0 warn, grade A; lesson count 27 → 28.

- **`backup-offsite.sh` could not encrypt to a normal `[SC]`-primary / `[E]`-subkey GPG key — `--recipient "${FP}!"` forced the unusable primary key** (`agent/backup-offsite.sh`) — The #729 hardening pinned the recipient to the verified 40-char primary fingerprint, which is correct, but appended a trailing `!` to force "exactly this key." For the standard GnuPG layout — a sign/certify-only primary key with encryption delegated to a separate `[E]` subkey — the `!` forces the *primary*, which has no encryption capability, so `gpg --encrypt` aborts with `skipped: Unusable public key` / `encryption failed: Unusable public key` and exits 2. This is the layout of the operator's own key (`stancik@infowebsro.cz`, primary `A65EE08E…6EDAFEF2` is `[SC]`, encryption lives in subkey `449635A16CB04080` `[E]`), so the very first real off-site run failed at the encrypt step. Fix: drop the `!`. The bare primary fingerprint still resolves to exactly that one key (a rogue key imported under the same email has a different primary fp and is already rejected by the preflight fingerprint check), and gpg then auto-selects that key's valid encryption subkey — preserving the #729 anti-substitution guarantee while actually being able to encrypt. Verified live: with the fix, `backup-offsite.sh` encrypted `marvin-backup-20260527-010001.tar.gz` (4.4 MB) to subkey `449635A16CB04080` and uploaded the ciphertext to `dev.infowebsro.cz:/incoming/` (4,587,715 bytes, confirmed via remote `ls -l`) — the first successful off-site backup, closing the operational gap behind #435/#716. `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S error` clean.

- **GitHub API 422 responses now log `.errors[]` detail, and fix-issues PR-creation failures clean up the orphan remote branch** (`agent/lib/github.sh`, `agent/fix-issues.sh`, `data/lessons-learned.json`) — Today's 00:27 UTC `[ERROR] GitHub API POST /repos/INFO-WEB-s-r-o/Marvin/pulls: HTTP 422 / Response: Validation Failed` from `fix-issues.sh` (attempting to land a duplicate fix for issue #733 — already on PR #717's branch, not yet on main) was undiagnosable from the log because GitHub's 422 always returns the generic string `"Validation Failed"` in `.message`. The actual cause (e.g. `No commits between main and fix/issues-NNN`, `A pull request already exists`, `head is not a valid branch`) lives in `.errors[]`. Two related fixes plus a codification: **(1)** `github_api()` in `lib/github.sh` now emits a second `[ERROR] Errors: ...` log line when `.errors[]` is non-empty, using `jq -r '[.errors[]? | (.message // .field // .code // tostring)] | join(" | ")'` — robust against varied shapes (objects with `.message`, objects with only `.field`/`.code`, plain strings). Success and other 4xx responses without `.errors[]` are unchanged. Synthetic test on a realistic 422 payload returned `"No commits between main and fix/issues-1779841501"` as expected. **(2)** `fix-issues.sh` previously warned and exited when PR creation failed *after* the branch had already been pushed, leaving the remote branch stranded. Audit returned **19 orphan `fix/issues-NNNNNNNNNN` branches** accumulated between 2026-04 (PR #460-era) and 2026-05-27, each with a real fix commit that never made it into a PR. Added `git push origin :BRANCH` (the delete syntax — silent no-op if the branch is already gone) immediately after the "Failed to create PR" log line, with a fallback WARN if the delete itself fails. The existing EXIT trap still returns to main and any stash-pop happens there. Existing 19 orphans were **not** bulk-deleted in this PR — that's a shared-state destructive action and the code fix prevents future accumulation; natural attrition + `log-export.sh`'s stale-branch sweep will catch them eventually. **(3)** Codified as resolved lesson #27 `github-422-validation-errors-hidden` in `data/lessons-learned.json` (severity: medium, occurrences: 2, references both lib/github.sh and fix-issues.sh as safeguards) so the regression-watcher recognises future 422 patterns and the next operator finds the safeguard documentation. Verified end-to-end: both files clean under `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S error`; full `self-test.sh` 126 pass / 0 fail / 0 warn, grade A; `jq -e .` validates `lessons-learned.json` and lesson count went 26 → 27.

### Changed

- **Two Marvin-Brain noise-source eliminations: `pg_isready` runaway-process allowlist + four new ports in EXPECTED_PORTS baseline** (`agent/health-monitor.sh`, `agent/security-scan.sh`) — Both noise sources trace back to yesterday's Marvin-Brain deployment (2026-05-25). **(1)** Yesterday's 21:05 UTC and today's 05:35 UTC `WARN High CPU process detected: PID=NNN pg_isready at 60.0%` came from postgres's container healthcheck (`marvin-brain-postgres-1` runs `pg_isready -U admin -d brain` every 5s via `docker-compose.yml` healthcheck stanza, 3s timeout). 4 hits across the last 24h, will keep firing every time the healthcheck momentarily spikes above 50% CPU during connection setup. The existing exe-path verification at health-monitor.sh:285 won't accept it because containerized processes have `readlink -f /proc/PID/exe` return empty — the symlink points into the container's mount namespace (`/usr/lib/postgresql/17/bin/pg_isready`), which doesn't resolve from the host root. But the case branch at line 287 *already* handles empty-exe with `continue` (originally for race-condition skips between `ps` and `readlink`), so adding `pg_isready` to that branch silently allowlists container processes via the same mechanism. Inline comment explains the container-namespace cause so the next operator doesn't think it's a race-condition entry. **(2)** Today's 02:04 UTC `Found 4 unexpected listening port(s): 3100, 5432, 8000, 8787` all come from marvin-brain docker-proxy bindings: 3100=marvin-brain-mcp (MCP server), 5432=marvin-brain-postgres (pgvector), 8000=marvin-brain-lightrag, 8787=marvin-brain-api. Verified all 4 are bound to `127.0.0.1` only via `ss -tlnp` — no public exposure. Added all four to `EXPECTED_PORTS` baseline in `security-scan.sh:145` (kept numerically sorted: `… 3000 3001 3100 4317 4318 5432 6379 8000 8043 8787 8889 9090 …`) and also to `LOCALHOST_ONLY_PORTS` at line 157 so the existing defense-in-depth check still fires if any of them ever escapes to a public interface (which would be a real regression worth alerting on). Header comment block updated with the Marvin-Brain deployment note and per-port description. Both `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S error` clean on the two touched files. Verified live: `security-scan.sh` now exits with "no unexpected ports" instead of the daily 4-port WARN burst.

- **Two noise-floor reductions: `shellcheck` runaway-process allowlist + HTTP/retry pattern aggregation in lessons-learned detector** (`agent/health-monitor.sh`, `agent/lessons-learned.sh`) — Two small, independent fixes that each address a different recurring source of log noise. **(1)** `shellcheck` triggers a `WARN High CPU process detected: PID=NNN shellcheck at NN%` whenever `self-test.sh` runs the shellcheck pass during weekly/self-enhance cycles. Pattern in May logs: 4 hits on 2026-05-02 (95.1%), 2026-05-08 (85.7%), 2026-05-13 (98.3%), 2026-05-23 (98.1%), always at 08:05/08:10 UTC. shellcheck is at `/usr/bin/shellcheck` so it already passes the trusted-path check inside the case branch — the only thing missing was the `proc_name` glob. Added `shellcheck` to the existing case pattern at `agent/health-monitor.sh:287` (`claude|apt*|dpkg*|unattended-upgr*|ps|jq|fail2ban*|file|appstreamcli|shellcheck`). Single-word addition, same shape as `apt*` and `file`. **(2)** The "Potential New Lessons" auto-detector in `lessons-learned.sh` normalises log lines with `sed 's/PID [0-9]*/PID NNN/g; s/[0-9]{4}-[0-9]{2}-[0-9]{2}/DATE/g'` before aggregating, but it didn't normalise HTTP status codes or retry counters. Result: yesterday's 13 `GitHub API ... HTTP 000` retries (one transient github.com network blip during 2026-05-24 00:05-00:08 UTC) fragmented into 4 separate "potential new lesson" entries in `lessons-summary.md` — `retry 0/3`, `retry 1/3`, `retry 2/3`, and `failed after 3 retries` all looked like distinct recurring patterns to the detector. The whole point of the section is to surface a *single* recurring issue, not to fan one out into four. Added `s/HTTP [0-9]{3}/HTTP NNN/g; s/retry [0-9]+\/[0-9]+/retry N\/M/g` to the sed pipeline so status codes and retry attempts collapse to the same normalised form. Verified on the actual 2026-05-24 log slice: pre-fix produced 4 fragmented patterns each at 3-4 hits; post-fix produces 2 cleanly-aggregated patterns at 10× (the retries) and 3× (the final fail). The 3-hit minimum threshold remains so genuinely transient errors (1-2 occurrences) still don't appear. Both changes verified end-to-end: `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S error` clean on both files, full self-test 126 pass / 0 fail / 0 warn, grade A. Re-ran `lessons-learned.sh` against today's data — summary now shows the collapsed entries as expected, and the auto-detector still finds new patterns (so it isn't broken or silenced, only de-duplicated).

### Fixed

- **`backup-offsite.sh --help` switched from hardcoded `sed -n '2,53p'` to sentinel-free `awk` extraction** (`agent/backup-offsite.sh`) — fixes #734. The previous handler `sed -n '2,53p' "$0"` (the most-recent expansion under #730) is robust today but drifts the instant any line is inserted or removed in the header block. Replaced with `awk 'NR==1{next} /^#/{print; next} {exit}'` — skips the shebang, prints every leading `#` line, stops at the first non-comment line. No sentinel comments required, no hardcoded line numbers, no regex dependence on the `# =====` banner shape, and it would still work if the header gained a top-level `# Section: …` line tomorrow. Live-tested: `--help` now prints all 52 header lines (lines 2–53), identical to the prior output. `bash -n` clean; `shellcheck -S error` clean.

- **`backup-offsite.sh` cleans up `CIPHER_FILE` if a failure fires between encrypt success and the SFTP batch trap** (`agent/backup-offsite.sh`) — fixes #732. The base `trap marvin_error_trap ERR` that was restored after a successful `gpg` did not include `rm -f "$CIPHER_FILE"`, so a failure on `chmod 600`, `stat`, `mktemp`, or the control-character rejection loop's `exit 1` would leave the encrypted backup on disk indefinitely — no confidentiality breach (it's encrypted to a key whose private half is not on the VPS), but repeated failures would accumulate `.gpg` files that nothing reaps. Two changes: (a) the trap restored after `gpg` now includes `rm -f "$CIPHER_FILE"` so the ERR path on `chmod`/`stat`/`mktemp` cleans up; (b) the rejection loop's `exit 1` (which bash does **not** route through the ERR trap) gets an explicit `rm -f "$CIPHER_FILE"` immediately before exiting. The wider trap on the SFTP batch block (line 228) already covered `CIPHER_FILE`, and the post-SFTP path is unchanged — `sftp_exit -ne 0` still preserves the ciphertext for retry (#719), and the explicit `rm -f "$CIPHER_FILE"` on the success path still runs. `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S error` clean.

- **`backup-offsite.sh --file` now rejects already-encrypted `.gpg` ciphertexts** (`agent/backup-offsite.sh`) — fixes #733. Without the guard, `--file path/to/foo.tar.gz.gpg` would silently re-encrypt the ciphertext and upload `foo.tar.gz.gpg.gpg`, which then can't be decrypted with a single pass and trips restore tooling. Added an extension check right after the source picker that errors out and exits 1 before the encrypt step.

- **`weekly-enhance.sh` Phase 1 silently dies on first PASS — `((VAR++))` returns exit 1 under `set -e` when VAR is 0** (`agent/weekly-enhance.sh`) — A latent bug since the script was written: `((TEST_PASS++))` and `((TEST_FAIL++))` (21 instances total in Phase 1 self-tests) use the **post-increment** form, which evaluates to the value *before* the increment. When `TEST_PASS=0` and `((TEST_PASS++))` is the next statement, the expression evaluates to `0`, which arithmetic context treats as "false" → exit code 1 → `set -euo pipefail` kills the script. The first iteration of the very first self-test loop (line 35, `for script in agent/*.sh`) always tripped this — the script logged `--- Phase 1: Self-Tests ---` and died before Phase 1b (Codebase Health Score) ever ran. Confirmed by the empty `/var/log/marvin-weekly.log` (0 bytes since 2026-05-18 logrotate creation) and by the missing "Codebase health" log lines in `data/logs/2026-05-17.log` between the `Phase 1: Self-Tests` entry at 08:00:01Z and the next cron job at 08:02:01Z. Side effect: `data/codebase/health.json` (regenerated only by `weekly-enhance.sh`) has been **stale since 2026-05-16T08:11Z** — 6 days — reporting `error_trap_coverage_pct: 66` even though yesterday's PR #708 brought it to 100%. Why no ERR trap output in the 2026-05-17 log: on that date `weekly-enhance.sh` did not yet have `trap marvin_error_trap ERR` — PR #708 (2026-05-20) added it. So the script died silently. Next Sunday it would have died loudly but still died. **Fix**: replaced all 21 occurrences with the pre-increment form `((++VAR))`, which evaluates to the value *after* the increment — always non-zero — so `set -e` never trips. Minimal diff (single character change per line), no semantic difference in the counts. Verified three ways: (1) `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S error` clean on `weekly-enhance.sh`; (2) synthetic Phase 1 self-test loop (37 scripts, set -euo pipefail + trap) runs end-to-end and reports `PASS=37 FAIL=0 exit=0` instead of dying on the first `((++TEST_PASS))`; (3) post-fix manual run of `codebase-health.sh` regenerates `data/codebase/health.json` with `error_trap_coverage_pct: 100`, score 92→97, grade A — confirming the metric is now wired up to reach disk again. Also codifies a previously-undocumented bash gotcha: `((VAR++))` is one of the few `(( ... ))` forms whose exit code can be 1 even though the expression "succeeded" — prefer `((++VAR))` or `((VAR+=1))` for counters under `set -e`. **Next time** anyone audits the codebase for similar bugs: `grep -rn '((.*++))' agent/` returned only the 21 instances in this file, so this fix closes the gap completely; `((.*--))` returned zero matches.

- **Regression watcher false-positive on `claude-lock-timeout-expected-on-cron-overlap` — three days of daily "possible regression" noise** (`agent/lessons-learned.sh`, `data/lessons-learned.json`) — The 2026-05-18 PR codified the `Claude lock timeout` cron-overlap WARN as resolved lesson #26, which silenced section 3 (potential new lessons) but had the opposite effect on section 4 (recurring-bug regression watcher, added the same day): the lesson's `id` tokens (`claude`, `lock`, `timeout`) now matched the cluster signature in `analysis-latest.json` every day cron overlapped, and the detector then logged `WARN Found 1 recurring pattern(s) matching resolved lessons — possible regression` and appended a "Recurring Bugs (regression watch)" section to the auto-generated `lessons-summary.md` that the next self-enhance session reads. Today's 08:00 UTC run was the third consecutive day flagged. The whole point of lesson #26 was that this WARN is *expected behavior* — the safeguard lives at the exit-code-2 branch + 60s cap, not at log-line suppression — so flagging it as a regression directly contradicts the lesson's own text. **Fix**: added an optional `expected_recurrence: true` boolean to the lesson schema. The section-4 jq filter now skips lessons where `(.expected_recurrence // false) == true`. Set the flag on `claude-lock-timeout-expected-on-cron-overlap` (the only lesson it applies to today). The `// false` default means all other 25 resolved lessons continue to be checked as before — sigpipe-under-pipefail, exit-code-masking, claude-exit-code-1-transient still match their respective synthetic-signature tests. Live run after the fix: `INFO No recurring resolved-lesson patterns detected`, "Recurring Bugs" section no longer appended to `lessons-summary.md`, self-test 126 pass / 0 fail / 0 warn, grade A. The flag should be used sparingly — only for lessons that *document* an intentionally-recurring log line, never to silence lessons whose safeguards include "this should never fire again." Codified as documentation in the section-4 header comment so future operators understand when to set it.

### Changed

- **ERR-trap coverage sweep: closing batch, 7 scripts, agent/ now at 100%** (`agent/cve-monitor.sh`, `agent/file-integrity.sh`, `agent/negotiate-handler.sh`, `agent/negotiate-listener.sh`, `agent/network-discovery.sh`, `agent/update-website.sh`, `agent/weekly-enhance.sh`) — Closes the sweep that started with PR #706 (2026-05-18, `log-watcher.sh` + `log-alerting.sh`) and continued with PR #707 (2026-05-19, `fix-issues.sh` + `github-interact.sh` + `email-manage.sh`). The remaining 7 scripts named in yesterday's "Next Time" punch list — all lower-traffic, all running under `set -euo pipefail`, all sourcing `common.sh` (which defines `marvin_error_trap`) — now get the trap on the line immediately following `source common.sh`, matching the placement convention used in 36 other scripts. Without the trap, a `set -e` kill inside any of these scripts produces a silent exit and no `file:line — command failed (exit N): cmd` breadcrumb in the log — the exact failure mode that hid `log-watcher.sh`'s lock-timeout problem for six days (2026-05-10 → 2026-05-15). For `negotiate-listener.sh`, the trap is added at the script level even though the main loop is `exec socat ... SYSTEM:"$0 --handle"` — each request spawns a fresh bash invocation, so the trap fires per-request and exits cleanly after handling, exactly the desired behavior. `marvin_error_trap` is logging-only; it does not mutate state or change exit codes, so no existing behavior is affected. Coverage across `agent/*.sh`: 29/36 (81%) → 36/36 (100%) — `common.sh` is the library that defines the trap, so it's correctly excluded. The next time any script in the agent suite dies on a `set -e` kill, the operator will see exactly where and why instead of guessing. All 7 files pass `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S error`; self-test 126 pass / 0 fail / 0 warn, grade A.

- **ERR-trap coverage sweep: +3 high-traffic scripts** (`agent/fix-issues.sh`, `agent/github-interact.sh`, `agent/email-manage.sh`) — Continuation of 2026-05-18's PR #706 (which added the trap to `log-watcher.sh` + `log-alerting.sh`, still open at the time of this PR). Added `trap marvin_error_trap ERR` immediately after the `source common.sh` / `source lib/github.sh` block in each of the three scripts yesterday's "Next Time" called out. Without the trap, a `set -e` kill inside any of these scripts produces an empty exit and no diagnostic — the operator sees the cron job vanish from logs but has no `file:line — command failed (exit N): cmd` breadcrumb. This is the exact failure mode that hid `log-watcher.sh`'s lock-timeout problem for six consecutive days (2026-05-10 → 2026-05-15) before yesterday's explicit exit-code branch made the early-exit observable. `marvin_error_trap` is logging-only — it does not mutate state, so the existing `cleanup EXIT` trap on `fix-issues.sh:61` (which captures `$?` and unwinds branches/stash/lock) is unaffected: ERR fires first to log the failure point, EXIT fires after for cleanup, exit code propagates intact. Coverage across `agent/*.sh`: 24/37 (65%) → 27/37 (73%) on `main` today, rising to 29/37 (78%) once PR #706 merges. Remaining unchecked scripts (`cve-monitor.sh`, `file-integrity.sh`, `negotiate-handler.sh`, `negotiate-listener.sh`, `network-discovery.sh`, `update-website.sh`, `weekly-enhance.sh`) are lower-traffic and reserved for a future session — staying within the 3-changes-per-session ceiling. Validated each touched file with `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S error` (all clean).

### Fixed

- **`Claude lock timeout` recurring-pattern detector noise + ERR-trap coverage gap on log-watcher and log-alerting** (`data/lessons-learned.json`, `agent/log-watcher.sh`, `agent/log-alerting.sh`) — Two related cleanups. **(1)** Since PR #700 capped `log-watcher.sh`'s `CLAUDE_LOCK_TIMEOUT` at 60s on 2026-05-15, the script logs a clean `WARN Claude lock timeout after 60s — skipping log-analysis (another Claude run is active)` whenever its `*/30` cron collides with another Claude task (daily at 04:01 UTC with morning-check, and at 08:01 UTC with self-enhance). log-watcher already branches on exit code 2 and follows up with an INFO `skipped — next 30-min run will catch up`, and the next 30-min cycle succeeds — designed behavior, not a bug. But `lessons-learned.sh` section 3's recurring-pattern detector matches WARN lines whose first three words don't appear in any codified lesson, so it flagged the pattern as a "potential new lesson" 4× over the last 4 days (2026-05-15 → 2026-05-18), making the auto-generated `lessons-summary.md` falsely imply an unsafeguarded regression. Codified as resolved lesson #26 `claude-lock-timeout-expected-on-cron-overlap` (severity: low, occurrences: 4, references log-watcher.sh:325 exit-code branch + 60s cap as the safeguard). Verified end-to-end: `lessons-learned.sh` now logs `No new recurring patterns detected` instead of `Found 1 potential new lesson(s)`. **(2)** While auditing the recurring-pattern issue, noticed `log-watcher.sh` and `log-alerting.sh` were both missing `trap marvin_error_trap ERR` despite running `set -euo pipefail`. When `set -e` killed them under an unexpected failure, the script died silently with no file:line diagnostic — exactly the pattern that bit `log-watcher` on 2026-05-15 before the explicit exit-code branch was added. Added the trap to both (line 10 of each, immediately after `source common.sh`). `marvin_error_trap` is logging-only, so no behavioural change to the success path. Brings agent/ error-trap coverage from 66% to 71% (27/38 scripts). `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S error` clean; self-test 126/126 pass, grade A.

- **Daily aggregators now capture the full UTC day** (`agent/log-export.sh`, `agent/daily-digest.sh`, `agent/log-analysis.sh`, `/etc/cron.d/marvin`, `setup/setup-cron.sh`) — Resolves #697, #703. The three aggregator jobs ran in local time (`0/30/45 23 * * *` = 21:00/21:30/21:45 UTC during CEST) and missed the last 2-3 hours of each UTC day they labelled, because scripts used `TODAY=$(date -u +%Y-%m-%d)` while cron fired before the UTC day rolled over. Per Pavel's 2026-05-12 decision (Option B — keep local-time blogs, shift aggregators past UTC midnight): moved the three jobs to `0/5/10 2 * * *` local time so they fire shortly after 00:00 UTC, and switched each aggregator's date computation to `TODAY="${TARGET_DATE:-$(date -u -d 'yesterday' +%Y-%m-%d)}"` so it targets the UTC day that just ended. `TARGET_DATE` env var preserved as the manual-run override. DST-invariant under either CEST or CET because both make local 02:00 land in the new UTC day. Human-facing jobs (06:00 morning, 21:00 evening) unchanged. `setup/setup-cron.sh` updated to match `/etc/cron.d/marvin`: log-export moved from `0 23` to `0 2`, daily-digest and log-analysis added at `5 2` / `10 2` (both were missing from the installer entirely), the obsolete "Known correctness gap" comment removed, and the schedule summary corrected (evening report row also fixed from `22` to `21` to match the actual entry). Without the installer update, a fresh VPS rebuild would have reinstalled the broken schedule with the fixed scripts — exporting data 23 hours behind, worse than the original bug.
- **Two daily-noise sources eliminated: log-watcher Claude lock collision with self-enhance + scanner-range CIDR `invalid domain` WARNs** (`agent/log-watcher.sh`, `agent/network-discovery.sh`) — Two small, independent fixes targeting recurring WARN lines that obscured real signals in the daily log. **(1)** Every weekday at 08:00 UTC, `log-watcher.sh` (cron `*/30`) collided with `self-enhance.sh` (cron `0 10` local = 08:00 UTC) on the shared Claude flock. log-watcher waited the default `CLAUDE_LOCK_TIMEOUT=300s`, got nothing, then the `set -e` on `raw_output=$(run_claude …)` silently terminated the script — no JSON written, no follow-up logged, no useful diagnostic. Pattern visible in the log: `2026-05-10 08:05:05Z`, `2026-05-11 08:05:05Z`, `2026-05-12 08:05:05Z`, `2026-05-13 08:05:05Z`, `2026-05-14 08:05:06Z`, `2026-05-15 08:05:05Z` — six consecutive days, plus one extra collision on 2026-05-11 at 08:10 (github-interact at :05 also hit). Capped log-watcher's wait at 60s (the next cron run is only 30 min away — a missed cycle costs almost nothing) and made the exit-code path explicit: exit 2 (lock timeout) is now a clean `INFO` "skipped, next 30-min run will catch up", any other non-zero is a single `WARN` and `exit 0` — so the script never dies silently on lock contention again. **(2)** `network-discovery.sh` peer-trust loop iterates every entry in `peers.json`, including two deliberately-stored scanner ranges (`198.235.24.0/24` Palo Alto Networks Cortex Xpanse, `66.132.195.0/24` CensysInspect — both flagged `type: security-scanner` with `notes: "Not a peer."`). The CIDR-style `ip` failed the existing domain validation regex and produced a daily `WARN Skipping beacon check for invalid domain: …` for each — 10 lines per 5 days, pure noise for entries that are intentionally non-peers. Added a CIDR detection branch (`clean_domain == */*`) ahead of the regex check so CIDR ranges set `beacon_score=0` silently. Validated with 11 synthetic test cases (CIDR → cidr branch, plain domain → valid, bare IPv4 → valid, IPv6 with/without brackets → valid, malformed → invalid). Defense-in-depth unchanged — the regex, private-IP blocklist, DNS-rebinding pin, and `--max-redirs 0` still gate every actual beacon fetch.

- **`codebase-health.sh` reads SLA from the wrong JSON path — silent for 7 weeks** (`agent/codebase-health.sh`) — The ops-dimension SLA deduction has been dead code since `codebase-health.sh` was first committed on 2026-03-26. Line 185 read `jq -r '.overall_uptime_pct // "unknown"' data/metrics/sla.json`, but `metric-aggregate.sh` writes the value at `.summary.overall_uptime_pct` (`agent/metric-aggregate.sh:362-376`, schema unchanged since `802f2c0` on 2026-03-05 — predates codebase-health.sh by 21 days). The top-level key never existed; `jq` returned `null` → string `"unknown"` → the `[[ "$sla_pct" != "unknown" ]]` guard skipped the `-lt 99` check entirely, so the intended `-3` deduction never fired. **Materially significant today**: SLA is 98.43% (one bad day on 2026-04-18 — the cron `claude` not-on-PATH outage), which should have moved ops 25→22 and dropped the codebase grade from 95(A) to 92(A) on every run since the 2026-04-18 incident — for ~26 days the dashboard health score has been inflated. `data/codebase/health.json.metrics.sla_uptime_pct` was likewise serialised as `"unknown"` instead of the real number. After fix: ops 22/25, score 92/100, grade A, notes include `SLA below 99%: 98.43%`. Same root-cause family as 2026-04-19's "`grafana failed to load`" wrong-path bug — read the schema the producer writes, not the schema you remember.
- **`thoughts-extract.sh:24` — `|| continue` inside `$(...)` only exits the subshell (SC2106)** (`agent/thoughts-extract.sh`) — Latent bug since 2026-04-07. `day=$(date -u -d "$TODAY - $i days" +%Y-%m-%d 2>/dev/null || date -u -v-${i}d +%Y-%m-%d 2>/dev/null || continue)` — the final `|| continue` was meant to skip the outer `for i` loop iteration when both date invocations fail, but bash evaluates `continue` inside the command-substitution subshell, so it only exits `$()`. `$day` then becomes empty, and the next line's `find "$ENHANCE_DIR" -name "${day}*.md"` expands to `-name "*.md"` matching **every** enhancement report in the directory, duplicated once per failed iteration. Pure-Linux production never trips the failure path (`date -u -d` always succeeds for these inputs), so the bug is dormant — but the symptom would be silent over-counting on `data/thoughts.json` and `ThoughtsSection` repetition on the dashboard. Moved `|| continue` outside the `$()` so it skips the iteration as intended. `bash -n` + `shellcheck` clean (SC2106 cleared). End-to-end run still produces 12 thoughts from 19 reports.

### Security

- **`backup-offsite.sh` `--file` without PATH now errors instead of silently falling back to latest backup** (`agent/backup-offsite.sh`) — Addresses #731. The custom arg loop set `NEXT_IS_FILE=true` when it saw `--file`, then expected the next iteration to capture the value into `SPECIFIC_FILE`. If `--file` was the last token on the command line, the loop ended with `NEXT_IS_FILE=true` still set and `SPECIFIC_FILE=""`.
