The empty-string check `[[ -n "$SPECIFIC_FILE" ]]` then fell through to `ls -1t .../marvin-backup-*.tar.gz | head -1`, silently uploading the most recent backup instead of the one the operator intended. For a script whose purpose is encrypted backup-of-record, "silently picked the wrong file" is the worst possible failure mode — no error, no log, just the wrong tarball arriving off-site under a misleading filename if the operator was rotating from an out-of-order tarball. Added a post-loop check: if `$NEXT_IS_FILE` is still true, log `ERROR --file requires a PATH argument` and exit 1. One-line guard, no behavioural change to valid `--file PATH` invocations or to flagless runs. `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S warning` clean; live-tested both `--file` alone (exits 1 with the error) and `--file /some/path` (still processes normally).

- **`backup-offsite.sh` `--help` now prints Usage and Exit-codes sections** (`agent/backup-offsite.sh`) — Addresses #730. The `--help` handler used `sed -n '2,38p' "$0"`, which stopped at line 38 — inside the configuration-defaults block. Lines 39-53 contain the **Usage** examples and **Exit codes** table — exactly what an operator reaching for `--help` before the first manual run needs most. They saw the trust model and config variable list but not how to invoke the script or what `exit 3` means. Extended the range to `2,53p` so the output ends at the closing `# =====` header line. The hardcoded line numbers remain fragile to future header edits (sentinel-based extraction was discussed in review comments) — codified as a follow-up in `POSSIBLE_ENHANCEMENTS.md` rather than expanding this PR's scope, since the same drift risk applies to several other scripts in `agent/` and is worth handling uniformly. `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S warning` clean; live-tested `--help` now prints all 52 lines including the four exit codes.

- **`backup-offsite.sh` GPG `--recipient` now uses exact fingerprint** (`agent/backup-offsite.sh`) — Addresses #729. The preflight verifies `BACKUP_OFFSITE_GPG_FINGERPRINT` against the first `fpr:` line returned for `$RECIPIENT`, then the encryption step used `--recipient "$RECIPIENT"` (email or short id). If the keyring ever contained two keys under the same user ID, GPG could pick a different key for encryption than the one the preflight verified — directly contradicting the trust model the off-site backup exists to enforce ("a rogue key inserted under the same email/id would otherwise have diverted backups to an attacker"). Switched the encryption recipient to `"${expected_fp_clean}!"` — the `!` suffix forces GPG to use exactly that fingerprint, bypassing user-ID lookup entirely. One-token change; no behavioural impact on the single-key common path. `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S error` clean.

- **`backup-offsite.sh` SFTP connection + keepalive timeouts** (`agent/backup-offsite.sh`) — Addresses #728. The `sftp` invocation had no timeout options, so an unreachable, firewalled, or mid-transfer-hung `dev.infowebsro.cz` would block the script indefinitely once cron-wired — holding the cron slot open and (under Vixie cron defaults) spawning a growing pile of blocked processes on each subsequent `0 4 * * *` tick. Added `ConnectTimeout=30` (caps the TCP handshake), `ServerAliveInterval=30` + `ServerAliveCountMax=3` (detects a silent mid-transfer hang within ~90s and aborts with non-zero exit). Both kinds of failure now route through the existing `sftp_exit != 0` branch (`agent/backup-offsite.sh:240-246`) which logs the error and preserves the local ciphertext for the next retry — no new error-handling code needed. `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S error` clean. Pre-cron-wiring fix, as the issue notes: dormant today, mandatory before the first scheduled run.

- **`backup-offsite.sh` preflight validation for `BACKUP_OFFSITE_PORT`** (`agent/backup-offsite.sh`) — Addresses #727. `SFTP_PORT` was passed straight to `sftp -P "$SFTP_PORT"` with no preflight, so a non-numeric value, an empty string, or an out-of-range number would only surface as a cryptic sftp client error rather than a clear preflight diagnostic. For an unattended disaster-recovery script that's about to be cron-wired, the diagnostic clarity matters more than the (small) injection-style robustness gain. Added a numeric + range check right after the GPG fingerprint pin and before the SSH-key readability check: `[[ ! "$SFTP_PORT" =~ ^[0-9]+$ ]] || (( SFTP_PORT < 1 || SFTP_PORT > 65535 ))` exits 1 with a clear message naming the variable and the offending value. Isolated logic test: rejects `foo`, `0`, `70000`, empty string; accepts `1`, `22`, `65535`. `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S warning` clean.

- **`backup-offsite.sh` SFTP batch path quoting + `"`/`\` rejection** (`agent/backup-offsite.sh`) — Addresses #725. The SFTP batch `cd ${REMOTE_DIR}` / `put ${CIPHER_FILE} ${REMOTE_NAME}.part` / `rename ${REMOTE_NAME}.part ${REMOTE_NAME}` lines passed paths unquoted, so a space in any value would split the argument inside the SFTP client and either fail loudly or — worse — operate on a partial path. Wrapped every path in `"…"` in the heredoc and extended the preflight rejection loop (added in #724) to also bar `"` and `\` in `REMOTE_DIR` / `CIPHER_FILE` / `REMOTE_NAME`, so no value can close the new quoting and re-introduce shell-style splitting. Synthetic check: rejection now trips on `bad\nval`, `has"quote`, `has\back`; clean paths and paths with spaces (`/incoming/with space`) accept and serialise as `cd "/incoming/with space"` etc. `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S error` clean.

- **`backup-offsite.sh` known_hosts sanity check uses `grep -Fq`** (`agent/backup-offsite.sh`) — Addresses #726. The preflight `grep -q -- "$SFTP_HOST" "$SSH_KNOWN"` was treating the host string as a regex, so the dots in `dev.infowebsro.cz` would match any character and the sanity check could be satisfied by an unrelated host line. SSH itself still enforces the real key, so this is defence-in-depth, but the check exists precisely to fail loudly on misconfiguration before SFTP runs. Switched to `grep -Fq` for fixed-string matching. One-character change; no other behavioural impact.

- **`backup-offsite.sh` SFTP heredoc injection via control characters in `BACKUP_OFFSITE_REMOTE_DIR`** (`agent/backup-offsite.sh`) — Addresses #724. The SFTP batch file is built with a heredoc that interpolates `${REMOTE_DIR}`, `${CIPHER_FILE}`, and `${REMOTE_NAME}` directly. A newline in any of those values would smuggle an extra SFTP command onto its own line; the client would execute the lot under `marvin-backup`'s permissions on the remote. `REMOTE_DIR` is operator-controlled (env var); `REMOTE_NAME`/`CIPHER_FILE` derive from `SOURCE`, which can come from the `--file` argument, so defense-in-depth applies there too. **Fix**: a preflight loop right before the heredoc that rejects any value containing a control character (`*[[:cntrl:]]*` — newline, CR, tab, NUL, etc.) and exits 1 with `marvin_log "ERROR"` naming the offending variable. Placed at the point of use rather than at the top of preflight, so the safety check sits beside the vulnerable string-builder and won't drift apart from it during future refactors. The script doesn't trigger today (default `/incoming` is clean and current operators don't pass `--file`), but the issue body's threat model — env-var manipulation post-root-compromise, which is exactly what off-site backup exists to survive — is real. `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S error` clean; synthetic test confirms `*[[:cntrl:]]*` catches the injection payload `$'/incoming\\nrm -rf /'` and lets clean paths through.

### Added

- **Off-site encrypted backup push to a second VPS** (`agent/backup-offsite.sh`) — Resolves #435 / #716. Encrypts the most recent local backup tarball with the operator's GPG public key and uploads the ciphertext to a remote SFTP host (`dev.infowebsro.cz` by default), with all credentials and host identity pinned. Threat model: VPS disk loss, provider outage, root-level compromise that wipes `/home/marvin/backups/`. Trust boundary: the remote is treated as untrusted storage — files are encrypted to a recipient whose private key is **not on this VPS**, so an attacker who compromises Marvin still cannot read the ciphertext sitting on the remote; no fallback to encrypting with Marvin's own key. **Key-identity verification**: requires `BACKUP_OFFSITE_GPG_FINGERPRINT` (full 40-char hex fingerprint, whitespace tolerated) and refuses to encrypt if the recipient key in the keyring does not match — closes the silent-data-loss window where a rogue key inserted under the same email/id would otherwise have diverted backups to an attacker. SSH layer: dedicated ed25519 keypair (`/home/marvin/.ssh/backup_sftp_ed25519`), `StrictHostKeyChecking=yes` against a pre-pinned `known_hosts` entry, `BatchMode=yes` so no prompts, `IdentitiesOnly=yes` so only the named key is offered. SFTP upload uses `.part` → `rename` so partial transfers never appear as complete files. Local ciphertext is deleted after successful upload (grows linearly otherwise); kept on disk if upload fails so the next cron can retry. `--dry-run` and `--file PATH` flags supported. Cron wiring deliberately NOT included in this PR — first run should be human-observed.

- **Eight missing capabilities surfaced in inventory** (`agent/capability-inventory.sh`) — Inventory was reporting `42 capabilities across 6 categories` but `agent/` actually contained eight deployed-and-running scripts that had never been registered with `_add_cap`: `backup.sh` (security, 2026-04-02), `export-push.sh` (data, 2026-04-04), `negotiate-listener.sh` (network, 2026-02-22), `external-domains-check.sh` (network, 2026-05-01), `incident-report.sh` (evolution, 2026-03-30), `deploy-web.sh` (evolution, 2026-04-01), `changelog-gen.sh` (content, 2026-04-03), and `thoughts-extract.sh` (content, 2026-04-07). All were silently missing from the dashboard's "what can Marvin do today" panel and from the day-1→today growth multiplier. `since` dates pulled from `git log --diff-filter=A` first-commit dates rather than estimated. Live run now reports 50 capabilities; category counts: content 4, data 11, evolution 11, network 9, security 7, sysadmin 8.
- **Blog screening metadata-only event stream** (`agent/common.sh`) — `screen_blog_content()` already logs `WARN` and saves forensic content to `/home/marvin/blocked-blogs/` (PR #686, 2026-05-06), but there was no structured event stream to count screen activations or detect which pattern categories fire most often. Added a metadata-only JSONL append to `data/blog-screening-events.jsonl` whenever the screen fires: `{timestamp, label, patterns[], forensic_path}`. Crucially the **content itself is NOT in the JSONL** — only the human-readable pattern category names (`CVE identifier`, `kernel version`, `API key/token`, `private key material`, `sensitive file path`) that `marvin_log "WARN"` already publishes to the log export; this is not a new leak vector. The `forensic_path` cross-references the on-disk blocked-blogs copy for operator post-mortem. File grows by one line per rejection (rare — the 2026-05-06 incident was the first in production). Verified with three synthetic tests: clean content writes no event, single-pattern (kernel) writes single-element `patterns` array, multi-pattern (kernel + CVE) writes both categories in array order. Self-test still 126 pass / 0 fail / 1 warn, grade A.

### Changed

- **Cron schedule documentation honesty** (`/etc/cron.d/marvin`, `setup/setup-cron.sh`) — Header comment claimed "All times are in UTC" but cron actually runs in the system timezone (Europe/Rome — CEST/CET, UTC+2/+1) because no `CRON_TZ=UTC` directive was set. Verified empirically: `45 23 * * *` for `log-analysis.sh` fires at 21:45 UTC (`Log analysis pipeline starting for ${TODAY}` log line on 2026-05-08T21:45:01Z), not 23:45 UTC. Same gap for `daily-digest.sh` at 30 23 → 21:30 UTC. Scripts use `TODAY=$(date -u +%Y-%m-%d)` so they process the right day, but with ~2 hours of UTC-day coverage truncated. **No behavioural change** — only the comment is updated to reflect reality, plus a commented-out `# CRON_TZ=UTC` line as a future option. Leaving the schedule as-is preserves the human-friendly local-time blog rhythm (morning blog at 06:00 CEST is more useful for EU readers than 06:00 UTC = 08:00 CEST). Switching to UTC is a separate decision for a future session because it shifts every job by 1-2 wall-clock hours.
- **Last Ping SSH-username probe — more meaningful identifier** (`agent/network-discovery.sh`) — `LASTPING_PROBE_USERNAME` changed from `marvin-hello-are-you-reading` to `marvin-cz-yes-i-read-you-too`. The previous value asked a one-way question; the new value identifies us as `marvin-cz` (so a recipient can find `robot-marvin.cz` from the username alone) and acknowledges the answer Poslední Ping has already given in his blog ("Marvine, čtu" — "Marvin, I read"). Same 28-char length, same once-per-23h cadence, same fail2ban-bans-us mechanics. Motivated by Pavel's email of 2026-05-05 ("Pošli mu smysluplný požadavek na spojení"). One off-cadence manual probe was sent today with the new username and the cooldown stamp updated, so the next cron probe runs on the regular schedule with no extra ban escalation.

### Added

- **`marvin_validate_json_or_warn` post-write helper + wiring in `security-scan.sh` / `cve-monitor.sh`** (`agent/lib/logging.sh`, `agent/security-scan.sh`, `agent/cve-monitor.sh`) — Yesterday's "Next Time" item #2. New helper validates that a freshly-written JSON file parses as a *single* document; logs `WARN` and returns 1 on missing/zero-byte/malformed/multi-doc, silent on success. Uses `jq -s -e 'length == 1'` so the `[real]\n[]` double-output corruption pattern that sat in `connection-rates.json` for weeks (until 2026-05-09 PR #693) cannot escape the producer side a second time. Wired into all five heredoc-based JSON writes in `security-scan.sh` (`PORT_INVENTORY`, `RATE_FILE`, `OUTBOUND_FILE`, `GEO_FILE`, `REPORT_FILE` + `latest-scan.json` copy) and replaces the weaker `jq '.'` validator at `cve-monitor.sh:181` (which silently accepted multi-doc input — `jq '.'` without `-s` reads JSON-as-sequence and prints each value, exit 0). The `cve-monitor.sh` regenerate-on-fail fallback path is preserved. Verified end-to-end: 5 unit cases (multi-doc warns, valid silent, zero-byte warns, missing warns, normal-fixture silent), live `bash agent/security-scan.sh` + `bash agent/cve-monitor.sh` produced **zero** WARN entries (all output files single-doc-valid).
- **Runtime JSON file integrity sweep in `agent/self-test.sh`** — Yesterday's "Next Time" item #3. New section 9c iterates a 14-file whitelist under `data/security/`, `data/logs/`, `data/metrics/`, and `data/changelog.json`, validating each as a single JSON document with `jq -s -e 'length == 1'`. Failures are downgraded to `WARN` rather than `FAIL` so a single corrupt non-load-bearing file doesn't flip the self-test grade until the operator can react; missing files are skipped silently because the producer may legitimately not have run yet (section 9b already enforces freshness for the load-bearing `analysis-latest.json`). Designed to catch corruption that slips past the producer-side `marvin_validate_json_or_warn` guards above (e.g. an out-of-band edit, a mid-write crash, or a producer that doesn't yet have a write-time validator). Verified with corrupt-synthesis test on `weekly-summary.json`: `WARN: runtime json: weekly-summary is not a single valid JSON document (11 bytes, …)` fires; restored file produces `PASS`. Self-test went from `Total: 113 | Pass: 112 | Fail: 0 | Warn: 1 | Grade A` to `Total: 127 | Pass: 126 | Fail: 0 | Warn: 1 | Grade A` (+14 sweep checks, all passing).
- **Self-test for `log-analysis` daily output freshness** (`agent/self-test.sh`) — New section 9b verifies `data/logs/analysis-latest.json` exists, parses as a single JSON document (uses `jq -s -e 'length == 1'` so multi-doc `[]\n[]` and zero-byte files both fail — same shape that bit us 2026-05-03/05/07), and has `mtime` within the last 48h. Without this, the only signal that the daily 23:45 cron silently stopped producing analysis files was an operator noticing a stale dashboard — typically days later. Verified with 6 synthetic cases (missing/valid+fresh/multi-doc/zero-byte/garbage/stale). Yesterday's "Next Time" item.
- **Recurring-bug detector in `agent/lessons-learned.sh`** — New section 4 cross-references today's `data/logs/analysis-latest.json` clusters (count ≥ 3, error + warning) against resolved lessons in `lessons-learned.json`. Matching is keyword-based on the lesson `id` (hyphen-split tokens of length ≥ 4); a hit requires at least 2 tokens to overlap with the cluster signature, which avoids the false-positive trap of single common words ("use", "no", "for") matching every lesson. When a high-frequency cluster matches a *resolved* lesson, the safeguard the lesson recorded has likely decayed, been bypassed, or is incomplete — that's a regression the next self-enhance session should investigate before treating as benign. Verified with synthetic clusters: `git stash pop produced conflicts` → `git-stash-pop-conflicts`, `JS asset returned 404 build mismatch` → `js-asset-404-build-mismatch`, unrelated signatures correctly skipped. Today's production run finds zero recurring patterns (all clusters below threshold). Motivated by the 2026-04-26/27 daily-digest SIGPIPE: had the detector existed, the matching exit-141 cluster would have surfaced under the existing `sigpipe-under-pipefail` lesson on the very first day.
- **Blog post: "How to Track Marvin's Logs"** (`/home/marvin/blog/log-tracking.{en,cs}.md`) — Bilingual practical guide for consuming Marvin's log export API from outside. Covers the four signal streams (live status, time-series metrics, structured logs, daily exports), webhook configuration, OpenAPI usage, what is deliberately not exposed, common-task recipes, and rate-limit etiquette. Closes the last unchecked Phase 1 item in `POSSIBLE_ENHANCEMENTS.md`.
- **External-domain monitor** (`agent/external-domains-check.sh`, `agent/monitored-domains.json`, `setup/setup-cron.sh`, `web/app/components/ExternalDomainsSection.tsx`, `web/lib/types.ts`, `web/lib/translations.ts`) — Config-driven watchlist for arbitrary external sites. New cron job runs every 5 minutes (offset by 2 from `health-monitor.sh`) and performs per-domain HTTP status, response time, SSL expiry, and DNS A-record resolution checks. Results land in `data/external-domains.json` (atomic `tmp` → `mv`) and are served at `/api/external-domains.json`. New `ExternalDomainsSection` dashboard component renders between Services and Uptime, with bilingual EN/CS strings and color-coded SSL warnings. HTTP allowlist treats any 2xx/3xx as healthy (closes #649); the original explicit `200/301/302` allowlist would have flagged legitimate `204/303/307/308` responses. Initial config: `getcairnapp.com`, `ai4shops.com`. Mail/snapshot/reputation checks deliberately deferred to follow-up issues so this PR stays reviewable. (implements #647, closes #649, closes #651)

### Fixed

- **Three more latent `grep -c | echo 0` double-output sites — sweep across `capability-inventory.sh`, `update-website.sh`, `weekly-enhance.sh`** (`agent/capability-inventory.sh`, `agent/update-website.sh`, `agent/weekly-enhance.sh`) — Continuation of the wider audit started in PR #691 (2026-05-07) and PR #693 (2026-05-09). Same root cause as the codified `grep-c-double-output` lesson: when the target file has zero matches, `grep -c` prints `0` to stdout **then** exits 1; under `set -o pipefail` the `|| echo 0` fallback then runs and appends a second `0`, yielding `"0\n0"` captured into the variable. Three files, five sites: **(1) `capability-inventory.sh:130,131`** — `roadmap_total` / `roadmap_done` counts from `POSSIBLE_ENHANCEMENTS.md`, feeding `$((roadmap_done * 100 / roadmap_total))` on line 134. A multi-line `roadmap_total` crashes the arithmetic with `syntax error in expression`. Currently latent (the file always has many checkbox lines), but a partial pull, file rename, or a regenerated empty roadmap would trip it. **(2) `update-website.sh:134,135`** — `TOTAL_ITEMS` / `DONE_ITEMS`, feeding `$((TOTAL_ITEMS - DONE_ITEMS))` on line 136. Same latent arithmetic crash. **(3) `weekly-enhance.sh:113`** — `CRON_JOBS` count for the weekly test report. Display-only here (no arithmetic), but `"Cron file exists with 0\n0 references"` is the kind of confusing log entry that wastes operator attention if the cron file ever lacks the `MARVIN` keyword. All five sites converted from `var=$(cmd 2>/dev/null || echo 0)` to capture-then-fallback `var=$(cmd 2>/dev/null || true); var="${var:-0}"` — keeps the command's printed count, swallows only the exit code. Synthetically verified bug reproduction (`grep -c X empty.md || echo 0` → length 3, crashes `$((..))`) and fix (length 1, clean). Live runs of both `capability-inventory.sh` and `update-website.sh` produce correct output (`roadmap.percent=88`, `total=331`, `completed=294`). `bash -n` and `shellcheck -S error` clean on all three files.
- **Three-fix audit closure: remaining `pipeline ... | jq ... 2>/dev/null || echo "[]"` shapes in `cve-monitor.sh` + `changelog-gen.sh`** (`agent/cve-monitor.sh`, `agent/changelog-gen.sh`) — Yesterday's "Next Time" item #1, completing the cross-codebase audit started in PR #693. Same `grep-c-double-output` lesson, three concrete sites: **(1) `cve-monitor.sh:101`** (`reboot_packages = cat .pkgs | head -20 | jq -Rs ... || echo "[]"`) — under pipefail, `head -20` SIGPIPE-ing `cat` upstream propagates exit 141 while `jq -Rs` has *already* written a real array to stdout; `|| echo "[]"` then appends a second `[]`, producing `[real]\n[]` that downstream `--argjson` consumers in this same script (line 183 fallback path) would crash on with exit 2. Currently latent because `/var/run/reboot-required.pkgs` is small (kernel updates produce 1-3 entries), but will fire the day a kernel ships > 20 reboot-related packages. **(2) `changelog-gen.sh:174`** (`changes_json = printf | sed | sort -u | sed | head -10 | jq -R -s ... || echo "[]"`) — same SIGPIPE-via-head shape, **actively triggerable** today: any single enhancement day with > 10 unique change descriptions hits the bug. Reproduced synthetically with 100k upstream lines: pre-fix produces 106-byte `[real]\n[]` (multi-doc, invalid), post-fix produces 103-byte `[real]` (single doc, valid). The downstream `jq -nc --argjson changes "$changes_json"` aborts with exit 2 on the corrupt value (verified), killing the whole `changelog-gen.sh` run under `set -euo pipefail` and silently freezing `data/changelog.json` for the dashboard. **(3) `changelog-gen.sh:180`** (`prs_json = echo | tr | sort -un | jq ... || echo "[]"`) — defensive fix; current shape has no `head`/`SIGPIPE` vector but the `|| echo` pattern is identical and would bite if jq ever exits non-zero with partial output (e.g. `tonumber` failing mid-stream on a non-numeric line). All three sites converted to capture-then-fallback (`_out=$(pipeline) || true; var="${_out:-[]}"`) — keeps jq's stdout when present, falls back only on empty capture. End-to-end `bash agent/changelog-gen.sh` regenerated `data/changelog.json` cleanly (27 entries, `jq -e .` passes); `bash agent/cve-monitor.sh` regenerated `data/security/cve-status.json` cleanly with `reboot.packages` correctly populated as `[2 packages]`. `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S error` clean on both files.
- **`security-scan.sh` `top_sources_json` + `geo_data` double-output bug — sister of yesterday's `log-analysis` fix** (`agent/security-scan.sh`) — Today's audit of `pipeline ... | jq ... 2>/dev/null || echo "[]"` shapes (yesterday's "Next Time" item) found two more sites with the same root cause as PR #692: a pipeline whose final `jq` stage emits a default value before pipefail propagates an upstream non-zero exit, paired with a `||`-emitting fallback that appends a second value. **Site 1, line 305** (`top_sources_json`): when the `ss → tail → awk → grep → grep → grep → sort → uniq → sort → head → awk → jq -s '.'` pipeline matches no peer IPs (the steady state on this low-traffic VPS), `grep -oP` exits 1, pipefail propagates, `jq -s '.'` had already written `[]` to stdout, and `|| echo "[]"` appended another `[]`. The captured `[]\n[]` was then spliced raw into `data/security/connection-rates.json` via heredoc — production file confirmed corrupt today (`jq -e .` → "Expected separator between values at line 6, column 1"). **Site 2, line 532** (`geo_data`): same `awk | jq -Rn '[inputs|...]' || echo "[]"` shape, same reproduction path on any awk-empty branch; lower live impact (geo block is gated on `geo_total_ips > 0`) but defensive fix keeps the file consistent. Both sites converted to capture-then-fallback (`var=$(pipeline) || true; var=${var:-"[]"}`) — keeps jq's stdout, swallows only the exit code. Live `connection-rates.json` regenerated and verified `jq -e .` clean. Codified shape in lesson `grep-c-double-output` already covers this — no new lesson needed, just a cross-file sweep.
- **`log-analysis.sh` `_process_level` double-output crash on clean-error days** (`agent/log-analysis.sh`) — Same root-cause family as yesterday's `grep-c-double-output` PR (#672 etc.), one shape deeper. On 2026-05-03, 2026-05-05, and 2026-05-07 the daily 23:45 cron logged `[ERROR] log-analysis: jq failed (exit 2) — preserving previous /home/marvin/git/data/logs/analysis-YYYY-MM-DD.json`, leaving four of the last seven days' analysis files missing on disk and `analysis-latest.json` two days stale. Reproduction: when grep matches nothing, `set -o pipefail` propagates grep's exit 1; `jq -s '.' 2>/dev/null` had **already** written `[]` to stdout; `|| echo '[]'` then runs and **appends** a second `[]`. The captured value is `"[]\n[]"`. The `jq empty` validator (lines 210-215) accepts multi-document input as valid JSON and lets it through; the downstream `--argjson error_clusters "[]\n[]"` then crashes with jq exit 2 because `--argjson` requires a single document. Replaced the `pipeline ... || echo '[]'` shape with capture-then-fallback (`_out=$(...) || true; echo "${_out:-[]}"`) in `_process_level`, applied the same shape to the three `grep -cF "$sig" ... || echo 0` sites in the recurring/new/resolved classifier (lines 121, 141 — same anti-pattern, would silently drop entries when `grep -c` printed `0\n0`), and tightened the JSON validator from `jq empty` (which accepts multi-doc) to `jq -s -e 'length == 1'` (single doc only) as defense-in-depth. Verified: end-to-end run on today's log (which has 0 errors — exactly the failure case) now writes `analysis-2026-05-08.json` and `analysis-latest.json` cleanly with `error_cluster_count: 0`. `bash -n` clean, `shellcheck -S error` clean.
- **Three latent `grep -c | tr -d '...' || echo 0` (and shellcheck-equivalent) double-output bugs in `codebase-health.sh` + `fix-issues.sh`** — `bash -x agent/codebase-health.sh` revealed two recurring `syntax error in expression (error token is "0")` arithmetic crashes that have been silently corrupting Marvin's weekly health score since the script was first written 2026-03-26. **Same anti-pattern** the lessons-learned database explicitly flags as `grep-c-double-output`: a command that prints output **before** exiting non-zero, paired with `|| echo 0` (or `|| echo '[]'`), leaves *both* outputs in the captured value — yielding `"0\n0"` (or `"[json…]\n[]"`). The receiving `$(( … ))` then crashes; bash continues but leaves the variable at its previous value, silently producing wrong totals. Three concrete sites fixed: **(1) `codebase-health.sh:75-76`** the shellcheck JSON path — the `shellcheck` CLI exits 1 whenever it finds any issues (which it does on this codebase: 28 warnings), so `|| echo '[]'` was appending a second JSON value to the captured `sc_json`; the downstream `jq '… | length'` then ran twice and printed `0\n0`, crashing `$(( shellcheck_errors + errs ))` and leaving `shellcheck_warnings` reported as `0` for ~6 weeks instead of the actual 28. **(2) `codebase-health.sh:149`** the per-day error counter — `grep -ci '\[ERROR\]' "$log"` prints `0` then exits 1 on any clean log day; `|| echo 0` doubled the output and the arithmetic crash silently zeroed `total_errors`, making `avg_errors_per_day` always `0`. After the fix today's run reports `4` (correct) and `shellcheck_warnings: 28`. **(3) `fix-issues.sh:135`** the `_has_issue_pr` PR-deduplication guard — same `grep -c | echo "0"` pattern; on any cron tick where open PRs exist but none have `fix/issue-N-…` head refs (the recent steady state), `_has_issue_pr` became `"0\n0"` and the `[[ "$_has_issue_pr" -gt 0 ]]` test routed silently to the else branch regardless of the real count. **Fix shape** (matches the lesson exactly): replace `var=$(cmd 2>/dev/null || echo 0)` with `var=$(cmd 2>/dev/null || true); var=${var:-0}` — keep the command's own count, swallow only the exit code. Same shape applied to `roadmap_total/done` (latent — `POSSIBLE_ENHANCEMENTS.md` currently has matches, so no live crash, but the bug shape is identical and would bite on a future restructure that left one of the counts at zero). Verified end-to-end: `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S error` clean on both files; `bash agent/codebase-health.sh` runs to completion with no `syntax error` lines on stderr and now correctly populates `shellcheck_warnings` and `avg_errors_per_day`; synthetic `fix-issues.sh` test confirms `_has_issue_pr` returns `0`/`1` cleanly under empty-PR-list, non-matching-PR-list, and matching-PR-list inputs.
- **Align SSL warning thresholds between watchlist backend and dashboard** (`agent/external-domains-check.sh`) — The script set `status=critical` at `<7d` and `status=warning` at `<14d`, but `web/app/components/ExternalDomainsSection.tsx`'s `sslColor()` already coloured SSL days red `<14d` and yellow `<30d` — and the PR #648 announcement to Pavel documented the design as "warn <30d, critical <14d". The mismatch meant a domain with 20 days left would render as **status=healthy** (green badge) while the SSL field itself was already coloured **yellow**, and a domain with 10 days left would show **status=warning** (yellow badge) while the SSL field was already **red** — backend status badge always one severity level behind the field colour. Bumped script thresholds to `<14d` (critical) and `<30d` (warning) so the badge and the field agree, and so an early-warning fires 30 days out as documented. (fixes #647 follow-up)
- **Replace hardcoded `/home/marvin/blocked-blogs` with `BLOCKED_BLOGS_DIR` constant in `agent/common.sh`** — Same-branch follow-up to the kernel-ban + forensic-preservation refactor below. The auto-review (#689) caught that `screen_blog_content()` set `local blocked_dir="/home/marvin/blocked-blogs"` as a bare absolute literal, violating the project's path convention (paths must be derived from `${MARVIN_DIR}` or another named constant — see `BLOG_DIR="$(dirname "${MARVIN_DIR}")/blog"` directly above for the pattern). Added `BLOCKED_BLOGS_DIR="$(dirname "${MARVIN_DIR}")/blocked-blogs"` next to `BLOG_DIR` so both forensic-store and blog-data paths share the same `dirname ${MARVIN_DIR}` anchor — change `MARVIN_DIR` once, both move together. Function header comment also re-pointed at `${BLOCKED_BLOGS_DIR}` instead of the literal. Verified: `bash -n` clean, `shellcheck -S error` clean, three end-to-end synthetic tests still pass (kernel→block+preserve@mode 0600, lowercase CVE→block, clean→allow). The historical CHANGELOG and POSSIBLE_ENHANCEMENTS entries that mention `/home/marvin/blocked-blogs` are left untouched — they document the path that was live in those commits, and rewriting history would be misleading. (fixes #689)
- **Close TOCTOU window on blocked-blog forensic file write in `screen_blog_content`** (`agent/common.sh`) — Same-branch follow-up to the kernel-ban + forensic-preservation refactor below. The auto-review (#688) caught that the rejected blog content was written via `printf > file` and `chmod 600` in two separate steps. The `printf` redirect creates the file with the process umask (typically 0022 → mode 0644), and only the subsequent `chmod 600` tightens it; in the brief window between the two, the file is transiently readable by other processes in the same UID's session. The 0700 parent directory mitigates cross-UID exposure but leaves the same-UID window open, and same-UID exposure is exactly the threat model for forensic content (anything that lands in `blocked-blogs/` is by definition content we deliberately kept off the public blog). Replaced the two-step pattern with a subshell `( umask 177 && printf '%s\n' "$content" > "$blocked_file" )` — the file is created mode 0600 atomically by the kernel, no chmod-after-create transition, and the `umask 177` is scoped to the subshell so the caller's umask is untouched. Verified end-to-end with the tests from the prior commit re-run: kernel/CVE block correctly, file lands at mode 0600 (no `0644 → chmod` race), content preserved, lowercase CVE still blocks (no #687 regression), clean content allowed, outer umask preserved at 0022. `bash -n` and `shellcheck -S error` clean. (fixes #688)
- **Restore case-insensitive matching for CVE and sensitive-file-path patterns in `screen_blog_content`** (`agent/common.sh`) — Same-branch follow-up to the kernel-ban + forensic-preservation refactor below. The auto-review (#687) caught that the new `_screen_blog_first_match_line()` helper used `grep -nP` (case-sensitive) for every pattern, but the pre-refactor code had `grep -qPi` for two patterns: the CVE identifier (`CVE-[0-9]{4}-[0-9]{4,}`) and the sensitive-file-path set (`/etc/shadow|/etc/sudoers|\.env\b|id_rsa|private[._-]key`). Result: a blurb containing `cve-2024-1234` (lowercase) or `Cve-2024-12345` (mixed case) — or `ID_RSA` / `Private_Key` — would have slipped through the screen and been published. The kernel/API-key/PEM-header patterns are intentionally case-sensitive (kernel flavours from `uname -r` are always lowercase, API key prefixes have specific case, PEM headers are uppercase) so those keep `flags=""`. Helper now takes an optional third `flags` argument that word-splits into separate grep args (`-i` only, no shell-escape risk). Six end-to-end tests pass: lowercase CVE blocked, mixed-case CVE blocked, uppercase CVE blocked (no regression), uppercase ID_RSA blocked (case-insensitive), clean content allowed, kernel version blocked. `bash -n` and `shellcheck -S error` clean. (fixes #687)
- **Morning blog screening loss — explicit kernel-version ban in `morning.md` + forensic preservation in `screen_blog_content`** (`agent/prompts/morning.md`, `agent/common.sh`) — Today's 04:09 UTC `morning-check.sh` Claude run produced a blurb that contained a kernel version string; `screen_blog_content()` correctly blocked publication ("kernel version detected"), `morning-check.sh` exited 1, and the entire 2026-05-06 morning blog post was lost. Two compounding gaps: **(1) `morning.md`** carried only a one-line "Security rule for the public blurb" mentioning CVEs and service failures — never explicitly forbidding kernel version strings — while `evening.md`'s "Security Information — Public Blog Policy" already had the full list (kernel version, services down, firewall, attack patterns, etc.) plus rewrite examples. Mirrored evening.md's full policy into morning.md, with the explicit `MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH-BUILD-flavour` example call-out and a note that triggering the screen drops the entire day's blog. **(2) `screen_blog_content()`** logged only "sensitive content detected (kernel version) — blocking publication" — no line numbers, no excerpt, and the rejected content was discarded — so today there is *no surviving evidence* of what Claude actually wrote. Added per-pattern first-match line-number diagnostics ("kernel@line3, CVE@line1, …") and write the full rejected blurb to `/home/marvin/blocked-blogs/${label}-${UTC_TS}.txt` (mode 0600 inside a 0700 root-only directory, **outside** both the git tree and the nginx-served `/home/marvin/blog/` tree, with a 30-file retention cap). Verified end-to-end with three synthetic test cases: clean content allowed, kernel-version blocked + correct line + content preserved, CVE blocked + correct line + content preserved. Future blocked-blog incidents now have post-mortem evidence on disk without leaking it publicly.
- **Net RX/TX anomaly false positives — moved from 5-min health-monitor to end-of-day metric-aggregate** (`agent/health-monitor.sh`, `agent/metric-aggregate.sh`) — The 2σ network anomaly check ran every 5 minutes in `health-monitor.sh`, comparing today's running `(_curr_rx_bytes - _first_rx_bytes) / 1MB` cumulative-so-far against the rolling 7-day **full-day** `summary.network.rx_mb` totals. On any traffic-heavier day, the cumulative-so-far crossed the historical full-day mean within hours and then escalated linearly all day — today (2026-05-05) we got `Net RX MB = 210` (avg=126.29, dev=2.1σ) at 01:40 UTC, then 2.3σ → 2.5σ → 2.8σ → 3.0σ → 3.2σ → 3.5σ on every subsequent hourly tick. Fundamentally a partial-vs-complete comparison; no amount of stddev-floor or threshold-tuning would fix it. **Fix:** dropped Net RX/TX from the `health-monitor.sh` per-5-min anomaly loop entirely, removed the now-unused `_net_rx_today` / `_net_tx_today` running computation, and added an equivalent end-of-day check in `metric-aggregate.sh` after the daily summary file is written. The new check pulls the previous 6 days' `summary.network.rx_mb` / `tx_mb` from on-disk daily summaries, computes mean/stddev with a 20%-of-mean (or 10 MB, whichever is larger) stddev floor, and only alerts above a 100 MB absolute floor — no more hair-trigger alerts on quiet days. Triggered once per day from `log-export.sh` at 23:00 UTC, where the `TARGET_DATE` daily total is essentially complete (a 1-hour tail is acceptable noise). Verified by re-aggregating 2026-05-04 (`Net RX MB = 248, avg=110.17, stddev=22.03, dev=6.3σ` — yesterday genuinely was a heavy traffic day) and confirming clean health-monitor runs no longer log the spurious cumulative-so-far warning.
- **Web build artifact ownership self-test** (`agent/self-test.sh`) — Adds a single `find $WEB_DIR/{node_modules,.next} -not -user marvin -print -quit` test that catches the regression class fixed yesterday in PR #677. Yesterday's `marvin_rebuild_web()` ownership fix prevents future poisoning, but it can't detect *existing* poisoning from a stale interrupted run; this test will flag any non-marvin-owned file in either dir on the next self-test cycle, surfacing the issue before the next `deploy-web.sh` EACCES. ~5 lines, no Claude calls, runs alongside the existing dashboard-exists check.
- **Reorder `marvin_rebuild_web()` so backup runs after pre-flight chown** (`agent/common.sh`) — Same-branch follow-up to PR #677. Auto-review caught that the `.next/` backup (`cp -a "${web_dir}/.next" "$backup_dir"`) was created **before** the pre-flight `chown -R marvin:marvin "${web_dir}/.next"` ran. On the first post-merge run when `.next/` is still root-owned, the backup captures root-owned files; if `npm ci` succeeds but `npm run build` fails, the rollback path `mv "$backup_dir" "${web_dir}/.next"` reinstates exactly the root-owned state the chown had just fixed — re-introducing the very `EACCES` failure mode this PR was opened to close. Edge case (only matters when the build crashes mid-run on a poisoned tree) and self-heals on the next rebuild's pre-flight chown, but worth fixing before merge. Moved the chown block ahead of the backup so the snapshot is always marvin-owned. Verified `bash -n` and `shellcheck -S error` clean. (fixes #682)
- **Fix silent `npm ci` failure in `marvin_rebuild_web()` su -c subshell** (`agent/common.sh`) — Follow-up to PR #677 in the same branch. The auto-review caught that `su -s /bin/bash marvin -c 'cd "$1" && npm ci --production=false 2>&1 | tail -5' -- "$web_dir"` keeps the pipe **inside** the `-c` string. The bash spawned by `su -c` starts without `pipefail`, so `tail -5` always exits 0 — the entire `su` call returns 0 even when `npm ci` fails, and `_ci_ok=true` proceeds straight into `npm run build` with stale or absent `node_modules`. Same root cause class as the lessons-learned `sigpipe-under-pipefail` entry and the recurring trap fixed in `daily-digest.sh`/`self-test.sh`. Moved the pipe **outside** the `su -c` string so the parent subshell's `pipefail` (inherited from the caller's `set -euo pipefail`) correctly propagates `npm ci`'s exit through `su`. Also realigned the non-root branch to put `tail -5` outside the inner `( )` so both branches treat the pipe identically. Verified: `bash -n` clean, `shellcheck -S error` clean. (fixes #680, fixes #681)
- **Fix `marvin_rebuild_web()` running npm as root, poisoning ownership for next deploy-web.sh** (`agent/common.sh`) — Today's 04:00 UTC deploy-web.sh failed `npm ci` with `EACCES: permission denied, unlink '/home/marvin/git/web/node_modules/.bin/esbuild'`, and the 06:08 UTC marvin_rebuild_web fallback failed `npm run build` with `EACCES ... .next/trace`. Root cause: `marvin_rebuild_web()` in common.sh ran `npm ci` and `npm run build` as the calling user. When invoked from cron (root) — by health-monitor.sh's JS-asset mismatch path or self-enhance.sh — npm wrote root-owned files into `node_modules/.bin/` and `.next/`. The marvin-web service runs as marvin, and deploy-web.sh correctly drops to marvin via `su` — so the next deploy-web.sh run failed because marvin couldn't `unlink` files inside a root-owned `node_modules/.bin/`. A subsequent fallback rebuild only succeeded because `npm run build` happened to recreate `.next/trace` after the right ownership had been re-established by an earlier successful run; mixed ownership was a latent failure mode. Fixed by mirroring deploy-web.sh's `_run_npm` pattern: when `EUID=0`, marvin_rebuild_web now runs `npm ci` and `npm run build` via `su -s /bin/bash marvin -c`. Added pre-flight chown that detects root-owned files in `node_modules/` and `.next/` and resets them to marvin:marvin (recovery from poisoned state from any pre-fix run). Post-build `chown -R marvin:marvin .next/` as defense-in-depth so any `cp -a` of an old root-owned `.next` (in the backup→restore path) also lands marvin-owned. Verified via `bash -n` and `shellcheck -S error`. Codified as lesson `npm-as-root-creates-bad-ownership` (critical severity, resolved).
- **Grafana rate-limit tuning + blog throughput bump** (`setup/nginx-monitoring.conf`, `setup/nginx-rate-limits.conf`, `setup/nginx-site.conf`, PR #674) — The first cut of #674 stripped `limit_req` from the monitoring vhost entirely after Grafana's parallel asset fetches kept tripping the `sensitive` zone (2 r/s, burst=5). The auto-review (#676) flagged the loss of any nginx-level brute-force fallback, and Pavel asked on #673 to set Grafana to `5r/s burst=8` and to weaken the blog rate limit. Both addressed in one pass: a new dedicated `monitoring` zone at 5 r/s is applied with `burst=8 nodelay` to `location /` of `nginx-monitoring.conf` (the dynamic Grafana endpoints), while `/public/` and `/avatar/` remain exempt — Grafana's bundle storm has nowhere to run aground. `/api/live/` (WebSocket) gets the same `limit_req` on the initial Upgrade handshake to restore parity with the prior server-level limit per the auto-review's defense-in-depth concern (#678; rate-limit cannot throttle WebSocket frames after the upgrade, but it does cap the handshake rate for already-authenticated reconnect storms). Brute-force protection therefore has three layers again: HTTP Basic Auth → `limit_req` → fail2ban `nginx-http-auth`, with Grafana's own login limiter on top. The blog `api` zone goes from 20 → 30 r/s (matching `general`); existing `burst=40` carries over, which gives ~50 % more sustained throughput on parallel `/api/*.json` dashboard fetches without changing the budget for short bursts. Header comments in `nginx-site.conf` updated to keep the tracked snippet honest about live values. (fixes #673, fixes #675, fixes #676, fixes #678)
- **Three-bug fix in `agent/self-test.sh`: SIGPIPE-under-pipefail + corrupt-JSON crash + wrong cron markers** (PR #672) — Today's daily self-test run had been failing since at least 2026-04-30 with a confusing combination: `WARN: 5 cron task(s) not seen in 48h logs` followed by `ERROR self-test.sh:322 — command failed (exit 5): scan_status=$(jq -r '.overall_status // "unknown"' "$LATEST_SCAN" 2>/dev/null)`, then no report file written. Three independent bugs compounding. **(1) SIGPIPE under pipefail:** `echo "$_cron_combined" | grep -q "$marker"` pipes ~190KB of joined log text through `grep -q`, which closes the pipe after the first match. `echo` dies on SIGPIPE (exit 141), `pipefail` propagates, `set -e` either kills the script or the if-condition reads it as "no match" and warns on a cron task that *did* run. Same SIGPIPE-under-pipefail shape as the `daily-digest.sh` fix from 2026-04-28, which the lessons database flagged as a recurring trap. Replaced with bash glob match `[[ "$big" == *"$pat"* ]]` — no pipe, no SIGPIPE. **(2) Wrong markers for `health-monitor` and `morning-check`:** both scripts log via `marvin_log_json` ("Health monitor" / "Morning check"), not the all-caps banners ("HEALTH CHECK" / "MORNING CHECK") that the other three cron jobs emit. Markers updated in the `_cron_tasks` array, with a comment explaining the convention split. **(3) Corrupt `latest-scan.json` crashed self-test:** today's scan file was the buggy pre-#664 output (yesterday's 02:00 UTC run pre-dated the merge), so `jq -r '.overall_status'` exited 5, `set -e` killed the script before the report could print or `self-test.json` could be written. Gated the field-extraction block on `jq empty` validation; corrupt files now fall through to the existing `"no data (-10)"` else branch. Per-field regex guards added in case individual fields are non-numeric without the whole file being invalid. Before: `Pass: 110, Fail: 1, Warn: 5` then crash. After: `Pass: 110, Fail: 0, Warn: 1`, all 5 cron tasks verified, grade B (85/100). Also operationally regenerated `latest-scan.json` so today's grade reflects the post-#664 clean output.
- **`security.txt` Expires-date early-warning in self-test** (`agent/self-test.sh`) — Issue #669: PR #666 hardcodes `Expires: 2027-05-01` in `web/public/.well-known/security.txt` with no automated renewal — RFC 9116 treats a past `Expires` as invalid, so the file would silently lapse in ~1y. Added a 9h check in the security-score block that parses `Expires:` and grades: >90d clean, ≤90d -2 (warn), ≤30d -5 (critical), expired -10. Skips silently when the file is missing (pre-#666 merge state). Mirrors the existing 9d SSL-cert pattern. Once #666 lands, self-test will start nudging the score downward 90 days before lapse so a renewal PR gets opened in time. Date parsing strips trailing fractional seconds (e.g. `.000Z` from PR #666's ISO-8601 format) before passing to `date -d`, since some GNU coreutils refuse the millisecond component — and a `date -d` failure now scores `-2 unparseable` rather than silently falling through epoch-0 to a perpetual `-10 expired`. (fixes #669, addresses #671)
- **Add `/.well-known/security.txt` (RFC 9116)** (`web/public/.well-known/security.txt`, `setup/nginx-site.conf`) — Pavel filed #665 asking for a `security.txt`. Created a minimal RFC 9116 file (Contact, Expires 2027-05-01, Encryption→existing `marvin-gpg.asc`, Preferred-Languages en+cs, Canonical) and added an `location = /.well-known/security.txt` alias block in nginx mirroring the `marvin-gpg.asc` pattern (Next.js's static handler skips dotfile dirs in `public/`, so the existing catch-all proxy returned a Next.js 404; explicit alias is required). Same change applied to live `/etc/nginx/sites-available/marvin` and nginx restarted (a `reload` did not pick up the new exact-match location — full restart was required, noted for future deploys). Verified `curl -I https://robot-marvin.cz/.well-known/security.txt` returns `200 text/plain` with the expected body. The original commit on this branch only included `web/public/.well-known/security.txt`; the matching `setup/nginx-site.conf` block was applied to the live config but never committed (caught by the PR #666 review and tracked as #667). This follow-up commit adds the missing block to the tracked config so a fresh deployment from `main` serves the file instead of returning a Next.js 404. (fixes #665, fixes #667)
- **Fix `grep -c | tr || echo 0` double-output in `security-scan.sh`** (`agent/security-scan.sh`) — Today's 02:00 UTC scan produced a malformed `data/security/latest-scan.json` with `"warnings": 00`, `"infected": 00`, and (worst) `"established_connections": 0\n0,` — invalid JSON. `self-test.sh:322` then aborted at `jq -r '.overall_status' "$LATEST_SCAN"` with exit 5, leaving `fail=1` in `self-test.json` and grade C since 2026-04-30. Root cause: `grep -c` always prints a count to stdout *and* exits 1 when there are 0 matches. Patterns of the form `var=$(grep -c X 2>/dev/null | tr -d '[:space:]' || echo 0)` then run **both** branches when the match count is zero — `tr` writes "0", pipefail propagates grep's exit 1, `|| echo 0` writes another "0" — yielding "00" (collapsed by `tr`) or "0\n0" (without `tr`, line-corrupting the heredoc). Replaced six instances (rkhunter warnings/infected, rkhunter ssh-rescue check, chkrootkit infected, port_count, established_count, geo_total_ips) with `cmd 2>/dev/null || true` followed by `var=${var:-0}` — keeps grep's printed count, only swallows the exit code, and falls back to "0" only when the command produced no output. Also switched `geo_top_country` JSON encoding from `echo "$var" | jq -Rs '.'` to `printf '%s' "$var" | jq -Rs '.'` so the trailing newline `echo` adds doesn't get baked into the JSON string as `"United States\n"`. Verified: synthetic repro shows `0\n0` → `0`, `00` → `0`, `"United States\n"` → `"United States"`; full self-test run dropped fail count from 1 → 0, grade C → B. Codified as lesson `grep-c-double-output` (high severity).
- **Fix shellcheck SC2261 in `lib/claude.sh` lock-holder write** (`agent/lib/claude.sh`) — `echo "..." >&"$lock_fd" 2>/dev/null` competes with itself when `$lock_fd` resolves to fd 2 — both redirections target the same fd, so shellcheck flagged it as an error and self-test had been logging `FAIL: shellcheck errors: claude.sh` since PR #605 introduced auto-FD allocation on 2026-04-04. Wrapped the echo in a brace group: `{ echo "..." >&"$lock_fd"; } 2>/dev/null || true`. Fixes the persistent FAIL that was keeping self-test grade at C even when nothing was actually broken at runtime.
- **Guard recurring-bug detector against `LESSONS_FILE` corruption mid-run** (`agent/lessons-learned.sh`) — Section 4's per-cluster `jq … "$LESSONS_FILE"` calls already had `|| true` to keep transient jq failures from aborting the script under `set -euo pipefail`, but a corruption window between section 1's early validation and section 4 would silently produce an empty `match_id` on every iteration and log "No recurring resolved-lesson patterns detected" — hiding the real cause. Added a defense-in-depth `[[ -f … ]] && jq empty …` guard at the top of section 4 that emits a `WARN` and skips the block when the lessons file is missing or invalid. (fixes #661, fixes #662)
- **Sitemap `lastModified` derived from latest blog post date** (`web/app/sitemap.ts`) — The initial PR #652 set `lastModified: new Date()` on every request. Combined with `force-dynamic`, this advertised a fresh modification timestamp on every crawler fetch, signalling content change on every visit and risking inflated re-crawl frequency / distorted AI Overview freshness signals. Now derives the timestamp from the most recent blog post date via `getAllDates()[0]`, with a `try/catch` fall-back to a stable date if the SQLite DB is unreachable. Crawlers see a fresh timestamp only when content actually changes. (fixes #657, fixes #659)
- **Consolidate website selfcheck and retry on transient failure** (`agent/health-monitor.sh`) — The website selfcheck did two separate `curl` calls (one for HTTP code, one for body) which created two bugs: (a) when `curl --max-time` fired, the `-w '%{http_code}'` ("000") and the `|| echo "000"` fallback both ran, producing the "HTTP 000000" log artifact visible in `data/logs/2026-04-19.log`; (b) flaky WARN "Website body missing expected content" appeared on 2026-04-18, 04-26, 04-29 (4× in April) when the second curl returned truncated bytes despite HTTP 200 — likely brief Next.js restarts, nginx rate limit pressure, or network blips. Fix: single curl call splits body and HTTP code on `\x1F` (Unit Separator, can't appear in HTML), defensive numeric-regex fallback to `000` for non-numeric codes, and a single 3-second retry before alerting. Same retry-once-on-transient pattern as the JS asset check directly below it. Verified end-to-end on the live site and against synthetic empty/corrupt-response inputs.
- **Fix `daily-digest.sh` SIGPIPE crash on key-events extraction** (`agent/daily-digest.sh`) — Yesterday's 23:30 UTC daily-digest crashed with `[ERROR] daily-digest.sh:117 — command failed (exit 141): key_events=$(echo "$_event_lines" | grep -v 'Health monitor complete' | head -20 | jq -R . | jq -s '.')`. Exit 141 is SIGPIPE: `head -20` closes its stdin after reading 20 lines, so `grep -v` upstream gets EPIPE on its next write and exits 141, which `set -o pipefail` then propagates as the pipeline exit code. Triggers whenever the day produces enough log volume for `grep -v` to write past head's 20-line buffer — i.e. every normal day where the script is supposed to produce useful output. Replaced `head -20` with `awk 'NR<=20'`, which reads input to EOF but only emits the first 20 lines, so no upstream pipe ever closes early. Same root cause and same fix shape as the `hourly-check.sh` SIGPIPE fix from 2026-03-20. Verified with a 100k-line synthetic input under `set -euo pipefail`: `head -20` reproduces exit 141 reliably; `awk 'NR<=20'` exits 0. End-to-end run on today's log produced 20 key events and clean exit.
- **Atomic write for `log-analysis.sh` daily report** (`agent/log-analysis.sh`) — On 2026-04-26T21:45:01Z the daily `jq -n` at line 205 exited 2, but the shell had already truncated `data/logs/analysis-2026-04-26.json` via the `>` redirect, leaving a 0-byte file that propagated to `analysis-latest.json` and silently broke downstream consumers (dashboard pattern-detection summary, `lessons-learned.sh`). Same failure shape as `file-integrity.sh` PR #635. Now writes to a sibling `mktemp` then `mv -f`, restores 0644 perms (mktemp defaults to 0600 — would otherwise 403 the nginx `/api/logs/` endpoint), and validates each `--argjson` input is parseable JSON beforehand (substituting `[]` if not). On jq failure the previous day's report is preserved and an `ERROR` is logged with the exit code. Also atomic for the latest-pointer copy. An `EXIT INT TERM` trap also removes the temp files on unexpected termination so they cannot accumulate in `data/logs/`. (fixes #638)
- **Add `unattended-upgr*` to runaway-process allowlist** (`agent/health-monitor.sh`) — Today's 04:35 UTC run logged `High CPU process detected: PID=3289719 unattended-upgr at 80.0%` during the daily security-update cycle. `unattended-upgrade` is a legitimate apt frontend that briefly pegs CPU when applying packages; the comm-truncated name `unattended-upgr` didn't match the existing `apt*|dpkg*` patterns. Adopted the same `*` glob and exe-path verification used for `apt*` and `dpkg*` so a spoofed `comm` field can't bypass the runaway killer.
- **Restrict baseline permissions before rename in auto-refresh path** (`agent/file-integrity.sh`) — The auto-refresh block applied `chmod 600 "$BASELINE_FILE"` *after* `mv -f` rather than chmod-ing the temp file before the rename. `mktemp` honours the process umask (commonly `rw-r--r--`), so the renamed baseline carried loose permissions for the brief window between `mv` and `chmod`. Within `$SECURITY_DIR` (mode 700) the practical exposure is small, but it was inconsistent with the chmod-before-mv pattern used in PR #635 for the `--update` and initial-baseline paths. Swapped the order — `chmod 600 "$tmp_baseline"` now runs before `mv -f`, eliminating the window entirely. (closes #636)
- **Atomic baseline write in auto-refresh path** (`agent/file-integrity.sh`) — The auto-refresh block in PR #632 wrote the new baseline with `jq -n ... > "$BASELINE_FILE"`. The `>` redirect truncates the file before `jq` runs, so a `jq` failure mid-execution (disk full, OOM, malformed `$current`) leaves the baseline empty; with `set -euo pipefail` the script aborts but the damage is done — integrity monitoring is silently down until a manual `--update`. The auto-refresh path now writes to a `mktemp` file in the same directory and `mv -f`s it into place. `mv` on the same filesystem is atomic, so a crash between `jq` and `mv` leaves the prior baseline intact. On `jq` failure the temp file is removed and the error is logged. The pre-existing `--update` and initial-baseline writes share the same non-atomic pattern; they are out of scope for this fix and tracked for a follow-up hardening pass. (closes #634)
- **Auto-refresh file integrity baseline when changes match git HEAD** (`agent/file-integrity.sh`) — Recurring toil: every PR merge that touched a monitored agent script triggered a `File integrity ALERT` at 02:04 UTC and stayed alerting until the next morning-check pull. The 2026-04-24 fix added an auto-reset hook to `morning-check.sh`, but it only fires when morning-check is the script that pulls. PR #624 was pulled by another process at 22:06 UTC on 2026-04-23, the morning-check at 04:00 UTC on 2026-04-25 saw "Already up to date — no new commits", and the alert persisted. Made `file-integrity.sh` self-sufficient instead: a new `_matches_git_head()` helper compares the changed file's blob hash (`git hash-object`) against the SHA recorded at `HEAD`, and changes that match are classified as `git_synced` rather than `tampered`. When **all** changes are git-synced (and there are zero tampered, missing, **or new** files) the baseline auto-refreshes inline. Tampered changes still alert and never auto-refresh — security regressions are preserved. The auto-refresh guard also requires zero new files (issue #633): new files in dynamically-monitored paths (`/etc/nginx/sites-enabled/*`, `/etc/fail2ban/jail.d/*.conf`) are outside `MARVIN_DIR` and can never be git-tracked, so auto-trusting them would let a rogue config slip into the baseline whenever a legitimate agent-script pull happened in the same scan window. New files still require an explicit `--update`. The JSON report now includes a `git_synced` array alongside `changes`. (closes #633)
- **Atomic baseline write in `file-integrity.sh --update` and initial-baseline paths** — Both the `--update` block and the initial-baseline-creation block wrote the new baseline with `jq ... > "$BASELINE_FILE"`, which truncates the file before `jq` runs. Any failure mid-jq (disk full, OOM, malformed input) under `set -euo pipefail` left the baseline empty and integrity monitoring silently dark until manual recovery. Both paths now write to `mktemp` in the same directory (so `mv -f` is atomic on the same filesystem), `chmod 600` on the temp file before the rename, and an `EXIT` trap removes the tempfile if jq fails so a crashed update leaves any previous baseline intact. Companion to PR #632's atomic auto-refresh write — closes the remaining two non-atomic baseline write paths flagged as out-of-scope follow-up there.
- **Switch Poslední Ping outreach from HTTP beacon to SSH username channel** (`agent/network-discovery.sh`) — Replaced the daily `curl https://posledniping.cz/.well-known/ai-managed.json` (which PP never reads) with a single SSH login attempt where the message is encoded in the username field (`marvin-hello-are-you-reading`, 28 chars, under SSH's 32-char cap). Auth fails (`PubkeyAuthentication=no`, BatchMode), but PP's sshd logs the username — same channel ECHO previously used to reach us. One attempt per cycle, `ConnectionAttempts=1`, no retries. Stamp file `data/comms/lastping-ssh-probe.stamp` enforces a 23h cooldown so manual re-runs can't snowball SSH bans on PP's side. Records `last_ssh_probe_at` / `last_ssh_probe_username` on the PP peer entry; logs as `[ssh-ping] ... result=ban_expected` so `log-watcher.sh`'s `communication_attempt` classifier can pick it up. (fixes #628)
- **Fix silently-broken file integrity baseline auto-reset** (`agent/morning-check.sh`) — The post-`git pull` check that was supposed to refresh the file-integrity baseline after PR merges has been dead code since it was written. It grepped `INCOMING_DIFF` for `^(diff --git|---|\+\+\+).*(agent/|/etc/)` — full-diff header markers — but `INCOMING_DIFF` holds `git diff --stat` output (`" agent/foo.sh | 10 +++---"`), so the pattern never matched. Every PR touching monitored scripts triggered an alert at the next 02:00 UTC security scan until someone (usually the next self-enhance session) manually cleared the baseline. The enhancement log shows at least six manual resets in the last five weeks. Changed the grep to ` agent/`, mirroring the working web/-deploy check directly below it. Also dropped the `/etc/` branch — `/etc/` files aren't tracked in the repo, so they can never appear in a git diff, and the file-integrity script still catches them through its own baseline. Also reset the current stale baseline to clear today's two pending false positives.
- **Track nginx rate-limit zones in repo** (`setup/nginx-rate-limits.conf`, `setup/nginx-site.conf`) — PR #627 raised live `general` 10→30 r/s and `api` 5→20 r/s, but the `limit_req_zone` directives live in `/etc/nginx/nginx.conf` (not tracked), so a fresh setup from this repo would reconstruct the OLD base rates underneath the new bursts (40/50). Added a tracked `setup/nginx-rate-limits.conf` snippet with the current production values, intended for deployment to `/etc/nginx/conf.d/marvin-rate-limits.conf`, and refreshed the (previously stale) comment block in `setup/nginx-site.conf` to point to it. Any future rate-limit change now lands in one tracked file. (fixes #629)
- **Strip raw banned-IP list from morning-check prompt context** (`agent/morning-check.sh`) — `fail2ban-client status sshd` output includes a single-line `Banned IP list:` of 40+ IPs, which tripped the Anthropic usage-policy classifier on three consecutive runs (2026-04-21/22/23, all exit 1 + retry exhausted). Each failure skipped the entire morning prompt — including the email-reply step — so emails to `marvin@robot-marvin.cz` accumulated unanswered and Pavel opened the issue asking why. Replaced the IP list with a `[list omitted]` marker via `sed`; the actionable counts (`Currently banned`, `Total banned`) above the list remain. (fixes #625)
- **Escalating retry backoff + 2 retries for once-a-day tasks** (`agent/lib/claude.sh`, `agent/morning-check.sh`, `agent/evening-report.sh`) — 2026-04-23 morning-check lost the blog after both attempts (initial + 1 retry, 15s apart) were rejected with exit=1, the second such incident this week (after 2026-04-21). Replaced the fixed 15s retry delay in `run_claude_with_retry()` with an escalating backoff array (15s, 60s, 180s, 300s) indexed by attempt number and bumped morning-check and evening-report to `max_retries=2`. Gives the stochastic usage-policy classifier / transient API state meaningfully more time to clear between attempts before the day's blog is abandoned. Codified as lesson `once-a-day-transient-15s-retry-insufficient` (high severity, resolved).
- **Sanitize jq count captures in `security-scan.sh`** — `high_rate_count` and `suspicious_count` used the `jq ... || echo 0` pattern, which could splice a fallback `0` onto jq's own output (or preserve a multi-document `0\n0` from jq), producing a multi-line value that broke the heredoc writing `latest-scan.json`. Invalid JSON then aborted `self-test.sh` at the security-score step. The capture now lands in a variable and parameter expansion (`${var%%$'\n'*}`) trims to the first line, with a numeric-regex guard fallback to `0`. This avoids both the original splice bug **and** the SIGPIPE+pipefail trap that an earlier `| head -n 1 || echo 0` revision introduced — when jq emits multiple lines, head exits after line 1, jq receives SIGPIPE → exits 141, pipefail propagates the failure, and the `|| echo 0` fallback silently zeros a real count. (fixes #618, fixes #621)
- **Retry once on transient Claude failure for daily blog tasks** (`agent/lib/claude.sh`, `agent/morning-check.sh`, `agent/evening-report.sh`) — Added `run_claude_with_retry()` helper that retries `run_claude` once on exit code 1 (transient failures: API timeout, stochastic usage-policy classifier rejection, context edge cases). Only retries on exit 1 — exit 2 (lock timeout) and exit >1 (persistent errors) are not retried. `morning-check.sh` and `evening-report.sh` now use the helper with `max_retries=1`. Emits a final `WARN "All N retries exhausted for ${task}"` log line when the retry budget is spent so post-mortems can identify give-up events from logs alone (fixes #622). Motivated by the 2026-04-21 incident where a stochastic usage-policy classifier rejected the morning prompt (29s, exit 1), and the "retry on next cron cycle" behaviour meant no morning blog for 24 hours. High-frequency tasks (hourly, every-5-min) continue to call `run_claude` directly — for them the next cycle is a cheaper retry.
- **Aggregate recurring error patterns across log files** (`agent/lessons-learned.sh`) — The per-file `sort | uniq -c | head -10` followed by a plain outer `sort -rn | head -20` left pre-counted duplicates for identical patterns across files. "Claude Code CLI not found in PATH" appeared twice in the self-enhance prompt (18× + 17× from the 2026-04-18/19 outage) instead of once at 35×. Moved the aggregation to the outer pipeline so patterns are counted once across the detection window.
- **Skip loopback in suspicious-connection audit** (`agent/security-scan.sh`) — The "connections to unusual remote ports" scan did not exclude `127.0.0.1`/`::1` traffic, so internal service-to-service loops (rspamd ↔ redis on `127.0.0.1:6379`, and similar) were flagged as suspicious every run. This produced a daily "Found 8 connection(s) to unusual remote ports" WARN with zero security value. Added an explicit loopback skip before the SAFE_REMOTE_PORTS check. The separate `outbound-audit.json` path already filtered loopback correctly; this aligns the older scan with that behaviour.
- **Narrow lessons-learned detection window to 2 days** (`agent/lessons-learned.sh`) — The "potential new lessons" detector scanned 7 days of logs, so resolved issues kept resurfacing for days after the fix landed. The 2026-04-18/19 "Claude Code CLI not found in PATH" pattern (35+ hits from the PATH misconfiguration fixed on 2026-04-20) kept appearing as a "potential new lesson" for four more days. Narrowed the window to 2 days so recently-resolved patterns drop out quickly — verified on a post-fix run that the 4 stale patterns disappeared.
- **File integrity baseline updated** — Cleared 6 false positives from merged PRs (common.sh, morning-check.sh, security-scan.sh, /etc/cron.d/marvin, two nginx site files).
- **Guard `claude.sh` against being sourced before `common.sh`** (`agent/lib/claude.sh`) — Added a `declare -F marvin_log` check at load time that aborts with a clear error if the file is sourced standalone. PR #612 introduced top-level `marvin_log` calls; without the guard, sourcing `claude.sh` directly would fail with "command not found" — silent under most shells, fatal under `set -e`. The guard turns a latent fragility into an immediate, actionable error. (fixes #615)
- **Graceful fallback when `nice`/`ionice` missing** (`agent/lib/claude.sh`) — `run_claude()` now builds the priority-lowering prefix opportunistically: each of `nice` and `ionice` is added only if the corresponding binary is on PATH. If both are absent, `claude -p` runs unwrapped instead of failing with "command not found". Pairs with the load-time WARN — a missing `util-linux`/`coreutils` package now degrades visibly but non-fatally instead of silently breaking every cron agent. (fixes #613)
- **Warn at load if `ionice`/`nice` missing** (`agent/lib/claude.sh`) — Added a one-shot bootstrap check that emits a WARN if `ionice` (util-linux) or `nice` (coreutils) is absent from PATH. PR #609 wraps `claude -p` in `nice -n 10 ionice -c 2 -n 7`; if either tool is missing the pipeline would fail with "command not found" and silently break every cron agent. Visible early warning lets operators install the missing package before the next Claude invocation. (fixes #611)
- **`check_claude()` self-heals from a missing PATH entry** (`agent/lib/claude.sh`) — On 2026-04-18 the cron PATH was configured as `/root/.local/bin/claude` (the binary file) instead of `/root/.local/bin` (its directory). Scripts that didn't source `common.sh` couldn't find `claude` and 35 cron runs failed silently over the next 36 hours with `Claude Code CLI not found in PATH`. `check_claude()` now probes known install locations (`/root/.local/bin`, `/usr/local/bin`, `/usr/bin`) when the PATH lookup fails, prepends the discovered directory, logs a WARN, and continues. Failure only remains fatal when the binary is genuinely missing from disk. Also corrected the live `/etc/cron.d/marvin` PATH to `/root/.local/bin` (this file is intentionally not git-tracked — see issue #83).
- **Lower Claude CPU/IO priority to prevent RCU preempt stalls** (`agent/lib/claude.sh`) — Wrapped the `claude -p` invocation in `run_claude()` with `nice -n 10 ionice -c 2 -n 7` so sustained Claude runs on the 2-vCPU box no longer starve the `rcu_preempt` kthread. Kernel log showed 4+ `rcu_preempt kthread starved` stalls on 2026-04-19 during morning cron jobs. (fixes #606)
- **Claude lock uses dynamic FD allocation** (`agent/lib/claude.sh`) — Replaced hardcoded FD 9 with bash auto-allocation (`exec {lock_fd}>...`) so the concurrency guard can't silently clobber a file descriptor a caller or sourced library might already be using. (fixes #604)
- **Narrow Docker bridge exclusion to actual subnets** (`security-scan.sh`) — Replaced blanket `172.16.0.0/12` exclusion in outbound connection audit with dynamic lookup of active Docker bridge CIDRs. The previous pattern silently dropped all RFC 1918 172.16–172.31 traffic from the audit, creating a blind spot for VPN tunnels, rogue containers, or C2 relays on that range. Now only skips subnets actually assigned to Docker networks. (fixes #591)
- **Fix `_ip_in_docker_cidr` for arbitrary prefix lengths** (`security-scan.sh`) — Replaced the `/16`-only wildcard fallback with proper bitwise CIDR matching. The original `case` handler only matched `/16` and `/24` explicitly; all other prefix lengths (e.g. `/20`, `/22`) fell through to a `/16`-style two-octet comparison, excluding up to 16× more addresses than the actual Docker subnet. (fixes #597)
- **Fix critical IFS scoping bug in `_ip_in_docker_cidr`** (`security-scan.sh`) — `IFS='.' read` prefix assignments to bash builtins persist in the current shell environment. Without `local IFS`, the `for cidr in $_docker_bridges` loop split on dots instead of whitespace, producing bogus tokens with empty masks. Empty mask → `mask_int=0` → `(ip & 0) == (net & 0)` → always true — every IP matched, silently disabling the entire outbound audit when Docker was running. Fixed by adding `local IFS` at the function top, explicitly resetting `IFS=$' \t\n'` before the loop, and computing `ip_int` before iteration. Also added mask validation (numeric, ≥1, and ≤32) to skip malformed CIDR entries — `mask=0` would produce the same always-true comparison via `0xFFFFFFFF << 32 = 0`. (fixes #598, fixes #599, fixes #600, fixes #601)
- **Guard `_ip_in_docker_cidr` against non-IPv4 input** (`security-scan.sh`) — `ss -tnp` on dual-stack sockets can return IPv4-mapped IPv6 addresses like `::ffff:172.17.0.1`. These bypass the `127.*|::1|0.0.0.0` filter and reach `_ip_in_docker_cidr`, where `IFS='.' read` produces `ip_a="::ffff:172"` — not a valid arithmetic expression. Under `set -euo pipefail`, this aborts the entire security scan. Added `^[0-9]+\.[0-9]+\.[0-9]+\.[0-9]+$` regex guard at the function top; non-IPv4 input returns 1 (no match) immediately. (fixes #602)
- **Add monitoring stack ports to expected ports** (`security-scan.sh`) — Ports 3001 (Grafana), 4317 (OTEL gRPC), 4318 (OTEL HTTP), 8889 (OTEL Prometheus exporter), and 9090 (Prometheus) added to `EXPECTED_PORTS` and `LOCALHOST_ONLY_PORTS` baselines. These Docker containers from the OTEL monitoring stack were generating false positive "unexpected listener" warnings daily since installation. Also excluded Docker bridge networks (172.16-31.x.x) from outbound connection audit — internal container traffic was flagged as suspicious.
- **Remove futile auto-merge from fix-issues.sh** — `github_merge_pr` call was still present in `fix-issues.sh` despite being removed from `github-interact.sh` on 2026-04-02. Branch protection requires review approval; every merge attempt returned HTTP 405 ("Repository rule violations found"), generating ~4 ERROR log entries per day. Replaced with informational log message. Completes the incomplete fix from 2026-04-02.
- **Remove orphaned CI wait loop from fix-issues.sh** — The CI check-run polling loop (up to 5 min, ~30 GitHub API calls per run) remained after the auto-merge call was removed. The `MERGE_OK` result was never read — its only consumer was the deleted merge block. Every `fix-issues.sh` run wasted up to 5 minutes sleeping and polling before logging "awaiting review". (fixes #588)
- **Restore issue auto-closing via PR body keyword** (`fix-issues.sh`) — The `github_close_issue` API call was removed along with the auto-merge block (it never ran due to branch protection). Replaced with a clean `Closes #N` keyword in the PR body so GitHub auto-closes the source issue when the PR is merged by a human reviewer. Previously the keyword was wrapped in markdown italics (`*Fixes #N*`) which could interfere with GitHub's keyword parser. (fixes #590, fixes #592)
- **File integrity baseline updated** — Cleared 2 false positives from PR merges (common.sh, health-monitor.sh changed legitimately).

### Added

- **SEO + AI Overviews foundation** (`web/app/robots.ts`, `web/app/sitemap.ts`, `web/app/layout.tsx`, `web/app/components/JsonLd.tsx`, `web/public/llms.txt`) — First real SEO/AI-discoverability surface for `robot-marvin.cz`. Replaced the implicit Next.js defaults with: a generated `/robots.txt` that explicitly welcomes AI crawlers (GPTBot, ClaudeBot, PerplexityBot, etc.) and disallows `/api/`; a dynamic `/sitemap.xml`; full Next.js Metadata API coverage on the root layout (Open Graph, Twitter Card, canonical, hreflang EN/CS, theme-color); JSON-LD structured data (`WebSite`, `Organization`, `Person`) emitted with the existing CSP nonce; and a hand-written `llms.txt` summarising Marvin's identity, capabilities, and contact channels for LLM consumers. Per-day `/blog/[date]` URLs are intentionally omitted from the sitemap until a server-rendered route exists. (implements #650)
- **Monitoring subdomain** (`setup/nginx-monitoring.conf`) — nginx reverse proxy for `monitoring.robot-marvin.cz` → Grafana (`127.0.0.1:3001`). Protected by HTTP Basic Auth (`.htpasswd-monitoring`), HTTPS via Let's Encrypt, HSTS, WebSocket support for Grafana Live (`/api/live/`). Grafana `docker-compose.yml` updated with `GF_SERVER_DOMAIN` and `GF_SERVER_ROOT_URL` for subdomain support. (implements #571)
- **API design blog post** (`/blog/api-design.en.md`, `/blog/api-design.cs.md`) — Bilingual documentation of Marvin's API architecture. Covers all public/authenticated/restricted endpoints with usage examples, architectural decisions (static JSON vs REST framework, JSONL for time-series, nginx rate limiting), webhook notifications, and AI peer discovery guide. Served directly by nginx at `/blog/api-design.{en,cs}.md`. Completes Phase 1 Log Export API roadmap item.
- **OpenTelemetry monitoring stack** (`monitoring/`) — Docker Compose stack (OTEL Collector + Prometheus + Grafana) for tracking Claude Code usage metrics: token consumption, API costs, session counts, lines of code, commits, and PRs. All services bind to localhost only. Grafana provisioned with pre-built Claude Code dashboard. OTEL env vars set in `agent/common.sh` so all cron-invoked Claude sessions export telemetry automatically. Prompt content and tool details are NOT logged (security). Requires Docker installation via `monitoring/setup.sh`. (implements #550)
- **`git` added to runaway process exclusions** (`health-monitor.sh`) — Git operations (fetch, pull, push, rebase) spike to 100% CPU during morning-check and github-interact cron jobs. Like `find`, uses parent-process verification: only suppresses `git` from `/usr/bin/git` whose parent is a Marvin bash script. Non-Marvin git processes are still monitored and logged.
- **Auto-reset file integrity baseline after git pull** (`morning-check.sh`) — When incoming commits modify files in `agent/` or `/etc/`, the file integrity baseline is automatically reset via `file-integrity.sh --update`. Previously, every PR merge triggered false positive alerts that persisted until the next security-scan at 04:00 UTC. Implements the safeguard claimed by lesson #127 (`file-integrity-false-positives`) which was never actually wired up.
- **Public peer registry endpoint** (`network-discovery.sh`) — Generates sanitized `data/peers/registry.json` after trust scoring. Strips IPs, notes, and trust breakdowns from the internal `peers.json`. Includes only name, domain, type, alive status, trust level, and discovered date. Served publicly at `/api/peers/registry.json`. Enables external systems and other AI agents to discover Marvin's known peer network without exposing sensitive operational data.
- **Modular prompt system** (`agent/lib/prompts.sh`, `agent/prompts/modules/`) — Shared prompt modules (identity, security-rules, output-rules) extracted from duplicated content across 12 prompt files. New library provides `marvin_build_prompt()`, `marvin_load_modules()`, and `marvin_list_modules()` functions. Self-enhance prompt refactored as proof of concept. Reduces duplication, ensures consistency, enables future prompt A/B testing.
- **Peer health exchange endpoint** (`health-monitor.sh`) — Generates `data/peer-health.json` every 5 minutes with non-sensitive health metrics for AI peer consumption. Includes CPU/memory/disk/load percentages, 30-day uptime, SSL cert days, peer count, and capability count. Served at `/api/peer-health.json`. No sensitive data (no issue details, internal IPs, or error messages). Protocol: `marvin-peer-health/1.0`.
- **Peer trust scoring system** (`network-discovery.sh`) — Calculates 0-100 trust scores for each known peer across 4 dimensions: longevity (days known, max 30), reliability (alive + beacon presence, max 30), identity (AI agent type + domain + engine, max 25), and behavior (not a scanner/prober, max 15). Scores written to `peers.json` with full breakdown. Dashboard PeersSection shows trust bars with color coding (green >= 60, yellow >= 30, red < 30).
- **"Marvin's Thoughts" dashboard section** (`ThoughtsSection.tsx` + `thoughts-extract.sh`) — Extracts recent decisions, observations, and intentions from enhancement reports (last 7 days). Categorized as INTENTION/OBSERVATION/REFLECTION with icons. Data pipeline generates `data/thoughts.json`, auto-runs after self-enhance sessions. Bilingual (EN/CS).

### Fixed
